# FILE: DATASET.md

# Dataset

## VZCrash (primary training data)
- Dataset: https://huggingface.co/datasets/vzc-research-chapter/VZCrash
- Paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06074 (PDF https://arxiv.org/pdf/2606.06074)
- Licence: CC BY-NC 4.0, so non-commercial use only, and the paper must be cited.

### Beliefs to verify (from project notes, NOT yet confirmed)
| Item | Belief |
|---|---|
| Size | about 190,000 events, 31,090 verified crashes |
| Signal | IMU at 100 Hz, units of g |
| Axes | X forward, Y left, Z up (the same as the CrashGuard mounting) |
| Labels | binary crash / non-crash; no severity, no impact direction |
| Paper baseline | server-side models, best AP reported as 97.56%; no microcontroller deployment |

Claude Code must confirm or correct each row in `docs/05_data_code/DATASET_FACTS.md`. **Done on 2026-11-10; several beliefs were wrong:** labels are three-class, not binary; each event is a 16 s window (1600 samples at 100 Hz, 415 events differ); gyro is in deg/s with two resolutions; there is no vehicle or trip ID; access is gated on Hugging Face. See `docs/05_data_code/DATASET_FACTS.md`.

### How the dataset is used (decided 2026-11-10, see DECISIONS.md)
- near_miss is a negative class; FPR on near_miss is reported separately.
- A firmware-style window (earliest sample above `TRIGGER_G`, 1.0 s before to 1.8 s after) is cut from the 16 s event. Events that never trigger count as predicted negative at system level. `TRIGGER_G = 2.0 g` and `MPU_RANGE_G = 16 g` are placeholders in `src/crashguard/config.py` until confirmed from firmware v2.
- 15 events with a raw peak above 16 g are dropped; signals are clipped to the sensor range.
- The supplied train / validation / test splits are used. The paper states they are separated by vehicle; the release has no vehicle ID, so this cannot be checked. A near-duplicate check found cross-split duplicates (see DECISIONS.md); the primary test set leaves them out and the supplied split is reported as a secondary result.
- Every VZCrash event was already selected on the device by threshold heuristics (paper, Section II), so our trigger is a second trigger on pre-selected events.

## Author's scale-model data (`data/hardware/hw_events.csv`)
This file will be collected with firmware v2 during experiments E1–E8. See HARDWARE_SCHEMA.md. It is the only source for impact-side and severity labels.

<!-- nhtsa:start -->

## NHTSA Vehicle Crash Test Database (severity validation)
Downloaded 2026-11-11 from the public NHTSA NRD API (https://nrd.api.nhtsa.dot.gov/nhtsa/vehicle/swagger-ui/index.html), US government data (public domain). The ISO MME zip of each test (ISO/TS 13499) is cached in `data/raw/nhtsa/` (git-ignored); accelerations are converted from m/s^2 to g. Channels: vehicle-CG accelerometer if the test has one, otherwise the front sill accelerometer, vehicle axes, status ok. The per-channel TSV links were not used (the server returned the same file for every channel of some tests). Sensor-like processing (assumed): 260 Hz front-end low-pass, clip to +-16 g, 40 Hz low-pass, resample to 100 Hz. Tests are used only if the chosen channel shows a crash pulse (peak above 5 g); 668 candidate tests were skipped for that or another reason (listed in `results/tables/nhtsa_excluded.csv`).

### NHTSA dummy injury criteria used in X2 (generated by `python -m crashguard.injury`)

Source: the `OCCUPANT` records of the NHTSA test metadata (public domain). Field meanings: NHTSA Biomechanics Data Reference Guide, version 4.b, volume II (opened). A recorded zero is treated as 'not computed'. A criterion is analysed only where at least 20 tests have it. Test numbers per cell:

# FILE: DATASET_FACTS.md

# VZCrash facts

Status: **downloaded and profiled** (2026-11-10). All 16 parquet shards are in `data/raw/data/` (git-ignored). Counts and quality checks are CONFIRMED_FROM_DATA, computed by `make data` and listed in full in [DATASET_FACTS_QUALITY.md](DATASET_FACTS_QUALITY.md) and `results/results.json` (`vzcrash.*`, origin MEASURED_SOFTWARE). Other items are CONFIRMED_FROM_CARD (dataset card, read 2026-11-10).

| Item | docs/05_data_code/DATASET.md belief | Fact | Source | Matches? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Size | about 190,000 events, 31,090 crashes | 189,303 events; **31,079 crashes** (train 137,954 / validation 27,175 / test 24,174) | CONFIRMED_FROM_DATA | Yes (31,079, not 31,090) |
| Accelerometer | 100 Hz, g | 100 Hz, tri-axial, g. 188,887 events have exactly 1600 samples; 416 differ (1000 to 4100 samples) | CARD + DATA | Yes; 100 Hz is from the card, sample counts are measured |
| Axes | X forward, Y left, Z up | Same, 1 g at rest; card warns alignment is sometimes suboptimal. Median \|a\| of the events is about 1.02 g | CARD + DATA | Yes, with a caveat |
| Gyro | rad/s "or as dataset provides" | deg/s; 1 deg/s resolution (103,548 events) or 0.001 deg/s (85,755 events) | CARD + DATA | **Differs** |
| Labels | binary crash / non-crash | **three classes**: crash 31,079 (16.4%), near_miss 28,681 (15.1%), normal_driving 129,543 (68.4%). Paper uses near_miss as negative | CONFIRMED_FROM_DATA | **Differs** |
| Window | about 2.8 s around trigger | each event is about **16 s**. In crash events the peak \|a\| sits at sample 1003 (median; 5th to 95th percentile: 484 to 1314), so the crash is roughly 10 s into the window | CONFIRMED_FROM_DATA | **Differs** |
| GPS | not mentioned | 1 Hz speed, km/h, 14 to 17 samples per event | CARD + DATA | New |
| Vehicle / trip ID | needed | **none**; only `vehiclesize` (Light 27,615, Medium 67,937, Heavy 30,967, Unknown 62,784) | CONFIRMED_FROM_DATA | **Differs** |
| Splits | to be made by us | supplied train/validation/test; no event_id appears in two splits; the paper states vehicle-level separation but the release has no vehicle ID | CONFIRMED_FROM_DATA + PAPER | Cannot be verified; duplicates found across splits |
| Severity / direction labels | none | none | CONFIRMED_FROM_DATA | Yes |
| Licence | CC BY-NC 4.0 | CC BY-NC 4.0; cite Bianconcini et al., ITSC 2026 | CARD | Yes |
| Paper baseline (AP 97.56%) | stated | Table I of the paper lists it for its best deep model on full 16 s events | CONFIRMED_FROM_PAPER | Yes; not comparable with our 2.8 s post-trigger windows |

## Checked against the paper (arXiv 2606.06074, PDF read in full on 2026-11-10; CONFIRMED_FROM_PAPER)
- **Splits:** train 72%, validation 14%, test 14%, "strictly separated by vehicle" with stratified sampling at the vehicle level. This **contradicts our earlier note that grouping was unverified**: the paper claims vehicle-level separation. The release has no vehicle ID, so we still cannot check it, and the cross-split duplicates found by our audit (see DATASET_FACTS_QUALITY.md and results.json `leakage.*`) suggest the separation is not complete or that one recording is stored under several event IDs.
- **On-device triggers:** recordings "were triggered on-device based on multiple threshold heuristics" (high X-axis values for hard braking and acceleration, high XY-norm for shock events), then processed in the cloud. Every event, including normal_driving, was therefore already selected once by a trigger. Our trigger is a second one on top.
- **Counts:** the paper gives 31,090 verified crashes; the data we downloaded has 31,079 crash events (the difference is not explained).
- **Paper baselines** (full 16 s events, accelerometer only, server-side): Table I lists AP for a physical baseline and several deep models. Our windows are 2.8 s after a trigger, so the numbers measure different tasks and we do not compare them.
- **Not in the paper:** duplicate recordings, a near-miss-only false-positive rate, any microcontroller deployment. Direction and severity labels are listed as future work.

## Quality findings
- No missing values (NaN) and no constant accelerometer signals.
- No duplicated event_id.
- 416 events are not 1600 samples long; 6 events have gyro length different from accelerometer length.
- **Corrupt values:** 15 events have a peak |a| above 16 g (the largest is about 2.7 million g) and 15 have peak gyro above 2000 deg/s. Three events hit 32766 deg/s or more (looks like integer overflow). 120 events have a median |a| outside 0.5 to 2 g (bad calibration or axis alignment).
- Crash strength: median peak |a| in crash events is 4.2 g, and 1,209 crash events peak below 2 g. A rule detector on peak g alone will miss these.
- Class balance is similar in all three splits (crash 15.7% to 17.5%).

## Open decisions for M2
See DECISIONS.md: near_miss handling, trigger and window position, glitch filtering, and the split grouping.

# FILE: DECISIONS.md

# Decision log

| Date | Decision | Reason | Effect on results |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-11-10 | Results carry origin labels; no invented numbers | Academic integrity | — |
| 2026-11-10 | Group-held-out splits | Avoid leakage between windows of the same event or vehicle | Lower but honest scores |
| 2026-11-10 | matplotlib pin changed 3.9.1 → 3.9.2 | 3.9.1 has no Windows wheel for Python 3.12; the source build is blocked by an Application Control policy | None on results |
| 2026-11-10 | `make` is not installed on this Windows machine; Makefile commands are run directly from `.venv\Scripts\python` | Environment | None on results |
| 2026-11-10 | M1 completed after the author supplied an HF token (used only as an environment variable, never saved). Dataset differs from docs/05_data_code/DATASET.md: 3 labels, 16 s window, no vehicle ID, gyro in deg/s, mixed gyro resolution | Rule: report differences, then continue | Open for M2: near_miss handling, window position, glitch filtering, split grouping. Not yet decided |
| 2026-11-10 | near_miss is a negative class. Always report FPR on near_miss alone and a 3-class outcome table (not triggered / predicted negative / predicted crash) | Author decision; the paper also treats near_miss as negative | Harder negatives than normal driving; FPR on near_miss is reported next to overall FPR |
| 2026-11-10 | Firmware-style window: trigger = earliest sample with abs(a) above TRIGGER_G, window = 1.0 s before to 1.8 s after (280 samples). TRIGGER_G = 2.0 g and MPU_RANGE_G = 16 g are PLACEHOLDERS in src/crashguard/config.py until the author confirms them from firmware v2 | Author decision | Trigger rates and all system-level numbers depend on TRIGGER_G; re-run the features and train steps after changing it |
| 2026-11-10 | Events that never cross the trigger are 'not triggered' and count as predicted negative in system-level metrics. Two levels are reported: model-level (triggered events only) and system-level (all events) | Mimics the car | Model-level numbers alone overstate FPR (most triggered test events are crashes; the share is `prevalence_triggered` in results.json) and system-level recall includes crashes the trigger misses |
| 2026-11-10 | TRIGGER_G sweep over 1.5, 2.0, 2.5, 3.0 g with trigger rate per class; windows that run past either end of the event are edge-repeat padded and the counts logged per class. A peak-centred window (1.4 s each side of the peak) is a secondary comparison only | Author decision | The primary trigger (2.0 g) was fixed before looking at results and was not chosen on the test set |
| 2026-11-10 | Dropped 15 events whose raw peak abs(a) exceeds 16 g (table results/tables/dropped_events.csv). Accelerometer clipped to +-16 g per axis before features. Gyro clipped to +-2000 deg/s, an ASSUMPTION (MPU6050 maximum range; not stated by the dataset). The 415 kept events that are not 1600 samples long use the same window rule; 0 events could not be windowed; 6 events with a gyro length different from the accelerometer length have the gyro cut or edge-padded | Author decision (clip, drop); gyro range assumed | Mimics sensor saturation; the 15 dropped events are 14 normal_driving and 1 near_miss, none are crashes |
| 2026-11-10 | Splits: the supplied train / validation / test splits are the primary result and 'grouping unverified' is stated. Near-duplicate check found leakage: 217 groups of identical accelerometer arrays appear in more than one split (mostly crash events), and 150 test events (114 crash) have an identical array or a nearest neighbour within 0.005 g in train or validation. They are KEPT in the primary test set and a 'dd_' (de-duplicated test) sensitivity result is reported next to it | Author decision to keep the splits; conservative reporting added | Differences between primary and de-duplicated test are small (see the system-level table); the leak is a risk to state, not removed |
| 2026-11-10 | Leave-one-vehicle-size-category-out test added: train and validation from the other categories, test = the whole held-out category (Light, Medium, Heavy, Unknown). 'Unknown' is a mixed group and is reported separately | Author decision | Estimates transfer across vehicle sizes, not across vehicles |
| 2026-11-10 | Gyro converted from deg/s to rad/s for gyro_peak. Ablations: all 14 features, no gyro (13), scale-free (5) | Author decision; FEATURES.md asks for rad/s | Gyro resolution differs by device (gyro_is_hd); not corrected |
| 2026-11-10 | Thresholds are chosen on the validation split only: (a) maximum F1, (b) highest threshold with recall >= 0.95. Test set is scored once per (model, seed). Seeds: 42 (single headline run, used for PR curves, error analysis and the C export) and 0-4 (mean and sample std). LR and the rule detector are deterministic, so their std is 0 | CLAUDE.md rules 5 and 6 | FPR at recall 0.95 is the test FPR at the validation threshold; the achieved test recall is reported too |
| 2026-11-10 | Class imbalance: no resampling and no class weights; AP and PR curves are used. Hyper-parameters are fixed, not tuned: RF15 (15 trees, depth 6), RF200 (200 trees, min_samples_leaf 3), LightGBM (300 trees, lr 0.05, 31 leaves), LR on signed-log features, rule = peak_a >= P and dur_above >= D with P, D tuned on validation | Conservative option | Reference models may be below their best; no test-set tuning |
| 2026-11-10 | Feature details fixed in docs/05_data_code/FEATURES.md: epsilon 1e-3 in peak_ratio_xy and crest, excess kurtosis, jerk in g/s from 10 ms differences, dur_above uses a fixed 2 g | FEATURES.md allows refinement if logged | Must match any firmware re-implementation |
| 2026-11-10 | Packet: little-endian; ACK layout = version, msg_type, node_id, acked event_id, acked counter, base counter + 8-byte tag = 26 bytes; every transmission, including a retry, needs a fresh counter, because the base rejects a repeated counter as a replay and would never re-ACK after a lost ACK | Software proposal; align with firmware v2 | If firmware retries with the same counter, a lost ACK is never repaired: author to check |
| 2026-11-10 | No gcc on this machine; the C equivalence test is compiled with zig cc (clang, installed as the pip package ziglang==0.17.1). fontTools was reinstalled from source (pip install --no-binary fonttools fonttools) because a compiled extension is blocked by Windows Application Control and PDF export failed | Environment | Same C source; the Arduino toolchain uses gcc, so also compile the header there |
| 2026-11-10 | Clean rebuild (all generated outputs deleted, whole pipeline re-run) reproduced 3,379 of 3,380 earlier numbers exactly; the one difference is lovso.Heavy.rf200.ap.mean, 0.89356 against 0.89355 | Check of rule 5 | Last-digit floating-point difference in a multi-threaded forest; not treated as a change in the science |
| 2026-11-10 | Finding from our own testing: with strict anti-replay (counter must be above the last seen), a car whose ACK was lost retries the same packet and the base rejects it as a replay, so the car never receives its ACK. The attack suite reproduces this ('Strict verifier (before the fix)' case). Fix adopted: idempotent re-ACK. The verifier keeps the last 16 accepted (node_id, event_id, counter) entries with their ACK; an exact repeat with a valid tag gets the identical ACK again without being processed again; any other old counter is still rejected. docs/01_protocol/PACKET_FORMAT.md updated | Author decision; found by our own attack suite | Replay of the most recent packets now returns the same ACK instead of a rejection (no state change). New risk: a replay flood makes the base spend airtime on ACKs, not mitigated. Delivery simulation re-run with strict and re-ACK verifier modes |
| 2026-11-10 | Delivery simulation extended: ACK loss was already modelled (each uplink and each ACK lost independently); a verifier mode was added (strict_same_counter = before the fix, reack = after) and the sweep re-run for both | Author decision | Under strict mode alerts that reached the base stay unacknowledged after a lost ACK; the re-ACK mode repairs this |
| 2026-11-10 | The de-duplicated test set is now the PRIMARY result in all tables, figures and the report. The supplied split (which includes the cross-split duplicates) is kept as a secondary result with the prefix supplied_ in results.json. This replaces the earlier decision to keep the duplicates in the primary test set | Author decision | Primary numbers are slightly lower or equal to the supplied-split numbers; thresholds still come from validation. Validation may also contain duplicates of train; that is not corrected |
| 2026-11-10 | The C-export equivalence check still runs on every triggered event of the supplied test split, duplicates included | It tests code equivalence, and more rows is a stricter test | None on model results |
| 2026-11-10 | Report wording: our AP is not compared with the VZCrash paper's figure; the paper uses full 16 s events and server-side models, ours use 2.8 s post-trigger windows and an MCU-sized model, so the numbers measure different tasks. The lower delivery of low-severity alerts under the severity-aware policy is described as an intended trade-off | Author decision | Wording only |
| 2026-11-10 | Correction after reading the VZCrash paper in full: the splits ARE stated to be separated by vehicle (72/14/14, stratified at vehicle level). Our earlier wording 'grouping unverified, no vehicle ID' is replaced by 'the paper states vehicle-level separation; the release has no vehicle ID so it cannot be checked, and the duplicates we found suggest it is not complete'. The paper also says every event was already triggered on the device by threshold heuristics, so trigger rates in our tables describe pre-selected events | Prior-work check requested by the author | Wording in report, DATASET.md and DATASET_FACTS.md. No change to numbers |
| 2026-11-10 | Prior-work record added (docs/06_references/PRIOR_WORK.md, searches dated 2026-11-10) and the novelty analysis rebuilt from results.json (report/novelty_summary.md, docs/06_references/NOVELTY.md). N12 (retry versus replay) is a known LoRaWAN difficulty, so it is worded as an independent re-discovery with a bounded fix; N11 is reported as a breakdown because near_miss events were not harder than normal_driving events in our results | Rule: evidence only; downgrade when close prior work exists | Novelty ranking changes; no change to numbers |
| 2026-11-10 | Depth against size sweep (trees 10, 15 and 20; depth 6 to 10; 5 seeds; de-duplicated test). Selection rule fixed before the sweep: among Pareto configurations (higher AP, lower estimated flash) whose estimated flash is at most 5% of an ASSUMED 1.25 MiB default Arduino-ESP32 app partition, take the smallest one within 0.002 AP of the best in the grid; if none qualifies, take the highest AP under the flash limit. Estimated flash = nodes x 9 bytes + 2 bytes per tree (host estimate, ANALYTICAL). The rule fell back to the highest AP under the limit and recommended 10 trees at depth 9. The deployed model is NOT changed | Author request: report only | Recommendation only. Forests above 32766 nodes use unsigned 16-bit indices in the generated header (same size); the deployed header is unchanged. Check the real partition table before relying on the 5% limit |
| 2026-11-10 | Re-ACK flood mitigation added to the reference verifier (off by default so the unmitigated behaviour stays testable; firmware should turn it on): at most one ACK per (node_id, event_id) per retry interval (alert airtime + ACK window, about 0.43 s at SF7), and every ACK is paid from the base station's duty-cycle token bucket. Flood scenario constants (20 replays per second for 120 s, 30 s of budget left, a genuine alert at 100 s) are ours | Author request | Without the limit the base spends its budget and a genuine alert gets no ACK; with the limit it spends less airtime and serves the genuine alert. The limit reduces the cost of a flood but does not remove it |
| 2026-11-10 | Hardware plan mapped to the author's minimum set H1 to H6; E-IDs replaced in the report, novelty summary and `make merge`. Severity against impact strength (old E3) and 200 m runs dropped and listed as limitations. The old E7 transfer test is now part of H2 | Author decision | The merge no longer analyses severity. Older logs with E-IDs are mapped to H-IDs |

| 2026-11-11 | **Follow-up 3: cause of the 1e-5 differences between two clean runs.** Only the learned-trigger false-alarm entries of N15 differed, by one test event in one seed. Cause (by elimination, not proven by an instrumented run): `RandomForestClassifier.predict_proba` with `n_jobs > 1` sums the trees' contributions in a thread-dependent order, so probabilities differ in the last bits; the 1% target threshold sits just above a negative event's score, so one event can flip. Fix: every forest used for scoring is set to `n_jobs=1` after fitting (models.py, detector_extra.py, trigger_aware.py); training stays parallel (deterministic given the seed). The size and depth sweeps still score with parallel prediction; their 5-decimal AP values have not been seen to differ. docs/05_data_code/REPRODUCE.md states a tolerance of 1e-5 for any remaining last-digit difference | Author follow-up | Last-digit reproducibility; no conclusion changes |

| 2026-11-12 | **T13a selection record (written before any held-out signal was read).** Source: the public NHTSA NRD API (`by-search`, test number 3000 and above, no filter), fetched 2026-11-12 (the API was reachable from this machine): 7,965 tests, 7,758 with instrumentation information. The 176 tests of N13/N14 (116 frontal barrier, 0 degrees, 30 to 70 km/h; 60 left-side impactor, 270 degrees, 40 to 70 km/h; 174 distinct test numbers) are excluded. Test modes taken as OTHER (configuration, angle): VEHICLE INTO POLE (any angle); VEHICLE INTO BARRIER at an angle other than 0; VEHICLE INTO VEHICLE (any angle); IMPACTOR INTO VEHICLE at an angle other than 270 (oblique 345, rear 180, frontal 0, and the rest). Not taken: sleds, static air-bag, low-risk deployment, rollover, warning-system tests, impactor-into-impactor, vehicle-into-impactor and 'other', because their records are not a vehicle-centre-of-gravity crash pulse. The catalogue lists **1,340 candidate tests** in these modes. Rules per candidate are those of the 176 and are not changed: the vehicle-CG accelerometer (else front sill), status ok, vehicle axes; one record per usable vehicle role (moving vehicle, VEHSPD above 0; stationary vehicle, VEHSPD 0); a record is used only if its chosen channel shows a pulse above 5 g after the 260 Hz front-end filter and holds at least 5 ms before the trigger. Two added rules: the reference delta-V must be at least 10 km/h (the smallest of the 176 is 19.9 km/h; relative error is unstable at a few km/h), and only the lowest test number is kept per vehicle (make, model, model year), mode and role. Records whose make and model appear among the 176 are KEPT in the primary set and flagged; a sensitivity run drops them. Reference delta-V is the one of N13 (peak over the opening 300 ms of the integral of the original-rate unclipped baseline-removed horizontal resultant; the database gives no delta-V, so this is the reference, not a measured vehicle velocity change). Methods are NOT refitted: plain, spline (level from split G's tuning tests) and fill + ridge (triangle, level 0.7, release 0.1, ridge strength by leave-one-make-model-out on the 176) are fit on the 176 tests only. Decision rules, fixed now: (1) the 80% criterion is reached in a (range, rate) cell if the lower end of the 95% grouped bootstrap interval (groups = make and model) of the share within +-10% is at least 0.80, as in N13 (N13 found +-64 g at 200 Hz as the lowest cell); (2) fill + ridge beats the spline at +-16 g only if the whole grouped interval of the paired difference of median absolute error (fill + ridge minus spline) is below zero. The physical band v <= delta-V <= 2v applies to frontal rigid-barrier tests only and is NOT scored here (no such test is in this set) | Author instruction (T13a) | Held-out set size comes out of the filters; nothing is dropped after seeing errors |

V4 (`vz_leakage.py`) compares three test sets, using the duplicate sets of V1: supplied, without exact array copies of a train or validation event, and also without near-duplicate windows; metrics are system recall at the validation-F1 threshold, recall at the 1% and 5% system-level false-alarm targets (thresholds from validation), AP and system AP; 5-seed means, paired bootstrap over supplied test events. # FILE: EXPERIMENTS_EXTRA.md

# Extra software-only experiments X1 to X7 (N17 to N23): baselines, splits, metrics, primary variants and decision rules

Written 2026-11-11, BEFORE any of these experiments was run. Rules for all seven (from CLAUDE.md and the author's instruction):

- **Software only.** Nothing here is a hardware result. Every number goes to `results/results.json` with an origin label (MEASURED_SOFTWARE, SIMULATED, ANALYTICAL, NOT_RUN). The words "first", "novel" and "state-of-the-art" are not used. `TRIGGER_G`, `MPU_RANGE_G`, `PRE_S` and `POST_S` stay placeholders in `config.py`; results that depend on them say so.
- **A claim counts only if the improvement clears the 95% bootstrap interval on data the method was not tuned on.** A result that does not is reported as NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND in the same detail as a success. Every choice (window, threshold, defence parameter) is made on a tuning part or on the validation split; the held-out or test part is scored once.
- **Splits.** NHTSA experiments use the grouped NHTSA split (by make and model; the make, model and year split is also reported) and **resample whole groups** for every interval. VZCrash experiments use the de-duplicated splits (supplied train and validation, de-duplicated test set; 24,022 test events); thresholds come from validation only; seed 42 for headline numbers and seeds 0 to 4 for repeated runs.
- **Prior-work search.** Each experiment gets a dated search (queries, closest work, opened or snippet-only) in `docs/06_references/PRIOR_WORK.md`, and a verdict N17 to N23 in `report/novelty_summary.md`. A search cannot prove that nothing exists. Snippet-only items support no claim.
- **Status words:** SUPPORTED (clears the interval on data not tuned on), PARTIAL, NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND.
- **Wording for the novelty file:** the framing stays "what a low-cost crash node must measure" and the integrated system. New items are supporting evidence or negative results where that is what they show.

---

## X1 (N17). Impact direction on real crashes

**Question.** Does the firmware direction rule (angle of the horizontal impulse over the first 60 ms after the trigger) recover the impact side on real crash-test records seen through our sensor model?

**Data.** The 176 NHTSA tests already used, plus any further tests the API returns with the same selection rules for other impact configurations. What the database offers was checked on 2026-11-11 by scanning every impact angle 0 to 359 (test number 3000 and above): angles present are 0 (frontal barrier), 270 (impactor or vehicle into vehicle, side), 285 (vehicle into pole), 345 (oblique impactor) and 10 (sled); angle 180 (rear) has no instrumentation records. So **rear impacts cannot be tested** and the struck side of the 270 tests must be read from the metadata (dummy seat, test title). **A class with fewer than 10 usable tests in total is not scored; no accuracy is printed for it.**

**Axes and sign convention (documented, then verified on the frontal tests first).** CrashGuard: X forward, Y left, Z up. The ISO MME channels are read as longitudinal (X) and lateral (Y) vehicle axes; the sign of each is *checked against the data*, not assumed: in frontal tests the vehicle decelerates, so the mean X acceleration over the pulse must be negative if X points forward. If it is not, the axis is flipped and the flip is recorded. The side tests then check the Y sign using the struck side from the metadata. The vehicle accelerates away from the impact point, so **the impact side is opposite to the impulse direction**: impact direction = atan2(-Jy, -Jx), measured from +X (front) towards +Y (left).

**Rule (firmware).** After the 100 Hz sensor model (260 Hz front end, clipping at the range, 40 Hz low-pass), baseline (gravity) removed by the pre-trigger mean: impulse J = sum of horizontal acceleration over the first W ms after the trigger sample. Class by sector of the impact direction: front (within 45 degrees of +X), left (45 to 135), rear (beyond 135), right (-135 to -45). Reported angle = impact direction in degrees.
**Parameter tuned on the tuning groups only:** the window W from {30, 60, 100} ms (the firmware text says 60 ms; 60 is the default and is changed only if another value is better on the tuning groups by more than one test). No other parameter.

**Baselines.** (1) Always the majority class of the tuning groups. (2) The sign of the longitudinal impulse only (front or rear) for the front-rear question.

**Metrics.** Confusion matrix, per-class accuracy, overall accuracy; for tests with a recorded oblique angle, the absolute angle error (median, 90th percentile) where the reference angle can be read from the metadata without guessing (otherwise it is not reported). Ranges +-16, +-32 and +-64 g. Intervals: bootstrap over make-and-model groups. Failure cases are listed.

**Decision rule.** The rule is SUPPORTED for a class and range if the lower end of the 95% grouped interval of per-class accuracy on the held-out groups is above the best baseline's accuracy for that class. A class below the count limit is reported as NOT_SCORED.

**Prior work.** Start from Balfaqih 2022, Ebert 2016, Tadic and Vukajlovic 2016 (opened). Principal direction of force estimation from in-vehicle accelerometers is established, so N17 is worded as **validation of a supporting feature**, not a contribution, unless the evidence says otherwise.

---

## X2 (N18). Severity measures against dummy injury measures

**Question.** Which of delta-V, ASI, peak acceleration and specific energy track the dummy injury criteria recorded in the same crash tests, and does delta-V add anything beyond the impact speed?

**Data.** The occupant records in the NHTSA metadata already cached for each test (`OCCUPANT` entries: dummy type, seat position, HIC, chest, femur, neck and pelvis fields where present). Which tests have which criteria is recorded in `docs/05_data_code/DATASET.md`. **If the criteria are not usable (missing or all zero), the experiment stops, writes exactly what is missing and moves on.** The meaning and unit of each field (for example HIC15 against HIC36) is checked in the data dictionary the database provides before it is used; if it cannot be established the field is not used.

**Severity measures** from the same chain as the other NHTSA results: delta-V, ASI, peak pulse acceleration and specific energy (half the squared delta-V) computed from the vehicle-CG channel, at the unclipped reference chain and at +-16 g and +-64 g sensor models.

**Analysis.** Spearman correlation of each severity measure with each injury criterion, **separately for frontal and side tests**, per dummy position; the correlation of delta-V with the impact speed alone; and the **partial Spearman correlation** of delta-V (and of ASI) with each criterion controlling for impact speed (rank-based partial correlation). Grouped bootstrap (make and model) for every interval. A correlation counts as present only if its 95% interval excludes zero; "delta-V adds beyond speed" counts only if the partial correlation's interval excludes zero.
**Severity classes against limits.** For each of the four classes (Low, Moderate, Severe, Critical), the share of tests whose criterion exceeds a regulatory limit. Limits are cited from documents that were opened (FMVSS 208 or Euro NCAP text); any limit that could not be verified is marked UNVERIFIED and not used for a claim.
**Stated plainly:** dummy criteria are test measurements, not human injury, and the sample is small.

**Primary question for the decision.** Does the partial correlation of delta-V with the chest or head criterion, controlling for speed, have an interval above zero for frontal tests? Everything else is descriptive.

---

## X3 (N19). Early classification

**Question.** How much of the 1.8 s post-trigger window does the deployed forest need, and does a sequential early-exit rule cut the decision time without losing recall at a fixed false-alarm rate?

**Data and model.** De-duplicated VZCrash splits, the same windows as the deployed model with the post-trigger length truncated to {50, 100, 200, 400, 800, 1800} ms (pre-trigger length fixed at 1.0 s). Features recomputed for each length; the deployed forest (15 trees, depth 6) trained at each length, seeds 0 to 4 (seed 42 for headline), threshold chosen on validation for 1% and 5% false alarms of all non-crash events (system level, as in the detector study).

**Baseline.** The full 1,800 ms window (the deployed model).

**Early-exit rule.** Checkpoints at the six lengths. At checkpoint k the model of length k gives a probability p. If p >= hi_k the event is declared a crash at that time; if p <= lo_k it is declared not a crash at that time (no alarm); otherwise the decision waits for the next checkpoint; at 1,800 ms the validation threshold decides. **hi_k and lo_k are chosen on validation only** from a fixed grid of quantiles: for the false-alarm target, the pair (one global hi quantile and one global lo quantile, each applied to every checkpoint through the model's validation score distribution) with the highest validation system recall subject to validation system false-alarm rate at most the target; ties go to the lower mean validation decision time.

**Metrics.** System recall at the fixed false-alarm rate against decision time (fixed windows: the post-trigger length plus the trigger itself; early-exit rule: its own mean decision time); mean and 95th percentile of the decision time of crash events for the early-exit rule against the fixed 1,800 ms window. Paired bootstrap over test events for the difference in recall and in mean decision time. The MCU cost of incremental feature updates is **ANALYTICAL** (operation count per sample from the feature definitions). The effect on the end-to-end time before the cancel window is **SIMULATED** (stage times assumed from `docs/` and the replay study) and marked as unconfirmed by hardware.

**Decision rule.** SUPPORTED if the early-exit rule's mean decision time for crash events is lower than 1,800 ms by a paired interval below zero AND its recall difference interval does not fall below -0.01 (a one-point loss) at the same false-alarm target. Otherwise NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND.

---

## X4 (N20). Guaranteed false-alarm control

**Question.** Does split-conformal threshold selection give a false-alarm rate at most alpha when exchangeability holds, and where does it fail when it does not?

**Method.** Scores of the deployed forest. Calibration set = non-crash events of the validation split (system level: events the trigger discards have score -1 and count as non-alarms). Conformal threshold for alpha in {1%, 5%}: the k-th smallest calibration score with k = ceil((n + 1)(1 - alpha)); the alarm is score above the threshold, so that for a new exchangeable non-crash event P(false alarm) <= alpha. **Baseline:** the existing validation-chosen threshold (the smallest threshold whose validation false-alarm rate is at most alpha).

**Experiments.** (a) Test-set false-alarm rate and recall over seeds 0 to 4 and calibration-set sizes {200, 500, 1,000, 2,000, all}, with 200 random draws of the calibration set per size and seed; report the share of draws where the test false-alarm rate exceeds alpha (the guarantee is marginal over calibration draws: about alpha of draws at most are expected to exceed it only in a coverage sense, so the report states the expected and observed values). (b) Break exchangeability on purpose: calibrate on three VZCrash vehicle-size categories and test on the fourth, and the reverse (calibrate on one and test on the other three); report the false-alarm rate against alpha and by how much it fails.

**Decision rule.** The guarantee is SUPPORTED if in (a) the mean test false-alarm rate is at most alpha with the interval over draws and seeds not above alpha for calibration sets of 1,000 or more; (b) is a limitation study and has no decision rule. A recall difference against the baseline counts only if its paired interval excludes zero.

---

## X5 (N21). Sensor-spoofing robustness

**Threat model** (`docs/01_protocol/THREAT_MODEL_SPOOFING.md`). An attacker can add a bounded signal to the accelerometer reading. Budgets: amplitude epsilon in {0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2} g (maximum absolute value added on any axis), and bandwidth limited to a sinusoid or a narrow band (one to three frequencies chosen from 2 to 45 Hz; the sensor is sampled at 100 Hz, so nothing above 50 Hz exists). Goals: (a) make a non-crash window cross the trigger and be classified as a crash; (b) hide a real crash (make it not trigger or not classify). **Simulated only on the dataset windows. No attack code is written to act on a physical sensor** (no signal generator, no resonance search, no hardware interface); the perturbation exists only as an array added to a stored window.
Parts of this threat model are taken from published work on acoustic and resonant attacks on MEMS accelerometers and gyroscopes; which parts is stated in the report after the search.

**Attacks on the deployed forest.** (1) Random search: 500 random draws of a bounded signal per window. (2) Structured search: a grid over frequency (2 to 45 Hz), amplitude (the budget) and phase for sinusoids and narrow bands, 200 candidates. (3) Gradient-based attack on a surrogate (a logistic regression and a small neural network on the downsampled window), with the perturbation projected onto the budget and the narrow-band constraint, then transferred to the forest.
**Attacked windows.** A fixed random sample of 1,000 non-crash and 1,000 crash windows of the de-duplicated test set that pass the trigger (seed 42), scored once; nothing is tuned on them.

**Defences** (parameters chosen on validation windows only): (D1) median filter (3 and 5 samples) on the window before features; (D2) a plausibility check: delta-V against peak acceleration consistency and cross-axis share (a pure sinusoid has large peak and near-zero delta-V), limits taken from the training crashes; (D3) adversarial training: the forest is retrained with attacked training windows at the same budgets (labels unchanged); (D4) an ensemble with the physics-based severity check (alarm only if the forest and the severity check agree).

**Metrics.** Attack success rate against budget for the baseline and each defence, with bootstrap intervals over windows; the cost of each defence in recall and false-alarm rate on clean test events (seed 42 headline, intervals by bootstrap).

**Decision rule.** A defence is SUPPORTED at a budget if its attack success rate is below the baseline's by an interval that excludes zero AND its clean recall loss at the 5% false-alarm target is below 0.02 (two points). The attack results themselves are descriptive.

---

## X6 (N22). Many-car LoRa capacity

**ANALYTICAL part.** Pure-ALOHA collision probability for N cars with alert and heartbeat rates stated in the report, and with the capture effect (a packet survives a collision if its power exceeds the others' by 6 dB, a stated assumption), for SF7, SF9 and SF12 using the airtime formula already in `lora.py`; and the base station's own limit: ACKs per hour that fit in its 10% duty cycle (26-byte ACK).

**SIMULATED part.** Event-driven simulation, N from 1 to 500 cars on one channel (the 433 MHz testbed assumption), Poisson crashes per car, a burst scenario (several alerts within seconds), heartbeats at a stated rate, a half-duplex base that cannot receive while it sends an ACK, base ACKs paid from its token bucket, per-car duty-cycle bucket as in the single-node simulation. Policies compared: fixed 3-retry, severity table, severity-aware scheduler (the same rules and parameters as N16, not re-tuned).

**Metrics.** Critical-alert delivery (ACK received by the car) and trigger-to-ACK latency against N, with bootstrap intervals over simulated hours; the N at which the base's ACK budget becomes the bottleneck; which conclusions change with the heartbeat rate and the spreading factor.
**Decision rule.** Descriptive: no method is claimed. A policy comparison is reported as different only where the paired interval excludes zero.
**Prior work.** Start from Tomic 2020, Wei 2023, Zhong and Nie 2024 (opened), then search further.

---

## X7 (N23). Scale-model transfer by physical scaling

**Question.** If a scale model's crash windows are an amplitude- and time-scaled version of a real crash, how do the deployed model's all-feature and scale-free-feature versions behave?

**Method.** VZCrash de-duplicated test windows are transformed: amplitude a in {0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 4} (applied to the baseline-removed acceleration, gravity restored) and time-scale tau in {0.25, 0.5, 1, 2} (x'(t) = a x(t / tau), resampled at 100 Hz; for tau < 1 a zero-phase low-pass at 45 Hz is applied first so that the result is not aliased, and an unfiltered variant is also reported). The window is re-cut (trigger and features recomputed). The deployed models, trained on the unscaled data (all 14 features, and the 5 scale-free features), are applied unchanged (seed 42; seeds 0 to 4 for means).
**Metrics.** AP and system recall at the validation threshold over the (a, tau) grid; which features are invariant (checked analytically and numerically); the Nyquist problem when tau shortens a pulse below about 40 ms (share of crash pulses whose duration above 2 g is below 40 ms after scaling).
**Decision rule.** None: this is a plausibility check, stated plainly. **It cannot predict performance on the author's car; hardware transfer test H2 is the real test.**

---

## Order of work and files

X1 `src/crashguard/direction.py`; X2 `src/crashguard/injury.py`; X3 `src/crashguard/early.py`; X4 `src/crashguard/conformal.py`; X5 `src/crashguard/spoofing.py`; X6 `src/crashguard/capacity.py`; X7 `src/crashguard/physical_scaling.py`. Results under `x1.*` to `x7.*` keys (N17 to N23). Each module has its tests. Each experiment is committed with an entry in `docs/07_log/PROGRESS.md`.

---

# Round 3 plans (written 2026-11-11 before the runs; keys `s2.*` to `s12.*`)

## S2. Effect of the cross-split duplicates (N8) on the headline

**Claim type.** N8 is a data-quality finding about one dataset, not a performance finding. This item only measures how much the duplicates move the headline.

**Part 1: side by side.** Deployed forest (15 trees, depth 6) with seeds 0 to 4 (mean) and seed 42; also the validation-selected 10 x 9 candidate. Threshold = validation maximum-F1 threshold (the headline rule), chosen once. Metrics: system recall, system false-alarm rate, model-level AP (triggered events) and system AP, each on (a) the supplied test split and (b) the de-duplicated test split (the primary one). The paired difference (a minus b) comes from one bootstrap over the supplied events in which both sets are recomputed from the same drawn rows (the de-duplicated set is the drawn rows that are not flagged); 1,000 resamples, seed 42. The point estimate for the 5-seed mean is the mean over seeds of each metric; each resample averages over seeds.

**Part 2: near-duplicates.** Windows are the firmware-style windows (trigger-aligned, 1.0 s before and 1.8 s after, 280 samples, clipped to the sensor range) of the triggered events. For each test event: the maximum Pearson correlation to any train or validation window (per-axis mean removed, the three axes concatenated, so the 1 g offset on one axis cannot make quiet windows look alike) and the minimum over train and validation windows of the maximum absolute difference (raw g, all axes, all samples). **Thresholds, fixed now: correlation above 0.999 and maximum absolute difference below 0.01 g.** A test event is a near-duplicate if one train or validation window meets both for the same pair. The maximum-difference search is exact for the 0.01 g question: every pair with a maximum difference below 0.01 g has an L2 distance below 0.01 sqrt(840) = 0.29, so all pairs within that L2 distance are checked. The exact-hash result (`leakage.*`) is not changed. Events not triggered have no window and are not scanned (their count is reported).

## S3. N20 re-run with a clean calibration set

**Step 1.** Count exact (raw-array hash) and near-duplicate (same rule as S2) events between the validation split and the training split and between the validation split and the test split.
**Step 2.** Clean calibration set: validation non-crash events with every exact-hash or near-duplicate match to a training event or to a test event removed. Split-conformal thresholds for alpha 1% and 5% from the clean set; the baseline is the validation-chosen threshold from the same clean set. Empirical false-alarm rate on the de-duplicated test set with intervals over seeds 42 and 0 to 4, calibration sizes 200, 500, 1000, 2000 and all (200 random draws per seed and size), and the share of runs above alpha.
**Step 3 (exploratory, defined now).** Size-stratified variant: the calibration negatives are re-weighted so that their vehicle-size mix matches the test negatives' mix (weighted conformal with weights w_c = share_test_c / share_cal_c; the threshold is the weighted (1 - alpha) quantile with the test point carrying the largest weight). Leave-one-category-out shift test repeated with the clean calibration set (calibration negatives from the other categories, and a variant that re-weights to the held-out category's size mix is not possible, so the weights use the pooled test mix; the Unknown category is reported separately).
**Decision rule, fixed now.** The guarantee "holds" if the upper end of the 95% interval for the mean test false-alarm rate is at or below alpha plus the sampling error of the test set, where the sampling error is the binomial standard error sqrt(alpha (1 - alpha) / n_test_negatives) (one standard error; the 95% bound is also printed). Either outcome is reported in full.

## S4. N14 and N13: representativeness and the remaining gap (defined 2026-11-11 before the run)

**Facts known before fixing the bins (descriptive counts only, no error was looked at):** the 176 tests have closing speeds from 32 to 66 km/h (143 of 176 at 55 km/h or more) and reference delta-V from 15 km/h upwards (93 at 60 km/h or more). The example bins in the task (below 30, 30 to 45, 45 to 60 km/h of impact speed) would have 0, 13 and 52 tests, so the bins are set on the reference delta-V instead.
**Bin edges, fixed now.** Primary: reference delta-V (km/h) < 30, 30 to 45, 45 to 60, >= 60. Secondary: closing speed (km/h) < 45, 45 to 60, >= 60. Binning on the reference delta-V conditions on the outcome (low bins tend to be over-estimated, high bins under-estimated), so the closing-speed bins are the less biased view; both are shown.
**Scores.** Split R (repeated grouped 5-fold cross-validation over make and model, 10 repeats, all 176 tests, each test scored only by models that did not see its make and model). Per bin: number of tests; the share of tests where the saturation test fires (final pulse family and level) at +-16, +-32 and +-64 g; the median absolute delta-V error for plain, spline, fill, fill + ridge and the M+ capped variant; a grouped (make and model) bootstrap 95% interval only for bins with 10 or more tests.
**Gap to the N13 target.** Shares within +-10% and +-20% at +-16 g, 100 Hz, for plain, spline, fill, fill + ridge (splits R and G), next to the 80% target.
**M+ capped (exploratory, one variant, defined now).** Same as fill + ridge, but the predicted log-correction is clipped to the 5th to 95th percentile of the realised log-correction log(reference / fill estimate) of the saturated tests of the tuning groups; fitted on the tuning groups only. Reported on all four splits (O, G, A, R) beside fill + ridge, with the paired difference and its interval, for the median, the 90th percentile and the shares within 10% and 20%. It is not the primary variant.
**Failure cases.** The ten worst tests after fill + ridge at +-16 g, 100 Hz (split R, mean error over repeats): make, model, test type, peak g, clipped share.
**Wording.** fill + ridge was named primary after earlier results were seen; NHTSA tests are mostly high-speed barrier tests and are not representative of all real crashes.

## S5. N16 and X6: burst scenario with sensitivity (defined 2026-11-11 before the run)

**Status of the burst scenario.** It was added to X6 after the N16 steady-state comparison against the severity table was a null result (critical delivery +0.006 [-0.000, +0.012]). It is therefore exploratory (`docs/07_log/DECISIONS.md`).
**Simulator.** `crashguard.capacity.simulate` (the X6 many-car simulator); the new options only add parameters, and with the defaults it reproduces the earlier runs exactly (checked).
**Defaults.** 100 cars, SF7, heartbeat every 600 s with a 16-byte payload, burst of 30 critical alerts within 10 s at t = 600 s on top of the Poisson background (0.1 alerts per car per hour, mix 40/30/20/10), 10% uplink and 10% downlink loss, capture rule "6 dB" (a packet survives an overlap if it is 6 dB above every overlapping packet), one simulated hour.
**Tuning bursts (8 combinations, seeds 1000, hours 0 to 2) and held-out bursts (the sweep below, seed 2000, hours 0 to 39) are disjoint in seeds and in parameter values.** Tuning combinations (cars, burst size, SF, loss): (75, 20, 7, 0.20), (75, 60, 9, 0.45), (150, 20, 9, 0.45), (150, 60, 7, 0.20), (75, 40, 12, 0.20), (150, 40, 12, 0.45), (120, 20, 7, 0.45), (120, 60, 9, 0.20).
**Tuned object.** The 24-combination grid of the N16 scheduler (reserve, spacing multiplier, jitter, spreading-factor raise). Objective fixed now: mean critical delivery over the tuning bursts; ties go to fewer features, then to lower node airtime. The chosen setting is called "scheduler (burst-tuned)"; the setting of the N16 steady-state study ("scheduler (N16)") is also scored. Both are scored once on the held-out bursts.
**Sweep (one factor at a time, the others at their defaults; 14 settings):** capture rule {6 dB, none (pure ALOHA)}; burst size {10, 30, 100}; cars {50, 100, 200}; heartbeat period {60, 600 s}; heartbeat payload {16, 48 bytes}; SF {7, 9, 12}; channel loss {10%, 30%, 60%} (both directions).
**Metrics.** Critical delivery (share of critical alerts ACKed), critical 95th-percentile latency (delivered alerts only; read with the delivery share), critical share ACKed within 30 s of the alert's creation. Differences scheduler minus severity table and scheduler minus fixed 3-try policy, paired by scenario seed, 95% bootstrap interval over the 40 seeds.
**Reading rule, fixed now.** A conclusion "holds across the sweep" if the interval has the same sign and excludes zero in at least 10 of the 14 settings; it "depends on an assumption" if it flips or loses significance when one factor changes, and the factor is named.

## S6. N19: separate the ensemble effect from the early exit (defined 2026-11-11 before the run)

**Why.** The earlier N19 result (early exit about 1.1 s earlier, recall +0.030 [0.021, 0.039] at the 1% target and +0.0037 [0.0005, 0.0068] at 5%, against the single 1,800 ms model) mixes two things: the sequential rule uses six length-specific forests (an ensemble), and it decides earlier.
**Arms, all at the same false-alarm target (1% and 5%, threshold or rule chosen on validation only), seeds 42 and 0 to 4, test set = the de-duplicated test split.**
- **F, single model at 1,800 ms:** the 1,800 ms forest and its validation threshold (the existing fixed-1800 arm).
- **A, the same single model scored at the early-exit time (isolates the early exit):** the 1,800 ms forest applied to the window truncated at each event's early-exit time (the time the sequential rule of E gives for that event, taken from the length grid 50, 100, 200, 400, 800, 1,800 ms); the threshold is chosen on validation with the same truncation rule (validation events truncated at their own early-exit times) for the same false-alarm target. This shows what cutting the window costs without length-specific models.
- **E, ensemble with early exit:** the existing rule (six length-specific forests, sequential exit).
- **E', ensemble without early exit (isolates the ensemble):** the same six forests and the same rule, but every decision is released at 1,800 ms. By construction its alarms equal those of E, so E' minus E in recall is exactly zero and the time difference is the whole early-exit saving; E' minus F is the ensemble (plus rule) effect at no time saving.
**Reported per target:** recall at the fixed false-alarm rate and the mean decision time of the triggered crash events for F, A, E and E'; paired differences A minus F, E' minus F (ensemble), E minus E' (early exit with the ensemble) with 95% bootstrap intervals (paired bootstrap of test events, 1,000 resamples; seed 42 and the mean of seeds 0 to 4).
**Wording rule.** N19 is re-worded around the part that clears its interval at both targets; a part that does not is stated as not shown.

## S9. M1: conformal intervals for clipped delta-V (N24) (defined 2026-11-11 before the run)

**Claim to test.** At +-16 g the point estimate of delta-V is often wrong, so the alert should carry a calibrated interval or an "at least this severe" statement.
**Method (grouped CV+).** Point estimate: fill + ridge (the N14 primary, named after earlier results were seen). Group = make and model. Outer split: the repeated grouped 5-fold cross-validation of split R (5 repeats here, seeds 42 + repeat), so every test is scored by models and calibration residuals that never saw its make and model. Inside each outer training set, an inner grouped 5-fold CV+ (Barber, Candes, Ramdas and Tibshirani, jackknife+ and CV+): for each inner fold k a model f_-k is fitted on the outer training groups without fold k (the pulse family, level, release fraction and ridge strength are re-chosen inside each fit); the out-of-fold relative residual of a calibration test i is r_i = |y_i - f_-k(i)(x_i)| / f_-k(i)(x_i) (the residual scale is the relative error); for a new test x the interval is [q_lo, q_hi] with q_lo the floor(alpha (n + 1))-th smallest of f_-k(i)(x) (1 - r_i) and q_hi the ceil((1 - alpha)(n + 1))-th smallest of f_-k(i)(x) (1 + r_i), n = number of calibration tests. Nominal coverage 80% and 90% (alpha 0.2 and 0.1). Why CV+ and not one split: with 176 tests (about 100 make and model groups) a separate calibration split would leave too few tuning tests for the ridge step; CV+ uses every test for both roles and its coverage guarantee is 1 - 2 alpha in the worst case (stated, not measured here). Ranges +-16, +-32 and +-64 g at 100 Hz.
**Evaluation (held-out groups only).** Empirical coverage of the reference delta-V with a grouped (make and model) bootstrap interval; median interval width (relative, and in km/h); the share of tests whose interval lies in one severity class (the class is the maximum of the delta-V class and the ASI class; the ASI of the filled window is the point value, not an interval).
**Severity decision rule.** k = class of the point estimate, k_lo = class of the lower bound. Report k if k_lo = k; otherwise report the class below k (at least 0) and set a flag "at least this severe, probably more". The final class is the maximum of the delta-V class and the ASI class. Compared with the point-estimate class: agreement with the reference class, under-estimation share, over-estimation share, share flagged.
**Decision rule, fixed now.** An improvement only if (i) the nominal coverage lies inside the 95% grouped bootstrap interval of the empirical coverage, and (ii) the under-estimation share of the decision-rule class is below that of the point-estimate class by an amount whose paired grouped bootstrap interval excludes zero. Either outcome is reported in full.

## S10. M2: prevalence-adaptive alert threshold and cancel-window cost model (N25) (defined 2026-11-11 before the run)

**Claim to test.** A threshold chosen on a data set with a crash share of about one in six gives very low precision at the prevalence of real driving, so the threshold should be set for the deployment prevalence and a cost model.
**Populations.** For prevalences 0.02%, 0.1%, 1% and 16% (of events that the device passes to the detector; VZCrash events were pre-selected on the device, so these are conditional numbers and the prevalence among all driving is lower still) a population of 1,000,000 events is drawn by resampling the de-duplicated test events: first the test pool is bootstrapped (events drawn with replacement, which carries the finite-test-set error), then crashes (round(prevalence x 1,000,000)) and non-crashes (the rest) are drawn with replacement from the bootstrapped pool. 200 repeats. Events the trigger discards are in the pool with score -1 and are never alarms (system level). Models: the deployed 15-tree forest and the 10 x 9 candidate, seeds 0 to 4 (results are the mean over seeds within a repeat, intervals are 95% percentile intervals over repeats).
**Thresholds, all chosen on validation only, one per (model, seed) and rule.**
(a) the existing validation rule: the maximum-F1 threshold (also shown: the 1% and 5% false-alarm targets).
(b) prior-shift adjusted: the forest probabilities are calibrated on the triggered validation events by isotonic regression (prior pi_tr = crash share of those events), then adjusted for the deployment prior among triggered events, p' = a q / (a q + b (1 - q)), a = pi_dep / pi_tr, b = (1 - pi_dep) / (1 - pi_tr); an event alarms if p' >= 0.5 (equal costs). Two versions: pi_dep known (the expected crash share among triggered events of the population) and pi_dep estimated by the EM procedure of Saerens, Latinne and Decaestecker (2002) from the unlabeled triggered events of the drawn population.
(c) minimum expected cost: an event alarms if p' / (1 - p') >= C_false (1 - p_cancel) / C_miss, with a false alarm costing C_false only when the driver does not cancel in time (probability 1 - p_cancel). Base case: C_miss / C_false = 1,000 and p_cancel = 0.9 (our assumptions; there is no measured cost). Both pi_dep versions as in (b).
(d) target precision: the highest-recall validation threshold whose validation precision at the deployment prevalence (computed from validation recall and false-alarm rate) is at least 10% or 50%; "unreachable" if none exists.
**Reported per prevalence and rule:** system recall, precision (PPV), false alarms per 100,000 events, expected cost per 100,000 events in units of C_false (all with intervals over repeats), the PPV-recall curves (expected, from the test pool), and for (d) the recall given up against (a), or "cannot be reached".
**Sensitivity.** C_miss / C_false in {10, 100, 1,000, 10,000} and p_cancel in {0.5, 0.7, 0.9, 0.95} for rule (c) with known prevalence.
**Decision rule, fixed now.** Rule (b), (c) or (d) is an improvement over (a) at a prevalence only if the paired difference in PPV (rule minus (a), over repeats) has a 95% interval above zero AND recall is no more than 5 points lower. Reported in full either way.

## S13. Spaced-retry severity table as the baseline for SCT (defined 2026-11-11 before the run; chosen in `docs/06_references/NOVELTY_SCAN.md`)
**Why.** In the burst cells the N16 scheduler and the fixed 3-try policy both beat the severity table, and the burst tuning (S5) chose the scheduler with no reserve, no jitter, no spreading-factor raise and the longest spacing multiplier: the gain looks like retry spacing, not severity awareness. SCT (P2) therefore needs a baseline that has the severity table's tries and ordering but spaced retries.
**P1b.** The severity table (maximum tries 2, 4, 8 and 12, service by severity, cancel windows by class, point classes) with exponentially spaced retries: spacing multiplier 2, no jitter, no reserve, no spreading-factor raise (the S5 burst-tuned setting; it was tuned on S5 tuning bursts, not on the UA-SCT cells). It is the `scheduler` policy of `crashguard.scheduler` with those parameters.
**Held-out cells and seeds:** exactly those of `docs/02_preregistration/PREREGISTRATION.md` section 5 (3 ranges x 7 steady and 9 burst cells x 40 seeds), P1b paired by scenario seed with the stored P1 and P2 runs.
**Decision rule, fixed now.** SCT (P2) improves on the spaced-retry table if (i) in the burst cells the mean over cells of the paired difference in critical reach within 10 s (P2 minus P1b) has a 95% interval above zero at each range, and (ii) in the steady cells P2's reach within 10 s is not lower than P1b's by more than 2 points (lower end of the interval above -0.02). Otherwise the severity table with spaced retries is sufficient for that outcome. Reported either way, also for reach within 30 s and the critical 95th-percentile latency.

# FILE: FEATURES.md

# Window features (14)

Window: about 2.8 s at 100 Hz around the trigger. |a| = √(ax²+ay²+az²) in g. "SF" marks a candidate **scale-free** feature, meaning it does not depend on absolute g level.

| # | Name | Definition | SF |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | peak_a | max \|a\| | |
| 2 | peak_ax | max \|ax\| | |
| 3 | peak_ay | max \|ay\| | |
| 4 | peak_az | max \|az\| | |
| 5 | rms_a | RMS of \|a\| − 1 g | |
| 6 | jerk_peak | max \|d\|a\|/dt\| | |
| 7 | energy | Σ(\|a\|−1)² · dt | |
| 8 | dur_above | time with \|a\| > 2 g (ms) | |
| 9 | peak_ratio_xy | peak_ax / (peak_ay + ε) | SF |
| 10 | horiz_share | peak horizontal / peak_a | SF |
| 11 | kurtosis_a | kurtosis of \|a\| | SF |
| 12 | crest | peak_a / rms_a | SF |
| 13 | post_quiet | std of \|a\| in the last 0.8 s ÷ std of the whole window | SF |
| 14 | gyro_peak | max \|ω\| (rad/s, or as the dataset provides) | |

Claude Code may refine these definitions, but it must record every change here and in DECISIONS.md. The C export expects features in this exact order.

## Impact direction (firmware rule, not a trained model)
- Uses the first 60 ms after the trigger.
- Computes the angle as atan2 of the horizontal impulse.
- Classes are FRONT, REAR, LEFT, RIGHT and VERTICAL.
- Evaluated only on hardware data (E1).

## Implementation details (src/crashguard/features.py, fixed 2026-11-10)
- |a| is computed after clipping each axis to +-MPU_RANGE_G. Windows have 280 samples (1.0 s before, 1.8 s after the trigger sample) with edge-repeat padding.
- `rms_a` = sqrt(mean((|a| - 1)^2)); `jerk_peak` = max |difference of |a|| / 0.01 s, in g/s; `energy` = sum((|a| - 1)^2) * 0.01 s; `dur_above` uses a fixed 2 g, in ms.
- `peak_ratio_xy` = peak_ax / (peak_ay + 0.001); `horiz_share` = peak of sqrt(ax^2 + ay^2) / peak_a; `crest` = peak_a / (rms_a + 0.001).
- `kurtosis_a` is excess kurtosis (normal = 0), with a 1e-12 guard; `post_quiet` = std of the last 0.8 s of |a| / (std of the window + 1e-6).
- `gyro_peak` = max of the gyro vector norm, converted from deg/s to rad/s (gyro clipped to +-2000 deg/s first).
- Feature subsets: `scale_free` = features 9 to 13; `no_gyro` = features 1 to 13.

# FILE: FIGURES_TABLES.md

# Figures and tables: index

Generated by `make report` (`src/crashguard/report.py`) next to `report/software_results.md`. For each figure and table: the origin label, the `results/results.json` key prefixes behind it, and the claim it supports. Origin labels: MEASURED_SOFTWARE, SIMULATED, ANALYTICAL, HARDWARE, NOT_RUN.

| Item | Title | Origin | results.json keys | Claim it supports |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Table 1 | Dataset profile (events per split and class) | MEASURED_SOFTWARE | vzcrash.events.*, leakage.test_events_kept.* | The primary test set is the supplied test split without cross-split duplicates |
| Table 2 | Model level, triggered events of the de-duplicated test set | MEASURED_SOFTWARE | s2.trig_2.all.* | Model-level AP, F1 and Brier of the five models |
| Table 3 | System level, de-duplicated test set | MEASURED_SOFTWARE | s2.trig_2.all.*sys_* | System recall and false-alarm rate, including events the trigger discards |

| Table 112 | T12: flash and CANCEL properties of the bounded model (holds = no counterexample within the bounds; a check of the model, not of the implementation) | ANALYTICAL | s12x.* | A CANCEL that overtakes its alert is lost in the design; remembering it fixes the model |
| Table 113 | T14: the preregistered hypotheses with Holm correction (grouped-bootstrap p-values, 1,000 resamples, so the smallest p-value that can be shown is `0.002`) | MEASURED_SOFTWARE | t14.* | H-a, H-b and H-c survive the correction; all other intervals are uncorrected and exploratory where marked |
| Figure 24 | Events per class in each supplied split. | MEASURED_SOFTWARE | vzcrash.events.* | Class balance |
| Figure 25 | AP and F1 with all features, without the gyro feature, and with scale-free features only. | MEASURED_SOFTWARE | s3.* | Feature ablation |
| Figure 26 | AP when a vehicle-size category is held out of training. | MEASURED_SOFTWARE | lovso.* | Vehicle-size transfer |
| Figure 27 | Attack suite outcomes. | SIMULATED | attacks.* | Security |

The F1 rows use the nested cross-validation (main estimate). The sealed-fold rerun, scored once and labelled revised after the negative, gives for H-F1 at +-16 g: ratio 0 control +0.169 [+0.046, +0.291] (the original result, as it must), best ratio +0.133 [+0.020, +0.247]; criterion met in 0 of 3 ranges. In the cross-validation the best-ratio model minus the ratio-0 control is +0.017 in macro-F1 at +-16 g (a difference of two point estimates, no interval); in the sealed fold it is -0.036. Neither version meets the original criterion.

If no verdict changes, that is a reportable finding about the limits of synthetic augmentation in this setting; the table above shows whether it applies.

# FILE: FIRMWARE_SYNC.md

# Firmware sync: what the firmware author must set or check

Generated by `python -m crashguard.docs_gen` from `src/crashguard/config.py` and `results/results.json`. The software side is built against placeholders; every item below must be confirmed against firmware v2 before any hardware number is read next to a software number. Nothing here is a measurement on the car.

## 1. Values to set or confirm
| Item | Software value | What to check in firmware |
|---|---|---|
| Trigger level | `TRIGGER_G = 2.0` g (PLACEHOLDER) | The same level on |a| (magnitude of the three axes, gravity included). Every system-level result depends on it; the sweep is in the report |
| Accelerometer range | `MPU_RANGE_G = 16.0` g (PLACEHOLDER) | The configured full scale. The severity study needs at least +-64 g at 200 Hz for delta-V within 10% on 80% of crash tests; the +-16 g range clips full-speed crash pulses |
| Sampling rate | `FS_HZ = 100` Hz | The IMU output rate and the window length in samples. The ASI low-pass coefficients in `severity.h` are computed for this rate; regenerate the header if it changes |
| Window | `PRE_S = 1.0` s before and `POST_S = 1.8` s after the trigger (PLACEHOLDERS), `WIN_N = 280` samples | The ring buffer must hold `PRE_N = 100` samples before the trigger; edge-repeat padding is used in software when a recording is too short |
| Gyro | clipped to +-2000 deg/s (assumed MPU-6050 maximum), converted to rad/s for the `gyro_peak` feature | Confirm the gyro range and units; VZCrash mixes gyro resolutions |
| Axes | x forward, y left, z up | The sensor mounting; a different orientation needs a rotation before the features and the severity code |
| Packet | 97-byte alert, 26-byte ACK, little-endian, tag = HMAC-SHA256 truncated to 8 bytes over bytes 0 to 88 (`docs/01_protocol/PACKET_FORMAT.md`) | Byte-for-byte layout, counter persisted in flash, same bytes on retry, one shared key |
| Re-ACK | the base remembers the last 16 accepted alerts, sends the identical ACK for an exact repeat, at most one ACK per (node, event) per retry interval, every ACK paid from the duty-cycle budget | Implement on the base station; without it a lost ACK is never repaired |

## 2. Feature order (the C model indexes features by position)
| Index | Feature |
|---|---|
| 0 | `peak_a` |
| 1 | `peak_ax` |
| 2 | `peak_ay` |
| 3 | `peak_az` |
| 4 | `rms_a` |
| 5 | `jerk_peak` |
| 6 | `energy` |
| 7 | `dur_above` |
| 8 | `peak_ratio_xy` |
| 9 | `horiz_share` |
| 10 | `kurtosis_a` |
| 11 | `crest` |
| 12 | `post_quiet` |
| 13 | `gyro_peak` |

Details that must match (`docs/05_data_code/FEATURES.md`): epsilon 1e-3 in the ratios, excess kurtosis, jerk from 10 ms differences in g per second, `dur_above` counted at a fixed 2 g, energy over the whole window.

## 3. Deployed model and the validation-selected candidate
| Item | Deployed header (`crashguard_rf.h`) | Candidate header (`crashguard_rf_candidate.h`) |
|---|---|---|
| Trees and depth | 1749 nodes in 15 trees of depth 6 | 10 trees, 5744 nodes (depth 9) |
| Estimated flash (host estimate, ANALYTICAL) | 15741 bytes | 51696 bytes |
| Decision threshold | in the header (`CG_THRESHOLD`) | 0.4836 (validation, maximum F1) |
| Compiled C against scikit-learn (max difference) | 1.86e-07 | 1.39e-07 |
| Pure-Python float32 mirror against scikit-learn | 1.86e-07 | see `results/results.json` (`candidate.*`) |

The candidate does not replace the deployed header. Before it does: check the real app partition (the 5% flash limit assumes a default 1.25 MiB partition), compile the header with the Arduino toolchain (gcc), run `benchmark_rf.ino` for time per call, and compare the decisions on a few logged windows.

## 4. Severity header (`severity.h`)
- Plain C99, no heap, static scratch buffers (not re-entrant). Added static RAM for the saturation-aware code: 10360 bytes (a count of the arrays, ANALYTICAL); host object code added: 1998 bytes (host estimate; an ESP32 build differs).
- Release rule: horizontal acceleration below max(0.5 g, 0.1 x pulse peak) for 50 ms, capped at 250 ms; rollover flag from the gravity-frame tilt (reported, NOT in the class rule).
- Unverified constants carried in the header: the 13 Hz ASI pre-filter, the THIV flail distances. The class limits and names are our choice.
- The saturation-aware block is generated only if `data/processed/n14_model.json` exists; its constants (pulse family, saturation level, ridge coefficients) come from the tuning tests.

## 5. Delivery settings the replay assumed (change them if the firmware differs)
- Cancel windows 10, 8, 6, 5 s for Low to Critical; window fill 1.8 s; on-device compute time assumed 0.05 s (NOT measured).
- Retry: same bytes, same counter, listening window = processing + ACK airtime + margin, then retry at once (the severity table: 2, 4, 8, 12 tries for Low to Critical).
- Duty cycle 10% (not verified against WPC/DoT), token bucket capacity 360 s.

# FILE: HARDWARE_READINESS.md

# Hardware readiness: what H1 to H6 would add and how the merge works

Generated by `python -m crashguard.docs_gen`. The hardware results are the author's; this file shows only that the path from the log files to `results/results.json` works. The dry run below used the synthetic DUMMY files of `tests/dummy/` (watermarked 'DUMMY DATA'), wrote its outputs only to `tests/dummy/out/` (git-ignored), and left `results/results.json` unchanged. No dummy value is quoted here.

## 1. What each hardware experiment adds
| ID | Experiment | Software result it would replace or complete | Log files |
|---|---|---|---|
| H1 | Impact side: 10 hits on each of 4 sides and 10 vertical drops | Nothing exists in software (VZCrash has no direction labels): the only evidence for impact direction (N2) | `hw_events.csv` (`true_side`, `pred_side`, `angle_deg`) |
| H2 | Crash against not-crash (the H1 hits plus 30 non-crashes) and the transfer test from VZCrash | The VZCrash detector results gain a measurement on the scale model; tests the scale-free features (SF) | `hw_events.csv`, `hw_windows.csv` |
| H3 | LoRa delivery: 20 alerts each at 10, 50 and 100 m, optional wall | Replaces the end-to-end replay and the delivery simulation (N4, N5, N16); gives the loss against spreading factor relation the scheduler assumed | `hw_events.csv` (`airtime_ms`, `ack_rtt_ms`, `retries`, `delivered`, `rssi`, `snr`), transmitter and gateway totals |
| H4 | Security: forged, tampered, replay, spoofed ACK, base reboot, ACK-lost retry, 3 tries each | Replaces the simulated attack suite on the radios (N3, N12) | `hw_attacks.csv` |
| H5 | Edge cost: compile report and logged microseconds | Replaces the host size estimates and the assumed compute time (N1, N10) | `hw_memory.csv`, `hw_events.csv` (`classify_us`, `ml_us`, `verify_us`) |
| H6 | SW-420 ablation | Re-analysis of H1 and H2 with and without the vibration sensors (N7) | `hw_events.csv` (`sw_*`) |

Schemas are in `docs/05_data_code/HARDWARE_SCHEMA.md`. Severity against impact strength and runs at 200 m are not in the minimum plan. Older logs with E-IDs are mapped to H-IDs by the merge.

## 2. Dry run of the merge on the dummy files
`merge/ingest_hardware.py` ran on the dummy files and produced 74 entries, grouped as: `hw.H1` (3), `hw.H2` (14), `hw.H3` (28), `hw.H4` (2), `hw.H5` (20), `hw.H6` (6), `hw.lora_pdr` (1).

Output files written to `tests/dummy/out/`: `dummy_results.json`, `h1_confusion.csv`, `h1_direction_confusion.pdf`, `h1_direction_confusion.png`, `h3_delivery.csv`, `h3_delivery_vs_distance.pdf`, `h3_delivery_vs_distance.png`, `h3_simulation_vs_measured.pdf`, `h3_simulation_vs_measured.png`, `h4_attacks.csv`, `h5_latency_breakdown.pdf`, `h5_latency_breakdown.png`.

Every figure from the dummy files carries the watermark 'DUMMY DATA' and none of the dummy outputs goes into `results/`.

## 3. Current state of the real hardware data (`data/hardware/`)
- Wi-Fi protocol comparison (HTTP, MQTT, CoAP): ingested, HARDWARE. LoRa gateway snapshots: ingested, but a delivery ratio needs the transmitter total: NOT_RUN (the transmitter total is missing, so a delivery ratio cannot be computed).
- `hw_events.csv` does not exist yet, so H1 to H6 are NOT_RUN in `results/results.json`, each with its reason.

## 4. How to merge real logs
Put the files in `data/hardware/` (read-only for this repository), then run `python merge/ingest_hardware.py` (or `make merge`). A hardware number is written to `results/results.json` with the origin label HARDWARE; nothing is edited or extended.

# FILE: HARDWARE_SCHEMA.md

# Hardware data files (`data/hardware/`): author-supplied, read-only

## Already measured (included)
| File | Content |
|---|---|
| `protocols.csv` | HTTP/MQTT/CoAP results from the progress report (ranges as min/max) |
| `tcp.csv` | TCP connection measurements |
| `lora_packets.csv` | LoRa test and telemetry packet observations |
| `lora_gateway.csv` | Gateway counter snapshots |

## To be collected (firmware v2 event log)
`hw_events.csv`: one row per event:
- `event_id`, `experiment` (H1–H6, see EXPERIMENTS.md; older logs with E-IDs are mapped), `true_label` (crash / non_crash), `true_side` (F/R/L/Rt/V), `distance_m`, `walls` (`designed_severity` and `speed_setting` may stay in the log but are not analysed: severity against impact strength was dropped from the plan)
- `rule_sev`, `ml_prob`, `pred_side`, `angle_deg`, `peak_g`
- `sw_fl`, `sw_fr`, `sw_ml`, `sw_mr`, `sw_rl` (0/1 fired within −100/+300 ms), `sw_first`, `sw_pre_edges`
- `classify_us`, `ml_us`, `verify_us`, `airtime_ms`, `ack_rtt_ms`, `first_tx_to_ack_ms`, `trigger_to_ack_ms`, `retries`, `delivered` (0/1), `rssi`, `snr`

`hw_windows.csv`: raw windows: `event_id`, `t_ms`, `ax`, `ay`, `az`, `gx`, `gy`, `gz` (in g and rad/s).

`hw_attacks.csv`: `case`, `expected`, `observed`.

`hw_memory.csv`: `item` (flash/RAM), `bytes`, from the Arduino compile report.

Rules:
- Units are in the column names or in this file.
- Missing values are left empty, never filled with zero.
- Transmitter and gateway counts must both be logged so that a delivery ratio can be computed.
