# FILE: JOURNAL_RESULTS.md

# Journal push: results summary (generated from `results/results.json`)

Branch `journal-push`, software only (`data/hardware` is not read). Origin labels: MEASURED_SOFTWARE unless stated. Preregistration: `docs/02_preregistration/PREREGISTRATION_V3.md`; decisions and mistakes: `docs/07_log/DECISIONS.md`; claims with allowed wording: `docs/03_results/CLAIM_EVIDENCE.md`.

## One line per task

- **T13a (held-out NHTSA modes).** 739 records from 1,340 candidate tests in 326 make-and-model groups. The 80% criterion is reached in **0** cells (best lower end 0.794 at +-200 g and 200 Hz; 0.775 at +-64 g and 200 Hz). Fill + ridge does not beat the spline at +-16 g (1.31 [-1.41, 4.37] points); it does at +-32 g.
- **T13c (NHTSA near-duplicates).** 2 pairs among the 176 (the same test channel counted twice), 0 between the held-out records and the 176. The +-16 g result of N14 is unchanged without the flagged tests (exploratory).
- **Block 2 (sensor selection).** 30 of 99 configurations meet the rule on the 176 tests; the smallest range is +-64 g; no +-16 g part meets it; no configuration meets it on the held-out modes. Tables: `results/paper/tab_sel_*`, figure `sel_share_vs_range`.
- **V1 (independent audit).** Every stored count is reproduced (217 groups, 1,305 flagged windows); **V2** the 'a fifth' (22.3% of triggered test windows) and 'about 5%' figures use different denominators; **V3** `docs/04_audits/VZCRASH_AUDIT.md`; **V4** near-duplicate removal lowers recall at the 1% target by 0.0727 [0.0641, 0.0809].

## One line per hypothesis (Holm over the seven)

| Hypothesis | Verdict | Raw p | Holm p | Statement |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H-X1 | **NOT SUPPORTED** | 0.001 | 0.007 | X: the 16-bit fixed-point forest loses at most 0.005 AP and cuts the flash estimate by 30% (preregistered, Holm-corrected) |
| H-X2 | **NOT SUPPORTED** | 1.000 | 1.000 | X: a cheap-trigger cascade raises the share of crashes passing the trigger by 2 points over a 2 g threshold at an equal non-crash trigger rate |
| H-X3 | **NOT SUPPORTED** | 0.275 | 1.000 | X: group-aware training raises recall at the 1% false-alarm target on the strict test set by 1 point |
| H-X5 | **NOT SUPPORTED** | 1.000 | 1.000 | X: a +-16 g sensor with fill + ridge preserves the rank of delta-V (Spearman at least 0.90, lower end 0.85) |
| H-X6 | **SUPPORTED** | 0.001 | 0.007 | X: a lower-bound-anchored estimator lowers the 90th-percentile delta-V error by 5 points against fill + ridge at +-16 g |
| H-X7 | **NOT SUPPORTED** | 1.000 | 1.000 | X: a real two-part pair reaches 0.80 within +-10% where neither part alone does (preregistered fusion rules) |
| H-X8 | **NOT SUPPORTED** | 1.000 | 1.000 | X: a sequential rule decides 100 ms earlier than the early-exit rule at equal or better recall |

X4 (labels): the supplied files carry only crash, near_miss and normal_driving and the vehicle size; no direction or severity label (`x.x4.*`).

## Negative results (reported in full)

- N13 is not confirmed on other crash modes (T13a); fill + ridge does not beat the spline at +-16 g there; the ridge fitted on the 176 does not carry over (13.7% median for fill without the ridge against 18.0% with it).
- X5: the order of severity is not preserved at +-16 g (Spearman 0.751 [0.678, 0.805]; the plain estimate ranks better, 0.841 [0.791, 0.875]); N13 is strengthened.
- X1: the 16-bit forest keeps AP but the flash estimate falls only 22.2% in the existing layout.
- X2: the cascade is the 2 g threshold itself in most held-out categories; no gain.
- X3: group-aware training changes the 1% recall on the strict set by 0.0011 [-0.0022, 0.0045]; duplicates in training do not explain the gap.
- X7: no preregistered pair and fusion rule reaches a lower end of 0.80; a wide-mask rule defined afterwards does for 3 combinations (exploratory).
- X8: earlier at the 1% target, slower and lower recall at 5%; the recall gain over the single forest is an ensemble effect.

## The one supported hypothesis

H-X6: at +-16 g on split R the lower-bound-anchored estimator has a 90th-percentile error of 27.5% against 58.5% for fill + ridge (gain 31.0 [20.2, 37.8] points, raw p 0.001, Holm 0.007). Cautions: the gain has its interval above zero on split A only among G, A, O; the median is not better than the tail-aware model; the unanchored boosting model accounts for part of the gain (`x.x6.R.r16.paired.GBR_minus_ANC.p90`); the estimator over-corrects the 2 tests where the plain estimate already exceeds the reference.

## NOT_RUN items and why

- `export.benchmark_us_per_call`: needs the ESP32; sketch is built, not yet measured
- `x1.A.r16.angle_err.oblique_magnitude.median_deg`: 3 held-out oblique tests, fewer than 10
- `x1.A.r32.angle_err.oblique_magnitude.median_deg`: 3 held-out oblique tests, fewer than 10

- `x1.G.r64.angle_err.oblique_magnitude.median_deg`: 2 held-out oblique tests, fewer than 10
- `x2.limit.tti_pelvg.status`: TTI(d) and pelvis limits of the old SID dummy are not in the current FMVSS 214 text that was opened; no share against them is computed
- `ua.b1.fill_ridge.f200.r16`: the fill and ridge constants (pulse-fit offsets in samples, saturation level, ridge strength) were tuned at 100 Hz and are not re-tuned at 200 Hz; the plain estimate is reported at 200 Hz instead
- `ua.b1.fill_ridge.f200.r32`: the fill and ridge constants (pulse-fit offsets in samples, saturation level, ridge strength) were tuned at 100 Hz and are not re-tuned at 200 Hz; the plain estimate is reported at 200 Hz instead
- `ua.b1.fill_ridge.f200.r64`: the fill and ridge constants (pulse-fit offsets in samples, saturation level, ridge strength) were tuned at 100 Hz and are not re-tuned at 200 Hz; the plain estimate is reported at 200 Hz instead
- `s12.ATT.design.L1_no_ack_in_terminal_state`: not checked in this bound set
- `s12.ATT.strict_anti_replay.L1_no_ack_in_terminal_state`: not checked in this bound set

- `t6.external_scale`: no delta-V severity scale from an opened source: the candidate pages (Traffic Injury Prevention articles, National Academies report, BASt, SWOV) returned HTTP 403, 404 or an access check; snippets mention categories below 24, 24 to 48 and above 48 km/h but wer
- `t3.r64.s0p1.break_even_ratio_hi`: upper end infinite: some resamples have no mis-triage gain
- `t3.r64.s0p3.break_even_ratio_hi`: upper end infinite: some resamples have no mis-triage gain
- `t3.r64.sobs.break_even_ratio_hi`: upper end infinite: some resamples have no mis-triage gain
- `t3.r64.s0p9.break_even_ratio_hi`: upper end infinite: some resamples have no mis-triage gain

- `t13.heldout.grid.criterion.min_rate_hz_at_min_range`: value not finite
- `sel.caveat.esp32_read_rate`: effective I2C or SPI read rate of each part on an ESP32 was not measured: hardware item; the study uses the datasheet output data rate

## Limitations (paragraph for the paper)

All delta-V and sensor results use NHTSA crash tests, most of which are high-speed barrier tests, through a software sensor model whose bandwidth, range, rate, resolution and noise come from data sheets or assumptions; the output rates are data sheet rates, not rates read over I2C or SPI on an ESP32 (NOT_RUN, hardware). The reference delta-V is the integral of the unclipped original-rate channel, not a measured vehicle velocity change. The held-out test modes are dominated by pole tests, and the methods fitted on the original 176 tests do not carry over to them. Two of the 176 records are the same channel counted twice. VZCrash events were selected on the device before release, its recordings contain exact and window-level duplicates across the supplied splits (audited here), and it has no vehicle identifier, so vehicle-level separation cannot be checked; its licence is CC BY-NC 4.0 (confirmed by the dataset authors on 2026-11-12, Hugging Face revision 42e1482d005c0e545379d6cfa4939486c9fe4249). Hardware results are not included. The 1% duty limit and every simulator parameter of the delivery studies are assumptions.

## Index of generated tables and figures

Tables (CSV and LaTeX in `results/paper/`): `jp_tab_t13_heldout`, `jp_tab_t13_paired`, `jp_tab_t13_grid`, `jp_tab_t13_neardup`, `tab_sel_parts`, `tab_sel_sources`, `tab_sel_study`, `tab_sel_study_all`, `jp_tab_vz_leakage`, `jp_tab_x1`, `jp_tab_x3`, `jp_tab_x5_rank`, `jp_tab_x6_anchored`, `jp_tab_x7`, `jp_tab_x8`, `jp_tab_x_verdicts`, `vz_audit_cross_split_groups.csv`. Figures (PNG at 300 dpi and PDF, 3.5 inch wide): `sel_share_vs_range`, `jp_fig_x5_rank`, `jp_fig_x6_p90`, `jp_fig_t13_range`. Commands: `docs/05_data_code/REPRODUCE_FINISH.md`.

# FILE: LIMITATIONS.md

# Limitations (plain list)

Every item is something this software work cannot show, or an assumption a reader must know. Numbers behind each item are in `results/results.json` and the report; none are repeated here.

## Placeholders and settings
1. **Trigger level and sensor range are placeholders.** `TRIGGER_G` (2.0 g) and `MPU_RANGE_G` (16 g) in `src/crashguard/config.py` are not confirmed from firmware v2. The window lengths (`PRE_S`, `POST_S`) are placeholders too. Every system-level result is a function of them; the trigger sweep shows how much.
2. **The packet layout is a proposal.** Little-endian byte order, the counter persisted in flash, and a 97-byte alert are assumptions to be matched with the firmware (`docs/05_data_code/FIRMWARE_SYNC.md`).

## Data
3. **VZCrash events were pre-selected on the device.** The dataset authors say every event was triggered by threshold heuristics before release. The trigger in this work is a second stage on already-selected events; trigger rates and system-level numbers do not describe continuous driving, where false alarms would be far more frequent.
4. **No vehicle or trip ID.** The paper states vehicle-level splits; the release has no vehicle ID, so this cannot be checked. Identical recordings were found across splits and removed from the primary test set. The validation split may also repeat training events and was not corrected.
5. **Duplicates.** Exact and near-identical recordings (a 32-bin summary and an L-infinity distance) were found; other forms of repetition, such as the same vehicle at different times, cannot be detected without an ID.
6. **No severity, direction or rollover labels in VZCrash.** Severity measures on VZCrash are descriptive. The rollover flag is a false-alarm check, not an accuracy result.
7. **A small crash-test set.** The NHTSA tests are cars in frontal barrier and side impactor tests, a few dozen per speed class; the held-out parts have a few dozen tests, so intervals are wide. The reference delta-V is a defined integral (the largest running integral in 300 ms), not a measured vehicle velocity change.

## Severity
8. **Full-scale severity thresholds.** The class limits (24, 40 and 56 km/h) and the class names are our choice, informed by cited literature, not a published four-class scale. The class mix moves when each limit moves by 20% (Table in the report).
9. **The MPU-6050 range is +-16 g.** It clips full-speed crash pulses and underestimates delta-V. The severity study needs at least +-64 g sampled at 200 Hz for delta-V within 10% on 80% of tests; the saturation-aware estimator repairs a +-16 g sensor only partly.
10. **The sensor model is generic and ours.** A zero-phase low-pass at the front-end bandwidth, saturation, a digital low-pass at 0.4 of the sample rate and sampling. It is not the response of any one part; noise is not modelled (quantisation is, for the named parts). The datasheet values were read from PDFs opened on 2026-11-11; two of the four lack a noise figure in what was read.
11. **Unverified constants** (`docs/05_data_code/SEVERITY.md`): the 13 Hz four-pole ASI pre-filter; the THIV flail-space distances (0.6 m used; one read source gives 0.8 m); the class names and cut points. The ASI level C limit was read in a secondary source, not in EN 1317-2 itself. THIV is reported but does not enter the class.
12. **The rollover flag is not used for the class.** Both the integrated-angle flag and the gravity-corrected flag fire on more than 0.5% of normal driving.
13. **The release rule is tuned on a small set.** The fraction of the pulse peak was chosen on the NHTSA tuning tests; on VZCrash many pulses still run to the 250 ms cap because dashcam signals rarely stay quiet.

## Detector and proposed methods
14. **No improvement from N15.** The trigger-aware methods did not raise system recall beyond the interval; the learned trigger collapsed to a level rule.
15. **N14 helps a +-16 g sensor only partly** and has no effect where pulses do not saturate. It depends on the pulse family and saturation level chosen on a small tuning set.
16. **N16 is supporting evidence from a simulation.** It gains little over the severity table in most held-out cells; the spreading-factor effect rests on an assumed loss factor.

## Simulation
17. **Assumptions of the replay and the scheduler:** Poisson arrivals, independent or Gilbert-Elliott losses with stated parameters, assumed stage times (window fill, compute time, cancel windows), one node and one base, no collisions, the base receives all spreading factors, the real VZCrash severity mix is almost all Low, so a stress mix is used where classes must differ. These are not hardware measurements; hardware test H3 would replace them.
18. **Compute time on the ESP32 is not measured.** Sizes are host estimates (nodes x bytes, object code size on the host, static array counts). `benchmark_rf.ino` is built but not run.

## Security
19. **Replay flood.** A sustained flood can still use up the base station's duty-cycle budget; the per-event re-ACK limit reduces the cost but does not remove it.
20. **Key handling.** One shared key on both nodes, no key rotation, no per-node keys; a recorded alert is accepted again if the base loses its counter store; the 32-bit counter cannot wrap without a key change. Alerts are authenticated but not encrypted, so position is readable over the air.
21. **A blind forgery** needs about 2^64 attempts (ANALYTICAL); this says nothing about key theft or side channels.

## Hardware and regulation
22. **The 433 MHz testbed** is for the scale model only; the 10 mW e.r.p. and 10% duty cycle come from a secondary summary and are not verified against the WPC/DoT notification. A deployment would use 865 to 867 MHz.
23. **The scale model is not a car.** Its impact forces are not real-vehicle forces; scale-model severity would be impact strength, not injury. Severity against impact strength and runs at 200 m are not in the minimum hardware plan.

## Environment
24. **Compiled C checks can be blocked** by Windows Application Control. Then the result is NOT_RUN with the reason, and a pure-Python float32 mirror of the C code is the remaining check.
25. **The lock file was built from the working environment** (`requirements.lock`), for Python 3.12 on Windows 11 AMD64; other platforms may differ in the last digit.

## C checks and the N14 estimator (round 3, S7)
The C code of the N14 saturation-aware estimator (`cg_severity_sat`) equals the Python reference on the 224 real and mixed windows of the pipeline and on 47 of 48 synthetic clipped pulses. On the 48th (a half-sine pulse of 20 g and 40 ms with 2 saturated samples out of 5 pulse samples) the pulse-fit grid search has two cells with the same cost (equal to 3e-14 relative): the filled delta-V is 18.8 km/h in Python and 27.9 km/h in single-precision C, because the tie is broken differently. The fit is under-determined when a pulse has very few samples, and both answers fit the data equally well; the firmware was not changed in this round. The synthetic check therefore FAILS by its pass rule (it is kept, not removed) and is listed in `docs/04_audits/ALGORITHMS_COMPLIANCE.md` (W3).

<!-- jp_limits:start -->

## Journal push (generated; details in docs/03_results/JOURNAL_RESULTS.md)

26. **Held-out NHTSA modes** are dominated by pole tests, and the N13 and N14 findings were not confirmed on them.
27. **Two of the 176 NHTSA records are one channel counted twice.** The tagged results were not re-run.
28. **Sensor selection uses data sheet output rates and a software model.** The read rate on an ESP32 and any mounting or synchronisation effect are NOT_RUN or not modelled.
29. **VZCrash has duplicates across the supplied splits and no vehicle identifier.** Window-level duplicates affect about a fifth of triggered test windows and about 30% of crash test events; the exact-copy test events are under 1% of all test events.
30. **X6 is the only supported Block 4 hypothesis** and its gain is partly due to the anchoring and the boosting model; it holds on two of four splits and is not better in the median than the tail-aware model.
<!-- jp_limits:end -->

| N14 clipped-signal reconstruction (queued) | `("Abstract":"saturat*" OR "clipp*") AND ("Abstract":"accelerometer") AND ("Abstract":"reconstruct*" OR "restor*") AND ("Abstract":"impact" OR "crash")` | `TITLE-ABS-KEY((saturat* OR clipp*) AND accelerometer AND (reconstruct* OR restor*) AND (impact OR crash) AND velocity)` | `clipped accelerometer signal reconstruction impact crash pulse velocity change` | | | |
| N15 trigger plus classifier (queued) | `("Abstract":"cascade") AND ("Abstract":"detector") AND ("Abstract":"trigger") AND ("Abstract":"crash" OR "impact")` | `TITLE-ABS-KEY(cascade AND detector AND trigger AND (crash OR impact) AND "joint" AND threshold)` | `joint optimisation trigger threshold and classifier cascade detector cost-sensitive early rejection impact detection` | | | |
| SF scale-free features | `("Abstract":"scale-invariant" OR "scale-free") AND ("Abstract":"features") AND ("Abstract":"crash" OR "impact") AND ("Abstract":"accelerometer")` | `TITLE-ABS-KEY("scale-invariant" AND features AND crash AND accelerometer AND transfer)` | `scale invariant features accelerometer crash detection transfer small scale vehicle` | | | |
| | `("Abstract":"domain shift") AND ("Abstract":"crash detection") AND ("Abstract":"sensor")` | `TITLE-ABS-KEY("domain shift" AND "crash detection" AND sensor)` | `crash detection domain shift sensor placement vehicle type IMU` | | | |

## Other checks only you can do
1. Check the hardware-related legal numbers (433 to 434.79 MHz, 10 mW e.r.p., 10% duty cycle for India; 865 to 867 MHz for deployment) against the WPC/DoT notifications. They came from a secondary summary.
2. Look up the full text of EN 1317-2 (through your university library) for: the ASI level C limit, the 13 Hz filter, and the flail-space distances for THIV. See `docs/05_data_code/SEVERITY.md`, section "Verification of constants, 2026-11-11". The code carries `# UNVERIFIED` marks until you do.
3. Open the Osaka University paper on environment-aware probabilistic scheduling for emergency LoRa networks, and the arXiv paper 2009.09397 on reliable LoRaWAN uplinks. Neither could be opened here.
4. Check SAE 2025-01-5046 (pulse functions for EDR delta-V) and the clipped tibial acceleration restoration paper (Sensors 2023) before writing N14. They are close to the saturation-aware estimator.
5. Email the VZCrash authors the cross-split duplicate list (`docs/04_audits/vzcrash_email_draft.md`); nothing has been sent.

# FILE: NEGATIVES_REGISTER.md

# Negatives register

Generated by `crashguard.fin_docs` from `results/results.json`. One row per negative result. **No original verdict is removed or softened.** Causes marked (inferred) are not tested. A fix is a revised design tested after seeing the negative; it is reported next to the original, never in place of it.

| Item | What was tested | Original verdict | Suspected cause | Fix tried and outcome | What remains open |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| N20 false-alarm control (conformal guarantee) | Whether a conformal threshold holds the false-alarm rate at its target on the de-duplicated VZCrash test set. | NOT SUPPORTED (earlier rounds): rate about 0.053 at the 5% target on the supplied split; at 1% it holds only at 1.96 standard errors. | Validation and test are not exchangeable (inferred); vehicle-size shift breaks it by up to 8 points. | Yes: T7 variants (NOT SUPPORTED) and P4c variants V1 to V5 (NOT SUPPORTED); F2 rerun of H-P4c NOT_RUN (see F rows). | No vehicle identifier in VZCrash; exact control costs recall. |
| N28 equal-escalation test | Whether the class-conditional upper-end policy (P3) beats the lowered-threshold table at equal escalation. | NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND: pool-level mis-triage P3 minus P1e at +-16 g -0.003 [-0.011, +0.003] (interval includes zero); decision rule not met at +-16, +-32, +-64 g. | A lowered table reaches the same escalation; the interval adds nothing over it (as found; the cause is not tested further). | Yes: B2 (P1+, NOT SUPPORTED), F3 with improved estimates (H-F3a NOT SUPPORTED). | Whether any estimate quality makes the interval pay. |
| H-P1 (direct classifier) | Direct ordinal classifier and 1D CNN against the anchored estimator on the sealed fold. | NOT SUPPORTED: macro-F1 difference at +-16 g +0.169 [+0.046, +0.291]; criterion met in 0 of 3 ranges. | Small sealed fold (186 records, 7 Low) and 913 real pulses (inferred). | Yes: F1 with real plus synthetic training, original criterion (H-F1 NOT SUPPORTED); r = 0 control and best ratio are in `f.f1.*`. | Whether a larger real set would change it. |
| H-P2 (anchored interval) | Whether [plain, plain x q] is narrower than the relative-residual interval at 90% coverage. | NOT SUPPORTED: coverage 0.914 (condition met) but width ratio reduction 0.018 against 0.20 needed. | A one-sided bound from the plain estimate cannot be much narrower when the plain estimate under-reads by a factor of about 2 (inferred). | Yes: F1 (H-F1b NOT SUPPORTED). | A narrower interval with 90% coverage. |
| H-P3a (abstention) | Whether abstaining on 30% of events halves under-triage among retained events. | NOT SUPPORTED: ratio 0.744 (upper end 0.915), needed at most 0.5; retained p90 error 18.8%. | The reliability score ranks errors weakly (inferred). | Yes: F1 (H-F1c NOT SUPPORTED). | A better reliability score. |
| H-P3b (abstain as escalation signal) | Whether abstain routing mis-triages less than the lowered table at equal escalation. | NOT SUPPORTED: mis-triage A 0.081 against lowered table 0.048. | The abstain signal is a noisier escalation signal than lowering thresholds (inferred). | Yes: F3 (H-F3b NOT SUPPORTED). | Same as above. |
| H-P4b (augmentation) | Whether augmentation with merging raises recall at the 1% target on the strict set by 0.02. | NOT SUPPORTED: -0.0175 [-0.0219, -0.0132]. | Augmented crash copies shift the score distribution against the validation threshold (inferred). | Yes: F2 with surrogate windows (H-F2a NOT SUPPORTED). | Whether better surrogate windows help. |
| H-P4c (calibration under vehicle-size shift) | Whether group-wise calibration beats the trivial rule by 0.02 recall with the rate held. | NOT SUPPORTED: no variant gains recall over the trivial rule; variants that bring the rate to the target lose recall (see docs/03_results/POSITIVE_PUSH_RESULTS.md). | Structure of the vehicle-size categories (inferred). | Not re-run: H-F2f NOT_RUN. | Whole item open. |
| H-X1 (fixed-point forest) | 16-bit forest loses at most 0.005 AP and cuts flash by 30%. | NOT SUPPORTED: the AP clause held (raw p 0.001); the flash clause fails by arithmetic (22.2% for 16-bit thresholds in the primary layout). | Arithmetic of the node layout. | Yes: F2 AP clause with surrogate training (H-F2d NOT SUPPORTED); the flash clause cannot change. | A layout with a larger saving (P4a found one, +37% flash by fewer trees). |
| H-X2 (trigger cascade) | Whether a cheap cascade raises the crash pass share by 2 points at equal non-crash trigger rate. | NOT SUPPORTED: gain 0.000. | Closer to N15 (c), which also found nothing (stated in the preregistration). | Not re-run: H-F2c NOT_RUN (surrogate windows are trigger-aligned). | A record-level surrogate would be needed. |
| H-X3 (group-aware training) | Whether down-weighting near-identical training windows raises recall at the 1% target by 1 point. | NOT SUPPORTED: gain +0.0011 (lower end -0.0022). | Near-duplicates are few among training windows relative to the whole set (inferred). | Yes: F2 (H-F2b NOT SUPPORTED). | - |
| H-X5 (rank preservation) | Spearman correlation of fill + ridge delta-V with the reference at least 0.90. | NOT SUPPORTED: 0.751 [0.678, 0.805]; plain estimate 0.841. | The fill + ridge correction adds noise to the ranking (inferred). | Not re-run: H-F1e NOT_RUN. | Whole item open. |
| H-X6 (anchored estimator) | p90 error of the anchored estimator against fill + ridge. | SUPPORTED on split R in the original; the gain has its interval above zero on split A only among G, A, O (so it failed on G and O). | The estimator over-corrects tests where plain already exceeds the reference (as noted). | Not re-run: H-F1d NOT_RUN. | G and O. |
| H-X7 (two-part fusion) | Whether a two-part pair reaches 0.80 where neither part alone does. | NOT SUPPORTED: 0 of 32 pair-rule combinations count. | Mounting error and synchronisation are not modelled (the test favours the pair); pole and side modes dominate the shortfall (inferred). | No. | - |
| H-X8 (sequential rule) | Whether a sequential rule decides 100 ms earlier than early exit at equal recall. | NOT SUPPORTED: median time gain 120 ms at the 1% target (lower end 40 ms), but at the 5% target the sequential rule is later (median difference -20 ms, early exit minus sequential). | The 5% target favours the early-exit rule (as measured); why is not tested. | Not re-run: H-F2e NOT_RUN. | - |
| T4 single-car loss case | Whether the SCT policy reaches the base as often as the table for a single car at 60% loss. | PARTIAL (earlier rounds). | Spacing of retries against loss (inferred). | Quick retries repair it (earlier round). | - |
| B2: P1+ against P0 (this round) | Flash and hold on the severity table. | NOT SUPPORTED: 2 of 12 held-out cells gain 5 points; 1 loses by more than 2. | Gain of the full fast path comes from more than flash and hold (inferred: P3 is higher in most cells). | - | Which of the remaining parts (upper-end class, pre-emption, reserved share) matter is only partly separated in G2. |
| H-G3a slotted fast path (this round) | Reserved slots for Severe and Critical alerts against pure ALOHA. | NOT SUPPORTED: all 16 of 16 held-out cells lose more than 2 points (pooled -0.158). | 8 slots per 30 s frame for a burst of 30 cars (inferred; slot sweep in G5). | Not tuned (preregistered parameters). | Other slot structures were not tested. |
| H-G3b listen-before-talk (this round) | LBT for the fast path. | NOT SUPPORTED: 2 of 16 cells gain 5 points; pooled +0.018. | Hidden terminals (sensing probability 0.7, assumed) and the short backoff (inferred). | - | 433 MHz regulatory status UNVERIFIED. |
| H-G3c V2V relay (this round) | One repeat by a neighbour when the ACK is missing. | NOT SUPPORTED: pooled -0.002 on reception within 30 s in the 8 cells with loss 40%. | The relayed copy meets the same loss and collisions as the sender's own retries (inferred). | - | - |

Layer findings of block G that are negative are the three mechanism rows above and the single-assumption flips in `f.g.g5.flips.*`; the G7 summary is in `docs/03_results/FINISH_RESULTS.md`.

- Regulatory status of listen-before-talk on 433 MHz: UNVERIFIED.
- A second public vehicle-pulse dataset (T13) and an external delta-V severity scale (T6): candidates could not be opened.

- MDPI journal pages: HTTP 403; Europe PMC copies were read where available.
- Wald 1945 (sequential probability ratio test): mentioned, not opened.
- analog.com data sheet pages were not reachable by curl on 2026-11-11 (see `docs/05_data_code/SENSOR_DATASHEETS.md` for what was read and where).

# FILE: NOVELTY.md

# Novelty: what can and cannot be claimed

The contribution is the **combination**, measured end to end. Standalone IoT accident detection is not new.

## Already in the literature (do not claim)
- Accelerometer + GPS crash alert (2020, 2022 project papers).
- Cancel/countdown before sending (2022: 30 s alarm; 2024: cancel button).
- Accelerometer rollover detection (2024 Visual Situation paper).
- LoRa for accident/disaster alerts (2022 search & rescue; 2025 vehicle disaster; 2025 drones).
- Encrypted/integrity-checked LoRa packets (2022 search & rescue paper); ECIES accident reports (2023); blockchain + LoRa (2025 drones).

## Candidate points (each needs its measurement)
| # | Claim | Evidence needed |
|---|---|---|
| N1 | Real-crash-trained detector on a cheap MCU, with a transfer test to the scale model | S2–S6, E6, E7 |
| N2 | Impact side + angle from one IMU in the alert | E1 |
| N3 | HMAC + anti-replay + signed ACK on the MCU, with the spoofed-ACK case | S7, E5 |
| N4 | Severity-aware delivery under a 10% duty-cycle budget | S8, S9, E4 |
| N5 | Measured stage-by-stage latency against HTTP/MQTT/CoAP | E4 + protocols.csv |
| N6 | Open labelled scale-model dataset | release of hw_events + windows |
| N7 | Measured value of a cheap vibration array | E8 |
| N14 | Saturation-aware severity estimator (a pulse-model fill of clipped samples) | `n14.*` held-out result |
| N15 | Trigger-aware training (joint trigger and classifier design) | `n15.*` seed-averaged paired result |
| N16 | Severity- and budget-aware delivery scheduler (simulation, supporting only) | `n16.*` held-out scenarios |

In the 24 project PDFs, no text match was found for HMAC, duty cycle or delta-V, no protocol acknowledgement, and "replay" appears only for voice spoofing and for a federated-learning scheme (`docs/03_results/CLAIM_EVIDENCE.md`). This shows absence only in those 24 papers. Searches beyond them (`docs/06_references/PRIOR_WORK.md`) found an ESP32 HMAC-counter component, the LoRaWAN retransmission-versus-replay discussion, delta-V and direction from consumer MEMS in a full-size crash, a clipped-sensor estimator patent and two-stage crash pipelines, so each point below is worded as "we found / in the sources checked". IEEE Xplore and Scopus could not be searched; the strings are in `docs/06_references/MANUAL_SEARCH_CHECKLIST.md`.

## Wording rules
- The vibration sensors confirm an impact; they do not locate it (the chassis is about 20 cm).
- Scale-model "severity" is impact strength, not injury severity.
- India 433–434.79 MHz: 10 mW e.r.p. and 10% duty cycle come from a secondary summary. Verify them against WPC/DoT. The 10 kHz channel-width mismatch is a testbed limitation; a deployed system would use 865–867 MHz.

<!-- novelty:start -->

## Status after the software milestones (generated, see `report/novelty_summary.md`)

**Headline contribution:** the integrated system (MCU detector, impact direction, authenticated severity-aware LoRa delivery), evaluated end to end. N8 and N9 are evaluation-rigour contributions, not the headline. N11 is a results breakdown, not a contribution. N16 is supporting evidence from simulation, not a contribution. N2 is a supporting feature and N6 is weak (RC-car and open-dataset precedents). N12 is an independent re-discovery of a known LoRaWAN issue in a custom point-to-point link, with a bounded fix.

Statuses: SUPPORTED = software evidence exists for the claim as worded (N14 to N16: the gain clears the 95% interval on data not tuned on); PARTIAL = software done, hardware NOT_RUN or simulation only; NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND = tried, no gain beyond the interval; NOT_YET = no evidence yet. The prior-work record is in `docs/06_references/PRIOR_WORK.md`. Hardware IDs H1 to H6 are the minimum plan (see `docs/02_preregistration/EXPERIMENTS.md`).

| Point | Claim | Role | Status | Closest prior work |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HL | Integrated system: MCU detector + impact direction + authenticated, severity-aware LoRa delivery (software parts built and tested; the end-to-end evaluation on hardware has not been run) | headline | PARTIAL | Each part has prior work; no source found that combines them end to end |
| N1 | Real-crash-trained detector on a cheap MCU, with a transfer test to the scale model | component | PARTIAL | TinyML crash projects; Katsidimas 2023 (forest on Arduino); Balfaqih 2022; emlearn |
| N2 | Impact side and angle from one IMU in the alert | component | NOT_YET | Ebert 2016 (phone IMU, RC car); Balfaqih 2022; Tadic 2016 (PDOF from MEMS) |
| N3 | HMAC, anti-replay counter and signed ACK on the MCU, with the spoofed-ACK case | component | PARTIAL | LoRaWAN MIC and counter; ESP32 HMAC-counter component (msg_auth) |
| N4 | Severity-aware delivery under a 10% duty-cycle budget | component | PARTIAL | Priority-aware LoRa scheduling; LoRaWANSim |
| N5 | Measured stage-by-stage latency against HTTP, MQTT and CoAP | component | NOT_YET | IETF MAPRG protocol slides |
| N6 | Open labelled scale-model dataset | component | NOT_YET | Ebert 2016 (RC car); Katsidimas 2023 (open impact data, plates) |
| N7 | Measured value of a cheap vibration-sensor array | component | NOT_YET | SW-420 hobby projects |
| N13 | Physics-based severity (delta-V + ASI) from one IMU window, carried in the authenticated alert, with a measured requirement on accelerometer range and rate (NHTSA crash tests, mostly high-speed barrier tests, not representative of all real crashes) | component | PARTIAL | EDR delta-V and ASI studies; NHTSA EDR round robin; Tadic 2016 |
| N14 | Clipping restoration for delta-V at low sensor range: a pulse-fit fill with a small ridge correction against spline restoration and plain integration (one range, re-split tests, not a blind test; fill + ridge was named primary after earlier results were seen; NHTSA tests are mostly high-speed barrier tests and are not representative of all real crashes) | component | SUPPORTED | Spline restoration (Ruder; Chan 2023); US 7,643,921; `SAE 2025-01-5046` pulse fits |
| N15 | Trigger-aware training: joint selection, cost-sensitive training and a learned trigger raise system recall at a fixed false-alarm rate | component | NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND | Cascade detectors; two-stage crash pipelines (Kubin 2021, Taccari 2018) |
| N16 | Severity- and budget-aware delivery scheduler (reserve, severity-and-age order, ACK-feedback back-off, optional spreading-factor raise): improves on a fixed retry policy; against a severity table it does not clear zero in the original held-out scenarios and does in the later burst simulator (S5) (SIMULATED; two simulators with different scenario sets; supporting evidence, not a contribution) | component | PARTIAL | Priority-based LoRa allocation (Wei 2023); duty-cycle blackouts (Tomic 2020); back-off with jitter |
| N17 | Impact direction (front against side) from one low-rate IMU, checked on NHTSA crash tests (validation of a supporting feature, not a contribution; NHTSA tests are mostly high-speed barrier tests and are not representative of all real crashes) | extra experiment | SUPPORTED | Kusano and Gabler 2013; Tadic 2016; Ebert 2016 |
| N18 | Severity measures against dummy injury criteria: delta-V adds no positive association beyond impact speed for frontal chest and head criteria (the partial association with chest acceleration is small and negative); ASI tracks chest acceleration (descriptive, small sample, NHTSA tests) | extra experiment | NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND | NHTSA and ESV dummy-criteria literature (snippets) |
| N19 | Early classification: a sequential early-exit rule over six length-specific forests decides about one second earlier (mean over triggered crash events, both false-alarm targets). The part that clears its interval is the time saving; the recall gain over the single full-length forest is an ensemble effect, not an early-exit effect (VZCrash, second trigger on pre-selected events; timing read from the window length, no ESP32 timing) | extra experiment | SUPPORTED | Airbag-algorithm timing; early-exit classification (snippets) |
| N20 | Guaranteed false-alarm control by split-conformal thresholds: the guarantee does not hold on the de-duplicated test set and the threshold is no better than the validation threshold | extra experiment | NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND | Split-conformal anomaly detection (snippets) |
| N21 | Sensor-spoofing robustness: a bounded added signal can make non-crash windows alarm from about `0.5` g; simple defences help a little against false crashes at small budgets and cost on hidden crashes (simulation on dataset windows) | extra experiment | PARTIAL | Tu et al. 2018 (acoustic injection); Son 2015 and Trippel 2017 (cited) |
| N22 | Many-car LoRa capacity: at SF7 and the low default alert rate the channel, not the base's ACK duty budget, limits delivery up to `500` cars (at SF12 and high alert rates both limits are large); spaced retries recover most of a burst in the X6 simulator (analytical and simulated) | extra experiment | PARTIAL | Tomic 2020; Wei 2023; Zhong and Nie 2024; LoRa ALOHA and capture analyses |
| N23 | Scale-model transfer by physical scaling of VZCrash events: amplitude dominates, the scale-free features are not invariant (a plausibility check, no claim) | extra experiment | PARTIAL | Scale-model crash similitude (snippets) |
| N24 | Conformal intervals for the clipped delta-V estimate: coverage is about nominal, but the class rule built on them (report the class below when the lower bound is lower, plus a flag) raises under-estimation instead of lowering it (grouped CV+, NHTSA tests, no simulation) | extra experiment | NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND | CHOIR (conformal injury severity on police records); jackknife+ and CV+ (abstracts opened) |
| N25 | Prevalence-adaptive alert threshold: the existing threshold gives very low precision at realistic prevalence, but no tested rule (prior-shift adjusted, minimum expected cost, target precision) raises precision within five points of recall at prevalences up to one per cent (VZCrash pools resampled; costs are assumptions) | extra experiment | NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND | Saerens 2002 prior shift by EM; Elkan 2001 cost-sensitive decisions (abstracts opened) |
| N26 | Severity-Classified Transmission (SCT): a fast path with a short authenticated flash, a spaced-retry rule and no hold for Critical alerts raises critical reach within ten seconds in bursts, also against a severity table with spaced retries; against the severity table it does not help in steady state (it does against the spaced-retry table, whose spacing costs reach there) and it is worse than the table for a single car at high loss (EXPLORATORY); in ablations (EXPLORATORY) the flash, the hold and the spreading-factor raise carry the gain, while the reserved duty share and the CANCEL message show no effect on reach, and faster retries repair the single-car case but cost reach in bursts (SIMULATED; one-hour duty rule, parameters tuned on separate scenarios) | extra experiment | PARTIAL | Tamang 2022 urgent packets; Shao 2020 interrupt access; Wei 2023; Tomic 2020 |
| N27 | Bounded model check of the alert protocol: within the stated bounds the counter, replay, re-ACK, signed-ACK and CANCEL rules hold against a bounded attacker; strict anti-replay without the re-ACK loses liveness; counters that are not persisted break replay protection and ACK matching (ANALYTICAL, a check of a model, not a proof of the firmware) | extra experiment | PARTIAL | Gao 2019 (Scyther); Dong 2024 (PAT); Wesemeyer 2020 (Tamarin, join procedure) |
| N28 | Uncertainty-aware SCT (UA-SCT): taking the class from the upper end of a conformal class interval lowers mis-triage of Severe and Critical events against the severity table (the preregistered hypotheses H-a and H-c hold; for H-b the escalation part holds and the airtime cap of P4 barely binds), at the cost of many more escalations of non-severe events; at equal escalation the same reduction is reached by lowering the thresholds of the plain table, and critical reach within 30 s in bursts is slightly lower than with plain SCT (SIMULATED on NHTSA class pairs, mostly high-speed barrier tests, not representative of all real crashes) | extra experiment | NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND | CHOIR; Tamang 2022; Shao 2020 |
| N29 | Tail-aware clipping estimator: a quantile boosting model on clipping descriptors and pulse-family features lowers the `90th`-percentile delta-V error against fill + ridge at +-16 g on repeated grouped cross-validation (interval above zero) and, as point estimates above 5 points, on two of three fixed splits (only one of them with its interval above zero) (preregistered; NHTSA tests are mostly high-speed barrier tests and are not representative of all real crashes); plain gradient boosting, tried as an EXPLORATORY variant, has a lower `90th` percentile still | extra experiment | SUPPORTED | Chan 2023 spline restoration (opened); other named papers not opened |
| N30 | Deterministic bounds and adaptive conformal intervals for the clipped delta-V estimate: the plain estimate is a lower bound for almost every test at +-16 g (not at `+-32 g` and `+-64 g`), but the pulse-family upper bound does not contain the reference for enough tests and normalised-score intervals are wider, not narrower, where clipping is mild (preregistered; NHTSA tests are mostly high-speed barrier tests and are not representative of all real crashes) | extra experiment | NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND | CHOIR; jackknife+ and CV+ (abstracts opened) |
| N31 | Clip-onset early severity flag: the time from the trigger to the earliest near-full-scale sample flags only part of the Severe or Critical events at +-16 g, with a median time saved under 100 ms, and it misses more Severe or Critical tests than a running delta-V threshold (preregistered; NHTSA tests are mostly high-speed barrier tests and are not representative of all real crashes; events that never clip cannot be flagged) | extra experiment | NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND | Early-exit classification (snippets); N19 |
| N32 | Load-adaptive escalation: five variants (a per-car controller, a token bucket, a base-station beacon, severity-scaled backoff, graceful demotion) do not raise critical reach within 10 s by 3 points over a matched fixed lowered-threshold rule in the simulated burst cells (small gains in a few cells); the beacon variant lowers it in some cells (SIMULATED; preregistered; every channel and traffic value is an assumption) | extra experiment | NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND | Tamang 2022; Shao 2020 (abstract) |
| N33 | Authentication-tag length: an 8-byte tag keeps the forgery work factor (a count of attempts, which the duty limit does not cap) far above 2^32 attempts (ANALYTICAL; truncated HMAC, secret key, no proof), and a 4-byte tag raises critical reach within 10 s in simulated bursts by less than one point (SIMULATED), so there is no capacity reason to shorten the tag | extra experiment | PARTIAL | Truncated tags are a known trade-off; no source opened |
| N34 | Severity-dependent hold: a 2 s hold of Severe alerts cuts the share of false Severe alerts that reach the base without a significant loss in critical reach (SIMULATED; cancel model and costs are assumptions), while the cost model puts the optimal Critical hold at zero only above a cost ratio that depends on the precision (ANALYTICAL) | extra experiment | PARTIAL | None opened |
| N8 | Dataset audit: identical recordings across the official VZCrash splits | evaluation rigour | SUPPORTED | Leakage audits of other datasets; VZCrash paper (III-B) states vehicle-level splits |
| N9 | Trigger-aware evaluation: system-level recall including the trigger stage | evaluation rigour | SUPPORTED | Taccari et al. 2018; two-stage patents; VZCrash real-world population test |
| N10 | Size against accuracy on the MCU, with C-export equivalence | design finding | SUPPORTED | Leaf-refinement paper (2021); emlearn |
| N11 | False-positive rate on near_miss events alone (a results breakdown, not a contribution) | results breakdown | SUPPORTED | VZCrash paper false-positive counts; phone crash-detection studies |
| N12 | Retry versus replay: an independent re-discovery of a known LoRaWAN issue in a custom point-to-point link, with a bounded fix | design finding | SUPPORTED | Known LoRaWAN retransmission-versus-replay issue (ChirpStack discussion) |
| SF | Scale-free features keep most of the AP (a hypothesis for the transfer argument in N1; the physical-scaling check in N23 shows them not invariant) | component | PARTIAL | none found |

# FILE: NOVELTY_SCAN.md

# Novelty scan (round 3, 2026-11-11)

Candidate contribution ideas that are not N1 to N23, testable in software, with the closest prior work, the experiment, the risk and scores from 1 to 5 for (a) distance from the closest prior work, (b) feasibility in three hours, (c) strength of the existing evidence at the time of the scan. Scores were assigned before the S11 and UA-SCT runs. Only sources that were opened and read support anything here; snippet-only sources are labelled and support nothing. A source missing from this list is not proof that none exists. Wording rules: no "first", "novel" or "state-of-the-art"; no claim beyond the evidence.

## 3. Does any own idea score higher than the author's four (M1 to M4)?
The best of M1 to M4 is M2 with 12. O2 scores 13 but it is already implemented (S2) as an extension of N8, so it is not an extra contribution. O1 and O3 score 12 (a tie with M2). O3 has the highest feasibility and a decision rule that can be fixed in advance, so it was implemented as **S13** (`docs/02_preregistration/EXPERIMENTS_EXTRA.md`, rule written before the run). O1 also qualifies on feasibility but only one idea was implemented.

## 4. What S13 found
SCT improves critical reach within 10 s in bursts over the spaced-retry table at every sensor range (the decision rule of S13 is met); the spaced-retry table by itself recovers most of the reach within 30 s. Numbers: `results.json` keys `s13.*`, reported in `report/software_results.md`.

## 5. What the closing block found (T1 to T13, 2026-11-11)
- **U1 (UA-SCT, N28).** At the same escalation the severity table with lowered thresholds does as well as UA-SCT on mis-triage (T1), the interval family has the larger trade-off area (T2), and UA-SCT is cheaper than the table only if a missed severe event costs several times a false escalation (T3). The idea therefore scores lower than it did before the test: the part that is not just a choice of threshold shows no benefit. The status of N28 is NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND beyond thresholds.
- **N26 (SCT).** The flash and the severity hold carry the gain in the ablations (T9); the reserved duty share and the CANCEL message change nothing in critical reach in the cells tested. Faster retries repair the single-car weakness but lower reach when many cars share the channel (T4, T9).
- **N27.** A CANCEL that overtakes its alert is lost in the design; remembering it fixes the model (T12). The firmware does not yet do this.
- **No new source was opened for this block.** A second public dataset (T13) and an external delta-V severity scale (T6) could not be opened and are recorded as NOT_RUN.

## 6. v2 studies (branch `v2-algorithms`, 2026-11-12)
No new paper was opened for the v2 studies. The prior work named in the task (Jao and Shkel 2022; Zhang et al. 2026) could not be opened in round 3 and is still not used; Chan et al. 2023 (opened in an earlier round) is the spline baseline. The triangle fit and the Gaussian-process fill in Study A are our own implementations of the ideas described in the task, not their code or results. N29 to N34 in `report/novelty_summary.md` therefore state only the earlier opened sources (CHOIR and jackknife+ for the interval study; Tamang 2022 and Shao 2020 for the delivery studies) and say "none opened" where nothing was opened. The results: N29 supported on the NHTSA tests (tail error), N30, N31 and N32 no improvement found, N33 and N34 partial (analytical and simulated).

Wusk and Gabler, 25th ESV paper 17-0407: the NHTSA address (https://www-nrd.nhtsa.dot.gov/pdf/ESV/Proceedings/25/25ESV-000407.pdf) appeared in a search result but the fetch returned HTTP 403, so it was not opened; the statement that delta-V and ASI are only weakly related to dummy injury criteria is used only as the author's description of it (unverified here) and no number from it is used. ## P1 (direct ordinal triage from clipped-signal descriptors and a CNN)
Queries: "monotone constrained gradient boosting ordinal classification crash severity clipped accelerometer direct classification versus delta-V regression"; "Jao Shkel low-g accelerometer high-g shock saturation extension range machine learning"; "Zhang 2026 accelerometer saturation crash detection delta-V estimation ..." (all 2026-11-12). Closest prior work opened: HEROS-GAN (range extension by a GAN, no severity class), Chan et al. 2023 (spline restoration of clipped tibial signals), US 7,643,921 (extrapolation rule). What would remain new if H-P1 holds: skipping delta-V and classifying the severity class directly from clipping descriptors on a pooled, sealed set of 913 crash-test records with mode inferred from the pulse. If it fails, the result is "the direct route is not better than binning an estimate on this set".

## P2 (interval from a physical lower bound)
Queries: "Conformal Risk Control Angelopoulos ...", "conformal prediction crash injury severity police records CHOIR" (2026-11-12). Closest: CHOIR (police records, ordinal intervals, no sensor), jackknife+/CV+ (our earlier N24 interval), US 7,643,921 (a slope rule, no interval). Difference: a one-sided interval [plain, plain x q] that uses the fact that clipping makes the plain estimate a lower bound, with q conformal on the ratio. Expected weakness: the lower bound fails more often at +-32 and +-64 g.

## P3 (selective prediction and abstain as an escalation signal)
Queries: "Selective classification for deep neural networks Geifman El-Yaniv risk-coverage" (2026-11-12). Closest: Geifman and El-Yaniv 2017 (the risk-coverage framework; not specific to sensors). Difference: the reliability score comes from clipping descriptors, and the abstain event is routed to a fast alert path in a LoRa protocol simulator. P3b retries the equal-escalation test that N28 failed with a different signal; if it fails again N28 stays NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND.

## P4 (VZCrash detector: memory, augmentation, false-alarm guarantee)
Queries: "Conformal Risk Control ...", "emlearn" repository page (2026-11-12). Closest: emlearn (tree-to-C export), conformal risk control, CV+. Earlier results of this project (X1 quantisation, X3 group-aware training, X4 conformal false-alarm control) are the nearest items and are cited as such; P4 re-tests them with a Pareto sweep, augmentation and group-wise calibration. VZCrash events carry no vehicle identifier, so "vehicle groups" are the four vehicle-size categories; this limits what P4c can show.

---

# FILE: PACKET_FORMAT.md

# Alert packet (97 bytes) and ACK

The byte layout below is a **proposal for software tests**. Align it with firmware v2 and correct this file if the firmware differs. The total must equal what the firmware sends.

| Off | Size | Field | Type |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | version | u8 |
| 1 | 1 | msg_type (1 = alert, 2 = ACK, 3 = heartbeat) | u8 |
| 2 | 4 | node_id | u32 |
| 6 | 4 | event_id | u32 |
| 10 | 4 | counter (monotonic, persisted in flash) | u32 |
| 14 | 4 | timestamp_ms | u32 |
| 18 | 1 | severity (0 low, 1 moderate, 2 severe, 3 critical) | u8 |
| 19 | 1 | impact_side (0 F, 1 R, 2 L, 3 Rt, 4 V) | u8 |
| 20 | 2 | angle_deg×10 | u16 |
| 22 | 4 | lat ×1e7 | i32 |
| 26 | 4 | lon ×1e7 | i32 |
| 30 | 2 | peak_g ×100 | u16 |
| 32 | 2 | ml_prob ×10000 | u16 |
| 34 | 1 | rule_sev | u8 |
| 35 | 1 | sw_mask (5 SW-420 bits) | u8 |
| 36 | 1 | sw_first | u8 |
| 37 | 1 | rollover flag | u8 |
| 38 | 2 | delta_v_kmh ×100 (resultant delta-V from the severity code, 0 to 655.35 km/h) | u16 |
| 40 | 2 | asi ×100 (acceleration severity index) | u16 |
| 42 | 47 | reserved / extended telemetry (zeros if unused) | bytes |
| 89 | 8 | tag = HMAC-SHA256(key, bytes 0..88)[:8] | bytes |

**ACK**: version, msg_type = 2, node_id, acked event_id, acked counter, base counter, and an 8-byte tag. It is signed with the same key.

Security properties:
- **Provided**: integrity, authenticity, and replay rejection (counter > last seen).
- **Not provided**: confidentiality. GPS position is readable over the air.

LoRa airtime for 97 B at SF7 / 125 kHz / CR 4/5 / 8-symbol preamble / explicit header / CRC is about 169 ms (ANALYTICAL).

## Software reference implementation (src/crashguard/packet.py, 2026-11-10)
- Byte order is little-endian (an assumption; check against firmware v2).
- ACK layout: offset 0 version (u8), 1 msg_type = 2 (u8), 2 node_id (u32), 6 acked event_id (u32), 10 acked counter (u32), 14 base counter (u32), 18 tag (8 bytes) = 26 bytes. The tag covers bytes 0 to 17.
- **Retry and replay rule (changed 2026-11-10, found by our own testing).** A strict rule "accept only if counter > last seen" blocks a legitimate retry: when the base received an alert but the ACK was lost, the car repeats the same packet and the base rejects it as a replay, so the car never gets its ACK. The reference verifier now keeps the last 16 accepted (node_id, event_id, counter) entries together with their ACK bytes. An alert that matches one of them exactly and has a valid tag gets the identical ACK again (decision `reack`) and is not processed a second time (no new alert, base counter unchanged). Any other counter that is not above the last seen one, including the same counter with different content, is rejected. The size of the list (16) is a design choice that firmware flash and RAM must confirm.
- Side effect: a flood of replayed packets makes the base spend airtime on ACKs and can use up its duty-cycle budget, so that a genuine alert gets no ACK. **Mitigation (added 2026-11-10):** the base sends at most one ACK per (node_id, event_id) per retry interval (the car's shortest spacing between two tries: alert airtime plus the ACK listening window, about 0.43 s at SF7), and every ACK it sends is paid from the base station's duty-cycle budget (token bucket, refilled at the duty-cycle rate). A repeat inside the interval gets no ACK (`reack_limited`); a repeat when the budget is empty gets none either (`reack_no_budget`). The limit reduces the cost of a flood but does not remove it: a long enough flood still uses the budget. The reference verifier keeps the mitigation optional (off by default) so that the unmitigated behaviour can be tested; firmware should switch it on. A retry interval shorter than the car's real retry spacing would block genuine retries, so firmware must use the real value.
- A retry may also use a fresh counter; this is accepted as a new alert and the base must de-duplicate by event_id.
- Known gap (software test, `make security`): if the base loses its stored counter, a recorded alert is accepted again.

## Severity fields (added 2026-11-10)
Bytes 38 to 41 now carry `delta_v_kmh x100` and `asi x100` (both u16, little-endian), taken from the reserved bytes, so the alert is still 97 bytes and the tag still covers bytes 0 to 88 (changing either value after signing is rejected; see the attack suite). The existing `severity` byte (offset 18, 0 low to 3 critical) holds the class from `docs/05_data_code/SEVERITY.md`, and `rollover` (offset 37) the rollover flag. These describe crash severity, not diagnosed injury. Values above 655.35 are clipped.

<!-- security:start -->
## Forgery, counter and flood analysis (generated from `results/results.json`)

- **Blind forgery (ANALYTICAL).** The tag has 64 bits: one random-tag attempt is accepted with probability 5.421e-20; one expected success needs 1.845e+19 attempts. At the airtime limit (back-to-back 97 B packets) that is 9.891e+10 years at SF7 and 2.303e+12 years at SF12; at the 10% duty cycle ten times longer. This assumes the key stays secret.
- **Counter.** 32 bits (4.295e+09 values), advancing once per new alert. At the fastest legal rate (SF7, 10% duty cycle: 2127 alerts per hour) it wraps after 230 years; at one alert a minute after 8166 years. After the largest value no new alert is accepted: the key must be rotated (there is no epoch field in this layout). The attack suite has a case for it.
- **Replay flood.** The idempotent re-ACK makes the base transmit for replayed packets. The limit (one ACK per event per retry interval, every ACK paid from the duty-cycle budget) lowers the cost but a long enough flood still uses up the budget (attack suite and the replay study).
- **Shared key.** One key on both nodes: any holder can sign an alert for any node id (an attack-suite case documents it). No key rotation, no per-node keys, no encryption (position is readable).
- **Property tests and fuzzing.** `tests/test_security_properties.py` (hypothesis): the counter never goes backwards for any input stream, a re-ACK is idempotent, any single-bit flip of an alert or an ACK is rejected, truncated or padded packets are rejected, and the parser never crashes on random or mutated bytes (every outcome is accept or reject).
<!-- security:end -->

---

# FILE: POSITIVE_PUSH_RESULTS.md

# Positive-results push: results (generated)

Branch `positive-push` (from tag `journal-push-v3`), 2026-11-12. Software only; `data/hardware` was not read. Every number below is read from `results/results.json` (keys `p.*`) by `src/crashguard/pp_docs.py`. Labels: MEASURED_SOFTWARE unless stated; the UA-SCT cells of P3b are SIMULATED; flash and RAM estimates are ANALYTICAL. The design (preregistration, sealed fold, freeze commits) is in `docs/02_preregistration/PREREGISTRATION_V4.md` and `docs/05_data_code/SEALED_SPLIT.md`. NHTSA records are crash tests, mostly high-speed; the sensor is a software model; VZCrash events were selected on the device before release. Nothing here is a field or hardware result.

## Verdicts (Holm over seven preregistered hypotheses)

| Hypothesis | Raw p | Holm p | Criterion met | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H-P1 | 0.836 | 1.000 | 0 | NOT SUPPORTED |
| H-P2 | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0 | NOT SUPPORTED |
| H-P3a | 0.130 | 0.780 | 0 | NOT SUPPORTED |
| H-P3b | 0.906 | 1.000 | 0 | NOT SUPPORTED |
| H-P4a | 0.001 | 0.007 | 1 | SUPPORTED |
| H-P4b | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0 | NOT SUPPORTED |
| H-P4c | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0 | NOT SUPPORTED |

Of the seven hypotheses, 1 is SUPPORTED and 6 are NOT SUPPORTED. Anything outside the seven (P4d, the cross-validation of P1, the matched-escalation comparison of P3b, the exploratory interval variants) is EXPLORATORY and says so in its key and caption.

## One line per task

- **Z0** pooled set of 913 records (174 + 739) in 325 make-and-model groups; modes: frontal barrier 116, pole 529, side 58, other 210; development 543, validation 184, sealed 186 records (`docs/05_data_code/SEALED_SPLIT.md`).
- **Z1** mode inferred from the pulse: sealed-fold balanced accuracy 0.754 at +-16 g, 0.800 at +-32 g, 0.867 at +-64 g (100 Hz); every mode-using model is reported with the true mode (oracle, labelled) and the inferred mode.
- **P1 / H-P1: NOT SUPPORTED.** Criterion (macro-F1 +0.03 and under-triage -0.03, both intervals excluding zero, in at least two of three ranges) met in 0 ranges. At +-16 g the best deployable direct model (fe_none) against the best baseline (c): macro-F1 0.169 [0.046, 0.291], under-triage -0.032 [-0.197, 0.125].
- **P1 exploratory cross-validation (all 913 records, not a test):** the augmented CNN against the anchored estimator at +-16 g: macro-F1 0.086 [0.039, 0.130], under-triage -0.029 [-0.076, 0.018]; at +-32 g macro-F1 0.045 [0.003, 0.087]; at +-64 g macro-F1 -0.016 [-0.054, 0.021]. The direct GBM is not better than the anchored estimator (+-16 g macro-F1 -0.017 [-0.060, 0.018], under-triage 0.075 [0.041, 0.112]).
- **P2 / H-P2: NOT SUPPORTED.** Sealed coverage of the interval [plain, plain x q] at +-16 g: 0.914 [0.871, 0.957]; lower end overall 0.871 and for pole 0.868 (coverage condition met); mean upper-to-lower ratio 1.93 against 1.96 for the existing interval, a reduction of 0.018 (needed 0.20).
- **P3a / H-P3a: NOT SUPPORTED.** At +-16 g the anchored estimator's under-triage among retained events is 0.74 of the unselected rate (upper end 0.91; needed at most 0.5); retained p90 error 18.8% (needed at most 20%, met).
- **P3b / H-P3b: NOT SUPPORTED.** Abstaining to the fast path minus the lowered severity table: mis-triage 0.032 [-0.030, 0.114] at +-16 g (needed at most -0.02, interval below zero); N28 stays NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND.
- **P4a / H-P4a: SUPPORTED.** Selected on validation: trees 8, depth 8, k 14, q16; flash estimate 20.6 kB against 55.1 kB (ratio 0.374); AP loss 0.0023 [0.0013, 0.0036]; measured host size 21725 against 56004 bytes (x86-64, not an ESP32).
- **P4b / H-P4b: NOT SUPPORTED.** Augmentation with merging changes recall at the 1% target on the strict set by -0.0175 [-0.0219, -0.0132] (needed +0.02): it is lower.
- **P4c / H-P4c: NOT SUPPORTED.** In leave-one-vehicle-size-category-out evaluation every variant, including the trivial rule, has a false-alarm rate with upper end below target + 0.01 (pooled conformal: 0.0535 at the 5% target, upper 0.0567); no variant gains recall over the trivial rule, and the variants that bring the rate to the target or below lose recall (V4 -0.019, V5 -0.115 at the 5% target).
- **P4d (EXPLORATORY).** An int8 tiny 1D CNN (1633 weights, 1756 bytes of weights and biases) reached AP 0.9614 [0.9571, 0.9658] on the de-duplicated test set and 0.9403 [0.9332, 0.9470] on the strict set, against 0.9383 and 0.9010 for the 10-tree depth-9 forest (about 55 kB). No hypothesis and no multiplicity correction.
- **B1** CrashClip package: `crashclip/` (records, parts, split, evaluation, de-duplication audit, baselines). **B2** requirement calculator: `scripts/requirement_calculator.py`. **B3** `docs/05_data_code/REPRODUCE_FINISH.md`.

## Negative results

- **H-P1 (NOT SUPPORTED):** the direct route does not lower under-triage in a way the intervals can tell from zero in any range (at +-64 g the upper end of the difference is exactly 0.0, at +-32 g the point difference is positive); macro-F1 gains have intervals above zero at +-16 g and at +-32 g, but the preregistered conjunction fails in all three ranges.
- **H-P2 (NOT SUPPORTED):** the one-sided interval is not narrower than the relative-residual interval around fill + ridge (reduction near 2%, needed 20%); at +-32 and +-64 g the lower bound fails in more records, so coverage falls under 0.90.
- **H-P3a (NOT SUPPORTED):** abstaining on 30% of events lowers under-triage among the retained events only to about three quarters of the unselected rate.
- **H-P3b (NOT SUPPORTED):** an abstain signal sends fewer non-severe events to the fast path than the lowered table and does not mis-triage less, with or without matching escalation on the sealed fold.
- **H-P4b (NOT SUPPORTED):** augmentation with merging lowers recall at the 1% target on the strict set.
- **H-P4c (NOT SUPPORTED):** no group-wise calibration restores more recall than the trivial rule; the guarantee at target + 0.01 held for all variants including the trivial one, and exact control at the target costs recall.

# FILE: PRD.md

# Product Requirements Document: CrashGuard software

| | |
|---|---|
| Owner | Mritula S |
| Version | 1.0 (10 Nov 2026) |
| Scope | Software, data science and simulation. Hardware is out of scope except for ingesting its logs. |

## 1. Background
CrashGuard detects a collision on an ESP32 with an MPU6050 IMU at 100 Hz and five SW-420 vibration sensors. It estimates severity and impact side, and sends an HMAC-signed alert over 433 MHz LoRa (SX1278 / Ra-02) to an ESP32 base station, which returns a signed ACK.

Hardware results so far are in `data/hardware/`:
- an HTTP / MQTT / CoAP comparison;
- LoRa test and telemetry packets;
- gateway counters.

No crash has been classified yet. The software side must supply:
1. a detector trained on real crashes that fits on the ESP32;
2. checks of the alert design;
3. simulations of delivery under the legal duty-cycle limit;
4. a merge path for the hardware experiments.

## 2. Goals
| ID | Goal | Linked novelty point |
|---|---|---|
| G1 | Train and compare crash detectors on VZCrash with vehicle- or trip-held-out splits | N1 |
| G2 | Export a 15-tree, depth-6 random forest to plain C that matches Python exactly | N1 |
| G3 | Test transfer from full-size crashes to scale-model data, with all features and with scale-free features only | N1 |
| G4 | Specify and test the 97-byte signed alert and signed ACK against an attack suite | N3 |
| G5 | Calculate LoRa airtime and duty-cycle budgets, and simulate severity-aware delivery against a naive retry policy | N4 |
| G6 | Ingest hardware logs and produce all paper tables and figures, including the measured latency breakdown | N2, N4, N5, N7 |
| G7 | Produce a reproducible repo and a plain-language results section | N6 (dataset release support) |

## 3. Non-goals
- Firmware development. The firmware already exists; this repo only generates `crashguard_rf.h` and a benchmark sketch.
- Encryption of alerts (future work).
- Blockchain (future work).
- Claims about human injury severity.

## 4. Users
- **The author**: runs the pipeline and merges hardware data.
- **The reviewer or examiner**: reruns `make all` and checks claims against `results.json`.

## 5. Functional requirements
### FR1 Data (`make data`)
- Download VZCrash with `huggingface_hub` or `datasets`. On failure, stop with a clear message and never fall back to fake data.
- Write `docs/05_data_code/DATASET_FACTS.md`, marking each fact CONFIRMED_FROM_DATA or CONFIRMED_FROM_CARD/PAPER. Highlight any difference from `docs/05_data_code/DATASET.md`.
- Run quality checks: missing values, sampling-rate consistency, duplicates, constant signals, and class balance.

### FR2 Features (`make features`)
- Window of about 2.8 s around the trigger at 100 Hz; adapt to the dataset and document the choice.
- Compute the 14 features defined in `docs/05_data_code/FEATURES.md`, and flag the `scale_free` subset.

### FR3 Models (`make train`)
- Models to compare:
  - RF with 15 trees, depth 6 (the deployable model);
  - RF with 200 trees (reference);
  - logistic regression;
  - gradient-boosted trees;
  - a rule detector (peak and duration).
- Splits: train/validation/test held out by vehicle or trip. Thresholds are set on validation.
- Metrics: AP, precision, recall, F1, FPR at recall 0.95, ROC-AUC, confusion matrix, PR curve, and calibration curve. Report mean ± std over 5 seeds.
- Ablation: all features against `scale_free` only.
- Error analysis: the top 20 false positives and top 20 false negatives.

### FR4 Export (`make export`)
- `firmware_export/crashguard_rf.h`: `const` arrays, no heap, and `float predict_prob(const float f[14])`.
- An equivalence test: compile with gcc and compare against Python on the full test set. The maximum absolute difference must be ≤ 1e-6.
- Host size estimate (labelled ANALYTICAL) and `benchmark_rf.ino`, which times 1,000 calls on the ESP32.

### FR5 Security (`make security`)
- Encode and decode the packet as in `docs/01_protocol/PACKET_FORMAT.md`.
- Reference verifier with counter persistence.
- Attack suite with expected outcomes:

| Case | Expected |
|---|---|
| Valid | accept |
| Forged tag | reject |
| Tampered field | reject |
| Replay | reject |
| Old counter | reject |
| Spoofed ACK | reject |
| Base reboot, counter persisted | reject replay |
| Base reboot, counter lost | **document** the vulnerability |

### FR6 LoRa (`make lora`)
- Semtech airtime formula, parameterised. Check: 97 B at SF7/125 kHz/CR4-5 should come to about 169 ms.
- Alerts per hour at a 10% duty cycle for SF7 to SF12.
- Event-driven delivery simulation with a token bucket. Packet loss is swept from 0 to 60%. Compare the severity policy against a fixed 3-retry policy.

### FR7 Merge (`make merge`)
- Read `data/hardware/*.csv` using the schemas in `docs/05_data_code/HARDWARE_SCHEMA.md`. Any file that is missing gives `NOT_RUN`.
- When `hw_events.csv` is present, run the following:
  - E1 impact-side confusion matrix and angle error;
  - E2 crash detection metrics, rules against the model;
  - E3 severity against peak g;
  - E4 delivery rate and latency by distance and severity;
  - E5 the measured attack table, if logged;
  - E6 timings;
  - E7 transfer;
  - E8 SW-420 ablation.
- Plot the hardware protocol results next to the LoRa results.

### FR8 Report (`make report`)
- Write `report/software_results.md` with these sections: what was run, tables, figures with captions, "What these results do and do not show", and "Still needed from hardware".
- Every number in it must exist in `results.json`. A test enforces this.

## 6. Non-functional requirements
- Python 3.11 or later with pinned dependencies. Runs on a laptop CPU; there are no GPU requirements.
- `make all` should finish within about 2 hours on a laptop. Subsample for development with `FAST=1`.
- Code is typed, has docstrings and passes `ruff`. Tests cover the packet, airtime, export equivalence and merge schemas.

## 7. Acceptance criteria (definition of done)
1. `make all && make test` pass from a clean clone.
2. `results.json` contains every reported number, each with an origin label. Nothing is labelled MEASURED unless it was computed.
3. The C model is equivalent to the Python model (≤ 1e-6).
4. The airtime check passes (97 B at SF7 comes to about 169 ms).
5. The attack-suite outcomes match the table in FR5.
6. The merge runs on the dummy file (with watermarks) and reports NOT_RUN without real files.
7. `report/software_results.md` exists, contains no invented numbers, and includes a limitations section.

## 8. Risks and limitations
| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| The VZCrash schema differs from the notes | FR1 facts file; log in DECISIONS.md |
| No vehicle ID, so leakage between splits | Group by the most independent key and state it |
| Transfer to the scale model fails | Report it as a finding (it motivates own data) |
| Class imbalance | Use AP and PR curves, not accuracy |
| The simulation loss model is unrealistic | Label it SIMULATED and compare with E4 hardware |
| Regulatory figures are unverified | Check against the official WPC/DoT notification |

## 9. Milestones
| M | Deliverable |
|---|---|
| M1 | `make data` + DATASET_FACTS.md |
| M2 | features + models + figures |
| M3 | C export + equivalence |
| M4 | security suite |
| M5 | LoRa analytics + simulation |
| M6 | merge + report |

---

# FILE: PREREG_V4_AUDIT.md

# Audit of the V4 preregistration addendum (A2)

Written 2026-11-13 on branch `finish`. Sources: `git log` of `docs/02_preregistration/PREREGISTRATION_V4.md`, `results/tables/p_sealed_access.csv`, file modification times of the evaluation logs and tables, and the V4 tables. Nothing was re-scored for this audit; no tagged result is changed.

## Timeline (all +05:30, 2026-11-12)

| Event | Commit | Time |
|---|---|---|
| Preregistration v4 committed | `b566b9e` | 08:11:34 |
| Wording-only fix (forbidden word) | `2c742a43` | 08:11:49 |
| Freeze P4a (before any test scoring) | `1a3aff9` | 08:33:51 |
| **Earliest held-out scoring: P4a test split** (`scored_at` 08:34; sweep table written 08:37:21) | earliest committed in `35d97ef` | 08:38:56 |
| Freeze P4b and P4c | `7a42da3`, `ab6239f` | 08:39:03, 08:39:05 |
| Freeze P1 | `1d22511` | 08:39:42 |
| P4b scored (table and log last written 08:41:22) | earliest committed in `aa2f6bc` (the P4b fix `bf53a1e` is 08:39:31) | 08:41:22 (file time) |
| **Addendum 1 committed** | `fd1b95a` | **08:41:41** |
| **Initial scoring of the sealed NHTSA fold: P1** (table written 08:42:13) | `aa2f6bc` | 08:42:27 |
| P2, P3a and P3b scored | `0582cba` and later | 08:43 |
| P4c (initial run logged at `scored_at` 08:41; log last written 08:42:44) | `aa2f6bc` | see below |
| P4c repeated after a closure bug fix (logged deviation) | `840e293` | 08:53:58 |

What the timeline shows:
- The addendum is dated before the sealed NHTSA fold was scored (P1 to P3: all after 08:41:41), as it says.
- It is **not** dated before every held-out scoring. P4a (test split) was scored about seven minutes before it and P4b about 19 seconds before it. The addendum's own header says so ("P4a had been scored and P4b was being scored"; P4b had in fact just finished).
- P4c: `p_sealed_access.csv` records its initial scoring at minute resolution (08:41); its log was last written at 08:42:44, after the addendum commit. The records do not show whether the run started before or after 08:41:41. It is reported here as unresolved.

## Exact text of each resolved reading

Reading 1 (p-value margins):
> 1. **p-values.** The bootstrap p-value of a condition of the form "interval above zero" uses the margin 0 (share of resamples at or below 0 for a gain, at or above 0 for a reduction of under-triage). Where the criterion also names a point margin (0.03, 0.02, 0.20, 0.01 +alpha), that margin is checked on the point estimate; where the text of a hypothesis gives the margin for the p-value (H-P2 width clause 0.20, H-P3a retained p90 0.20, H-P4a 0.01, H-P4b 0.02, H-P4c alpha + 0.01 and 0.02) that margin is used.

Reading 5 (P4b set definitions):
> 5. **P4b.** `frames_full` returns the de-duplicated test events, so the de-duplicated set is the whole frame and the supplied (24,174-event) set is not scored. The initial run of the evaluation stopped before scoring because of a code error (`te.leaky` does not exist); see `results/tables/p_sealed_access.csv` and `docs/07_log/DECISIONS.md`.

Reading 6 (P4c set definitions):
> 6. **P4c.** In the leave-one-category-out evaluation V2 has no calibration data of the held-out group and falls back to the pooled V1 threshold, so V2 equals V1 there; the pooled supplied-test evaluation (secondary) is where V2 differs. V3 is scored in the leave-one-category-out evaluation only.

## Did any hypothesis threshold change?

No. The addendum says "None changes a hypothesis, metric, threshold or rule". A line-by-line comparison of the hypothesis paragraphs (H-P4b: +0.02, interval above zero; H-P4c: alpha + 0.01 and +0.02 over the trivial rule) between `b566b9e` and the addendum shows no edit to them. The only edits to text above the addendum are two single words changed by the wording-only fix (two words of the repository wording rule, changed to `leading` and `earliest`, commit `2c742a43`, logged); the addendum is an appended section.

## Verdicts under the stated reading and the stricter alternative reading (computed from the stored V4 tables)

| Hypothesis | Stated reading | Stricter alternative reading | Flips? |
|---|---|---|---|
| H-P4b | NOT SUPPORTED. Strict set, augmentation with merging: recall at the 1% target -0.0175 [-0.0219, -0.0132] (needed +0.02). | Every variant must reach +0.02 on both the strict and the de-duplicated set: no variant does (strict: merge +0.0038 [-0.0002, 0.0076], aug -0.0165; de-duplicated: merge +0.0030, aug -0.0129). NOT SUPPORTED. | No |
| H-P4c | NOT SUPPORTED. Leave-one-category-out: no variant gains 0.02 recall over the trivial rule at either target (gains from -0.1165 to -0.0001; V2 equals V1 here by the addendum's reading 6). | (a) False-alarm upper end must be at most alpha (no +0.01 slack): fails for V0 to V4 at 1% (upper ends 0.0115 to 0.0127); V5 meets it at both targets (0.0090, 0.0355) but loses 0.1165 and 0.1147 recall. (b) V2 scored on its own pooled supplied-test evaluation (where it differs from V1): at 1% gain +0.0123 [0.0053, 0.0194] (below 0.02), at 5% gain -0.0245. NOT SUPPORTED under both. | No |

**Plain statement: no verdict would flip under the alternative readings checked here.** The p-value readings (reading 1) affect only the Holm-adjusted p-values, and the one SUPPORTED hypothesis (H-P4a, raw p 0.001, Holm 0.007, AP-loss upper end 0.0036 against the 0.01 margin) does not depend on them.

Limits of this audit: the stricter readings are the ones named above; other readings could be defined, and a reading chosen after seeing results is EXPLORATORY. The P4c time ordering remains unresolved (see timeline).
