# FILE: PREREGISTRATION.md

# Preregistration: uncertainty-aware severity-classified transmission (UA-SCT)

Written 2026-11-11 and committed BEFORE any UA-SCT, SCT or class-interval run of this study. The primary metrics, hypotheses, baselines, seeds and analysis below are not changed after the runs. Any scenario or variant added later is labelled EXPLORATORY in results.json (`exploratory` in the key or note) and in the report. Software only: no hardware log is read, H1 to H8 are untouched. Every number is labelled MEASURED_SOFTWARE (NHTSA tests and the VZCrash-based pools), SIMULATED, ANALYTICAL or NOT_RUN; a step that cannot run is NOT_RUN with its reason.

## 0. What was already seen before this document (disclosure)
- The N16 and X6 results: the scheduler did not beat the severity table in steady state (critical delivery +0.006 [-0.000, +0.012]); in a burst of 30 critical alerts at SF7 with 100 cars it did (0.87 against 0.25).
- S5 (held-out burst sweep, 12 settings) and S9 (grouped CV+ intervals of the delta-V estimate at +-16, +-32 and +-64 g: coverage about nominal, intervals wide at +-16 g, the "class below plus flag" rule increased under-estimation). S9 was planned in `docs/02_preregistration/EXPERIMENTS_EXTRA.md` before it was run. The conformal class intervals of this study (section 3) are a different construction (ordinal classes, one-sided upper bound) and have NOT been run.
- The distribution of NHTSA reference classes (counts only, no estimator error): 48 tests below 30 km/h reference delta-V, 16 at 30 to 45, 19 at 45 to 60, 93 at 60 or more.

## 1. Question
The severity estimate has uncertainty (sensor range, clipping, estimator error). If the transmission class is taken from the UPPER end of a conformal interval over the ordered severity classes, fewer Severe and Critical events travel through the slow normal path, at the price of more escalations of non-severe events to the fast path.

## 2. Data and the point estimate (B1)
- 176 NHTSA crash tests (116 frontal, 60 side), the existing sensor model at +-16, +-32 and +-64 g. Point estimate of resultant delta-V: **fill + ridge (the N14 primary, named after earlier results were seen)** at 100 Hz, which is the rate the estimator constants (pulse-fit grid, release rule) were built for. At 200 Hz the plain estimator is run for all three ranges (descriptive); fill + ridge at 200 Hz is run only if the estimator code accepts that rate unchanged, otherwise NOT_RUN with the reason.
- Class: maximum of the delta-V class (24, 40, 56 km/h limits) and the ASI class (1.0, 1.4, 1.9) as in `severity.classify`; the ASI is that of the filled window (a point value). True class: the same rule on the reference delta-V and reference ASI.
- Groups: make and model (132 groups; primary split). Tests with identical recordings (2 identical-signal pairs found by hash) are merged into one group before any split so a duplicate cannot sit on both sides. Splits: the repeated grouped 5-fold cross-validation of split R (5 repeats, seed 42 + repeat). Inside each outer training set the groups are split at random into tuning (60%) and calibration (40%); the point estimator (family, level, release fraction, ridge strength) is fitted on the tuning groups only; the calibration groups are disjoint from tuning and test groups.

## 3. Conformal class intervals (B2)
Ordinal classes 0 to 3. Prediction c_hat = class of the point estimate. Scores on the calibration tests: two-sided s = |c_true - c_hat|; one-sided s_up = max(0, c_true - c_hat). Split-conformal quantile q = the ceil((n + 1)(1 - alpha))-th smallest score (the interval is the whole range when that index exceeds n). Two-sided interval [c_hat - q, c_hat + q]; upper end U = min(3, c_hat + q_up). Two versions: **pooled** (one q) and **class-conditional** (one q per predicted class, Mondrian; where a predicted class has fewer than 10 calibration tests the pooled q is used and this is counted). alpha = 0.10 (primary) and 0.05 (secondary, pre-registered). Reported per sensor range: coverage of the two-sided interval and the one-sided validity P(c_true <= U) with grouped (make and model) bootstrap 95% intervals (1,000 resamples), mean interval width in classes, share of tests whose upper end is above the point class, and the number of calibration tests per class. "Holds" = nominal coverage inside the 95% interval of the empirical coverage.

## 4. Policies (B3), all in one simulator with the same payloads, ACK, retry limits and duty-cycle rule
- **P0** fixed 3-retry baseline (4 tries, one queue, no severity).
- **P1** severity table: point class selects the maximum tries (2, 4, 8, 12) and the service order; cancel window by class (10, 8, 6, 5 s). **The strong baseline.**
- **P2** SCT with the point class: fast path for Severe and Critical, two-stage flash (14 bytes), authenticated CANCEL, reserved duty share (docs/01_protocol/PROTOCOL_SCT.md).
- **P3** UA-SCT: as P2 but the class is the upper end U of the one-sided class-conditional conformal interval (alpha = 0.10).
- **P4** UA-SCT with a cap: P3 with the fast-path airtime limited to a fraction phi of the duty budget (phi tuned on the tuning scenarios from 0.1, 0.25, 0.5; the rule: the smallest phi that keeps critical reach within 10 s within 2 points of P3 on the tuning scenarios).
SCT parameters (hold of Severe alerts h_s in 0, 1, 2, 3 s; reserved share 0.2, 0.35, 0.5; spreading-factor raise on or off) are tuned for P2 on the tuning scenarios only (objective: critical-and-severe reach within 10 s minus a penalty of 2 per point by which normal-path delivery falls more than 5 points below the severity table) and the same parameters are used for P3 and P4. Held-out scenarios are scored once.

## 5. Simulator (B4)
LoRa 433 MHz, one 125 kHz channel, coding rate 4/5, SF7 and SF9 (SF12 only in the sensitivity), 97-byte alert, 14-byte flash and CANCEL, 26-byte ACK, 16-byte heartbeat, airtime from the Semtech formula. **Duty-cycle limit is a parameter, default 1%, applied as: the airtime of a transmitter in any trailing 3,600 s window never exceeds duty x 3,600 s (node and base station each); a transmission that would exceed it waits.** The 1% default and every other regulatory value (including the 10% sensitivity) are NOT verified for India (WPC/DoT not checked). 100 cars by default, background heartbeats every 600 s, Poisson crashes (0.1 per car per hour, a stress value), the burst scenario (a burst of simultaneous crashes within 10 s). Crash classes come from the NHTSA pairs of section 3 (assigned class by policy, true class from the reference), not from a stress mix. Decision times come from the N19 early-exit distribution (seed 42, 5% target). Capture rule 6 dB; independent loss 10% each way; fading as in X6. Every value is an ASSUMPTION of the simulator. Tuning scenarios (seeds 1000 to 1002, intermediate parameter values) and held-out scenarios (**40 seeds per cell**, seeds 3000 to 3039) are disjoint. Sensor range is a scenario factor (+-16, +-32, +-64 g).
Held-out cells: sensor range {16, 32, 64} x {steady, burst}; steady: cars {50, 100, 200}, SF {7, 9}; burst size {10, 30, 60} critical alerts, cars 100; heartbeat period {60, 600 s}; channel loss {10%, 30%}; duty {1% default, 10% sensitivity}. One factor changes at a time from the default (100 cars, SF7, 600 s, 10% loss, 1% duty, burst 30, range 16 g).

## 6. Metrics (B5), in this order; the leading metric is primary
1. **Mis-triage rate**: share of true Severe or Critical crash alerts that are delivered through the normal path (assigned class below Severe). Primary inference: grouped (make and model) bootstrap over the NHTSA pairs (1,000 resamples, MEASURED_SOFTWARE); the simulated value per cell is secondary.
2. **Critical delivery ratio within a deadline**: share of true Critical crash alerts with a signed ACK within 10 s and within 30 s of the crash instant.
3. Critical 95th-percentile latency (delivered alerts only; read with item 2).
4. Airtime per event, share of the duty budget used; **escalation fraction** = share of true Low or Moderate crash alerts assigned to the fast path (the cost of using the upper bound).
5. Share of runs in which the duty limit is exceeded (must be 0; checked by an independent scan of the logged transmissions, not by the enforcement rule).
All differences are paired by scenario seed with 95% bootstrap intervals over seeds (and, for pool-level quantities, over groups). An improvement counts only if the whole interval is on the favourable side of zero.

## 7. Hypotheses and what counts against them (B6)
- **H-a.** At +-16 g, P3 has a lower mis-triage rate than P1 (the grouped-bootstrap interval of P3 minus P1 is below zero). *Counts against H-a:* the interval contains zero or is above zero; or the interval is below zero but the reduction is under 2 points (reported as not meaningful).
- **H-b.** The cost of P3 is more escalations of non-severe events to the fast path, and P4 bounds the fast-path airtime. *Counts against H-b:* the escalation fraction of P3 minus P1 has an interval containing zero (no cost to bound), or P4's fast-path airtime share exceeds phi in any cell, or P4 lowers critical reach within 10 s below P3's by more than 5 points in the held-out cells.
- **H-c.** The gain shrinks at +-64 g because the intervals are narrower. *Counts against H-c:* the mean interval width at +-64 g is not below that at +-16 g (interval of the difference not below zero), or the mis-triage reduction of P3 relative to P1 at +-64 g is not smaller than at +-16 g (interval of the difference in reductions not below zero).
- **Secondary expectation (B7), also preregistered:** if the gain exists it appears in mis-triage and in bursts rather than in steady-state critical delivery. Reported either way. P2 against P1 and P3 against P1 are reported side by side; a null result is reported in the same detail as a success.

## 8. Analysis to be run, in order (and nothing else counts as the preregistered analysis)
1. `crashguard.ua_conformal`: class conformal intervals (section 3) per range, alpha 0.10 and 0.05, the NHTSA pair pools per policy.
2. `crashguard.s11_sct`: tuning of the SCT parameters and phi on the tuning scenarios, then the held-out cells for P0 to P4 (section 5), the metrics of section 6, the three hypotheses.
3. The ablations of SCT (no preemption, no flash, no severity-dependent hold, no reserved share, no spreading-factor raise) are EXPLORATORY.
4. Part A items (N20 with calibration de-duplicated by recording group; attack-suite count; W3; X6 burst sweep with capture none and 6 dB, burst 10, 30 and 60, heartbeat payload 16 and 26 bytes, cars 50, 100, 200, SF 7 and 9) are not part of the hypotheses; they are reported with the interval rules above.
5. Part C (bounded model check) is ANALYTICAL and reports a check of the model within its bounds, not a proof of the implementation.

---

# Addendum 1 (dated 2026-11-11, committed BEFORE the T1 to T4, T6 and T8 analyses are run)

This addendum fixes the analyses of the closing block. It changes no primary metric, no primary test set and no earlier result. Anything not written here (T5, T7, T9 to T13) is EXPLORATORY and labelled so in `results.json` and the report. What was already seen before this addendum: all UA-SCT results of the main study (mis-triage of P3 against P1 at +-16 g about 3 to 5 points lower, escalation about 50 points higher, little change in critical delivery over SCT, SCT gains in bursts, a spaced-retry table recovers most of the 30 s gain), the S9 class rule result, and the single-car result (SCT worse than the table at 60% loss).

## A1.1 T1: equal-escalation comparison
- **Escalation** = the share of true Low or Moderate crash alerts whose assigned class is Severe or Critical (fast path), as in section 6 of this document.
- **Lowered-threshold baselines.** P1e (severity table) and P2e (plain SCT) use the point estimates of P1 and P2 with every class boundary divided by a factor f in (0, 1]: class_f = classify(delta-V_hat / f, ASI_hat / f) (delta-V limits 24, 40, 56 km/h and ASI levels 1.0, 1.4, 1.9 all scaled). f is found per sensor range by bisection on the pooled held-out (test, repeat) rows of the class-interval run (class-conditional, alpha 0.10) so that P1e and P2e escalate the same share of true non-severe rows as P3, within 0.5 points; no simulated outcome is used to choose f.
- **Comparison.** P3 minus P2e and P3 minus P1e on the held-out cells and seeds of section 5 (40 paired seeds, all three ranges, steady and burst cells): mis-triage (pool level, grouped bootstrap, 1,000 resamples; simulated values as secondary), critical reach within 10 s and 30 s, critical 95th-percentile latency (delivered alerts only; read with delivery), airtime share. 95% bootstrap intervals over seeds (inside every cell), averaged over cells as in section 6.
- **Decision rule.** "P3 still wins at equal escalation" only if (i) the pool-level interval of mis-triage (P3 minus P2e) is entirely below zero at +-16 g AND (ii) in both steady and burst cells the lower end of the interval of critical reach within 10 s (P3 minus P2e) is above -0.02. Otherwise it is reported that P3 does not win at equal escalation, and which part failed. The same rule is applied against P1e and at +-32 g and +-64 g (reported, not decisive).

## A1.2 T2: trade-off curves
- **Families.** Point-class family for P0, P1 and P2 (their class assignment is the same): f on the grid 0.40, 0.45, ..., 1.60 (25 values, f = 1 is the current rule). Interval family for P3 and P4 (P4 differs from P3 only by an airtime cap, so its curve in mis-triage and escalation coincides with P3's): class-conditional one-sided upper end with alpha on the grid 0.50, 0.40, 0.30, 0.20, 0.15, 0.10, 0.07, 0.05, 0.03, 0.02, 0.01. All grid points are plotted and none is selected afterwards.
- **Axes and area.** x = escalation (share of true non-severe events sent to the fast path), y = mis-triage (share of true Severe or Critical events assigned a class below Severe). Both families are closed with the endpoints (0, 1) and (1, 0); area under the curve by the trapezoid rule over [0, 1], lower is better; 95% grouped (make and model) bootstrap interval for each area and for the area difference (interval family minus point family), 1,000 resamples, at +-16, +-32 and +-64 g.

## A1.3 T3: expected-cost view
- **Cost per crash event** = C_u x [true Severe or Critical and assigned below Severe] + C_o x [true Low or Moderate and assigned Severe or Critical]. Expected cost per crash event = s x mis-triage x C_u + (1 - s) x escalation x C_o with s = the share of true Severe or Critical events among the pooled rows (observed; NHTSA, not representative); s is also varied (0.1, 0.3, observed, 0.9).
- **Break-even ratio** r* = C_u / C_o at which P3 and P1 cost the same: r* = (1 - s) (escalation_P3 - escalation_P1) / (s (mis-triage_P1 - mis-triage_P3)), with a 95% grouped bootstrap interval; P3 is cheaper when C_u / C_o is above r*. P2 has the class assignment of P1, so its class-cost break-even is the same; a delivery term (EXPLORATORY): C_l x [true Critical alert without a signed ACK within 10 s] with C_l = C_u from the simulation separates P2 from P1. Shown for ratios 0.1, 0.3, 1, 3, 10, 30, 100, 300, 1000, not one ratio.

## A1.4 T4: two clarifications (no new hypothesis)
- (a) Define under-estimation (S9: share of tests whose reported class is below the reference class, under the lower-bound rule of S9) and mis-triage (H-a: share of true Severe or Critical events assigned below Severe, under the upper-end rule of the class intervals), give both per true class and per range, from the stored arrays; the two rules differ (lower bound against upper end), so both statements hold.
- (b) Single car, 60% loss both ways, SF7, 20 alerts per hour, 40 seeds: report the setup, compute the expected share of alerts acknowledged within 10 s analytically from the number of retry rounds that fit, and confirm the cause by running SCT with quick retries (spacing 0.3 s) as an EXPLORATORY variant.

## A1.5 T6: severity-threshold sensitivity
All three delta-V limits (24, 40, 56 km/h) shifted together by -2, -1, +1 and +2 km/h, and each limit shifted alone by the same amounts (16 settings, ASI levels unchanged); reference and assigned classes both use the shifted limits; the estimator, calibration (alpha 0.10 class-conditional), mis-triage comparison (P3 minus P1 at +-16 g) and the H-c quantities are recomputed. H-a "survives" a setting if the pool-level interval of the mis-triage difference is below zero and its point value is below -0.02; H-c "survives" if the interval of (reduction at +-16 g minus reduction at +-64 g) is above zero and the interval of the width difference is below zero. A delta-V severity scale from a source that was opened is used as an additional setting only if such a source is found; otherwise NOT_RUN.

## A1.6 T8: simulator assumption sweep
UA-SCT policies P0 to P4 at +-16 g, one factor at a time from the default (100 cars, SF7, heartbeat 600 s with 16 bytes, 10% loss, capture 6 dB, burst 30, duty 1%): capture none; burst 10 and 60; heartbeat payload 26 bytes; cars 50 and 200; SF9; loss 0%, 20%, 40%, 60%; duty 0.1%. Steady and burst cells where the factor applies; 40 paired seeds. "P3 beats P1 (or P2)" in a cell for a metric only if the whole paired 95% interval lies on the favourable side (critical reach within 10 s and within 30 s: above zero; mis-triage: below zero). Cells are counted; a result that holds in one corner only is reported as such.

---

# FILE: PREREGISTRATION_V2.md

# Preregistration v2: five algorithm studies (written and committed before any v2 result exists)

Date: 2026-11-12. Branch `v2-algorithms`, started from the tag `v1.0-round3`. This file is the initial commit of the branch. Software only; no hardware log is read. Every result gets a new key prefix `v2.`; no existing key or tagged result is changed (`check_results` runs before every commit, and the writer refuses to purge a prefix that does not start with `v2.`).

Anything not written here is EXPLORATORY and is labelled so. Primary metrics, primary splits, thresholds and decision rules below are not changed after the earliest v2 number is produced. If a mistake in this text is found later, it is corrected in a dated addendum at the end, never by editing the sentences above it.

Labels: MEASURED_SOFTWARE (computed from NHTSA crash tests or VZCrash windows), SIMULATED (event simulator, every channel and traffic value is an assumption; the 1% duty limit is not verified for India), ANALYTICAL (formula), NOT_RUN (with reason). NHTSA tests are mostly high-speed barrier tests and are not representative of all real crashes. The three words banned by the project rules are not used.

## 0. Common rules

- **Seeds.** 42 for everything single-seeded; folds use seed 42 plus the repeat index, as in `saturation_splits` (10 repeats of grouped 5-fold). Simulator evaluation: 40 paired scenario seeds per cell (`EVAL_SEED` 3000, hours 0 to 39); tuning, where a study has any, uses seed 1000 and 3 hours on the eight tuning scenarios of `s11_sct` (`TUNE_COMBOS`) and never touches an evaluation cell.
- **Intervals.** 95% percentile bootstrap, 1,000 resamples. Where the unit is a crash test, whole make/model groups are resampled together (the `boot.ci_grouped` rule of the earlier studies), which is more conservative than resampling tests and is the rule used throughout. Where the unit is a simulation seed, seeds are resampled inside each cell (the `s11_sct.agg_diff` rule).
- **p-values.** One-sided bootstrap p-values (share of resamples on the wrong side of the null, floor 0.001 with 1,000 resamples; a printed 0 means below 0.001).
- **Multiplicity.** One Holm correction over the nine hypotheses that have a statistical test: H-A1, H-A2, H-A3, H-B1, H-B2, H-D1, H-D2, H-E1, H-F2 (their p-values are defined in each study). H-B3 (a ratio of gains), H-E2 and H-F1 (formulas) have no p-value and are decided by their stated rule; they are not in the Holm family. A hypothesis is SUPPORTED only if its stated criterion is met AND its Holm-adjusted p is below 0.05. It is NOT SUPPORTED if a stated "counts against" condition holds or the criterion fails clearly. It is MIXED if one part of a two-part criterion holds and the other does not.
  Inside Study A, the baseline comparisons of A4 (4 baselines x 4 splits x 3 ranges) get their own Holm correction and are labelled EXPLORATORY except the one named in H-A1.
- **Reporting.** Every comparison is reported with its interval whether or not it is favourable. A negative result is an acceptable outcome of every study.
- **Prior work.** Jao and Shkel 2022 (triangular-function reconstruction with Gaussian-process regression), Zhang et al. 2026 (peak-aware saturation compensation) and Chan et al. 2023 (spline through clipped segments) are named in the task. Chan et al. 2023 was opened in an earlier round; the other two could not be opened (`docs/06_references/PRIOR_WORK.md`) and are not cited for any result. Their ideas appear only as "our implementation of the idea" baselines (a triangular pulse fit, a Gaussian-process fit over the unclipped samples, and our existing spline). No result of those papers is quoted.

## 1. Study A: tail-aware clipping estimator (MEASURED_SOFTWARE, 176 NHTSA tests)

**Data and splits.** The 176 tests, the sensor model (`severity_study.sensor_signal`, 100 Hz, ranges +-16, +-32, +-64 g) and the cached cells of `saturation_splits`. The primary split is **R**: repeated grouped 5-fold cross-validation on make/model groups, 10 repeats (132 groups), exactly as in N14. Also reported: **G** (vehicle-group hold-out, 58 tests), **A** (make/model hold-out, 63 tests) and **O** (original test-number rule, 58 tests), each with its tuning tests as training data. Everything fitted (including descriptor-based models, quantile models and calibration) uses the training tests of the fold only. The primary range is +-16 g; +-32 g and +-64 g are secondary.

**Reference numbers from the tagged results (split R, +-16 g, absolute delta-V error %):** plain median 25.7, p90 51.0, within 10% 0.068; spline 23.5, 51.8, 0.157; fill 16.5, 52.8, 0.290; fill + ridge ("M+", the earlier primary) 11.9, 58.5, 0.435. (The task text says spline 51.0 at p90; the stored value is 51.0 for plain and 51.8 for spline; the stored values are used.)

**A1 clipping descriptors.** Per event and range, on the pulse samples from the trigger to the pulse end of the severity code, with a sample counted as clipped when |a| reaches 0.7 of the range (the level the earlier study chose; `FINAL_SAT`): clipped duration (ms), number of clipped runs, clipped fraction of the pulse, slope into the earliest clipped run (g/s, last 3 samples before it), slope out of the last clipped run (g/s, the 3 samples after it), pre-clip rise time (ms from the trigger to the earliest clipped sample), area of the unclipped samples (g s), and the area between the clip level and zero over the clipped samples (g s). Report the Spearman correlation of each with the signed log error log(plain / reference) of the plain estimate, on the tuning tests of split G only (training data), with a grouped bootstrap interval. Descriptive.

**A2 deterministic bounds (ANALYTICAL plus empirical).**
- Lower bound: the delta-V of the clipped signal through the same severity code (the plain estimate). Statement tested: if the sensor can only reduce the magnitude of the true acceleration while keeping its sign on each axis, and the processing is the same linear integration, the integral of the clipped signal is not larger than the integral of the unclipped one, so the plain estimate cannot exceed the true delta-V. Conditions: the sign of each axis is unchanged by clipping; the baseline offset and the pulse end are those of the clipped signal; sensor low-pass filtering before and after clipping is linear and the filter is applied identically (here it is applied after clipping, so a small violation by filter ringing is possible). The empirical check counts, per range, tests where plain > reference x 1.00 and where plain > reference x 1.05, and reports where the assumptions fail.
- Upper bound: from the four pulse families of A3 (half-sine, haversine, triangle, trapezoid) fitted to the unclipped pulse samples by the grid search of `saturation.fit_pulse`. For each family, all grid points (start offset, duration) whose cost is at most **2.0 times the minimum cost** of that family (and at least the minimum cost plus a floor of 1e-6) are admitted; the amplitude of each admitted point is the best-fit amplitude, limited by the existing cap of 12 times the range. The upper bound is the largest filled-signal delta-V over all admitted points and families. The factor 2.0 is fixed here and is not tuned.
- Reported per range: share of tests with reference delta-V inside [lower, upper] (all 176 tests; no fitting involved, so no training split is needed), mean width in km/h and in percent of the reference, and the share where the lower bound already exceeds the reference by more than 5%.

**A3 pulse-shape model averaging.** Families: half-sine, haversine, triangle (as in the repo) and a symmetric trapezoid (rise over the opening quarter, plateau, fall over the last quarter). For each family the filled delta-V and the fit cost are computed. Features: the mean and the median of the four delta-V values, the delta-V of the minimum-cost family, a cost-weighted mean (weights exp(-cost / (median cost + 1e-6))), and the disagreement (standard deviation of log delta-V over families). Baselines "our implementation of the idea": (i) the triangle family alone (the triangular-reconstruction idea), (ii) a Gaussian-process regression over the unclipped samples of the pulse (RBF kernel plus noise, hyper-parameters by marginal likelihood, clipped samples replaced by the posterior mean with the posterior mean floored at the clip level), integrated by the same severity code.

**A4 tail-aware estimator.** Features: A1 descriptors, A3 features, the plain delta-V, pulse width and clipped fraction (the three features of the earlier ridge). Models fitted on the training tests of each fold: (a) the earlier ridge on its three features (reproduces M+), (b) a ridge on the full feature set predicting log(reference / fill estimate), (c) gradient boosting (scikit-learn `GradientBoostingRegressor`, 100 trees, depth 2, learning rate 0.05, subsample 0.8, seed 42) predicting the same target, (d) quantile gradient boosting for the 10th, 50th and 90th percentiles of the same target (same settings, `loss="quantile"`). The point estimate of the "tail-aware estimator" is the median-quantile model (d, 50th). Hyper-parameters are fixed here; no tuning. Events that never clip are returned as the plain estimate (as M+ does). Compared with plain, spline, fill, fill + ridge (M+), the triangular-fit baseline and the Gaussian-process baseline, on all four splits and three ranges. Metrics: median, 90th and 95th percentile absolute error, share within +-10%, paired differences with grouped bootstrap.

**A5 adaptive conformal intervals.** Target level 90% (alpha 0.10); the 5-fold structure of R with a vehicle-grouped calibration share of 0.4 of the training groups (as in the UA-SCT study). Intervals are on the multiplicative error log(reference / estimate): (i) plain split conformal on the absolute residual of the median model; (ii) conformalized quantile regression from the 10th and 90th percentile models (lower and upper end of the interval, widened by the usual CQR correction); (iii) a normalised-score variant: the absolute log residual divided by a predicted difficulty, where difficulty is a gradient-boosting model (same settings) of the absolute log residual fitted on the non-calibration training tests, floored at 0.02. Report coverage on held-out vehicles per range, and mean width (km/h and relative) and coverage per **clipped-fraction tercile** (terciles of the clipped fraction among clipped events, cut points from training tests; events that never clip form their own bin, "none").

**A6 abstention flag.** Flag an event when its clipped fraction exceeds the 75th percentile of the training clipped fractions among clipped events, or its predicted interval width (variant iii) exceeds the 75th percentile of training widths. Report the number and share flagged, the median and 90th-percentile error of unflagged and flagged events, and the share of Severe or Critical reference events among the flagged.

**Hypotheses for A (primary range +-16 g, primary split R, primary estimator = A4 (d) median model, comparison = M+).**
- **H-A1.** The tail-aware estimator lowers the 90th-percentile delta-V error relative to M+ at +-16 g on split R by at least 5 points (point estimate of p90(M+) minus p90(tail-aware) at least 5), with the 95% grouped interval of the difference excluding zero, and the point estimate of the p90 difference is a reduction of at least 5 points on at least two of the four splits (R, G, A, O). p-value: share of grouped resamples with a reduction at or below 0. Counts against: the interval includes zero, or the gain of at least 5 points appears on only one split.
- **H-A2.** For clipped events in the lowest clipped-fraction tercile, the normalised-score adaptive interval (A5 iii) is narrower than the plain split-conformal interval (A5 i) (mean relative width difference below zero; p-value: share of grouped resamples with a difference at or above 0), and its coverage on held-out vehicles at +-16 g is within 2 points of 0.90 (that is, between 0.88 and 0.92) for the whole test set. Counts against: no narrowing, or coverage outside the band.
- **H-A3.** The bounds of A2 contain the reference delta-V for at least 90% of the 176 tests at +-16 g. p-value: share of grouped resamples with containment below 0.90. Counts against: containment below 0.90 (point estimate), reported with the cases where the assumptions fail.
- Secondary (EXPLORATORY): everything at +-32 and +-64 g, all other comparisons in A4, the quantile coverage, A6.

## 2. Study D: clip-onset early severity flag (MEASURED_SOFTWARE, 176 NHTSA tests)

**Data.** Same tests, sensor model and splits as Study A (primary split R, repeated grouped 5-fold, 10 repeats; also G, A, O), at +-16, +-32, +-64 g and 100 and 200 Hz. Primary: +-16 g, 100 Hz. Reference severity class: maximum of the delta-V class and the ASI class from the reference delta-V and the reference ASI of the unclipped record (limits 24/40/56 km/h and 1.0/1.4/1.9 g, as in `ua_core`). Severe or Critical = class 2 or 3. Time zero = the trigger sample. "Full-pulse decision time" = the pulse length of the severity code (`pulse_ms` of the plain estimate; the time at which the full-pulse class can be known).

**D1 onset features.** Time from the trigger to the earliest sample with |a| at or above q x range on either horizontal axis; the rise slope over the 3 samples before that sample; whether any sample reaches that level in the pulse (clipping occurs at all). q in {0.7, 0.8, 0.9, 0.98}.

**D2 flag rule.** Flag Severe or Critical when the onset time is at most T_on ms (flag time = onset time). (q, T_on) are chosen inside each training fold by grouped cross-validation on the training tests only (inner grouped 4-fold on make/model): over q in the set above and T_on in 10-ms steps from 10 ms to 300 ms, choose the pair that maximises the detection rate of reference Severe or Critical tests subject to an over-triage (share of reference Low or Moderate tests flagged) of at most 0.05 on the inner validation folds; ties go to the smaller T_on, then the larger q.

**D3 evaluation and comparators** (each is a stand-alone Severe/Critical flagger with a flag time or no flag):
 (i) the full-pulse class of the earlier estimator (fill + ridge delta-V and the ASI of the filled window, class 2 or 3), flag time = full-pulse decision time;
 (ii) **the severity analogue of the earlier early-exit rule.** The N19 early-exit rule classifies crash against non-crash on VZCrash windows with forests; it cannot be applied to NHTSA severity without new training, so the analogue is a sequential rule on the running plain delta-V of the clipped window: flag Severe or Critical at the earliest sample where the running delta-V (same integration as the severity code) reaches the Severe limit of 40 km/h. This is the early-exit idea applied to severity; it is labelled "analogue", not "the earlier rule";
 (iii) an early-peak threshold without the clipping logic: flag when the resultant peak acceleration within the opening T_pk ms after the trigger reaches G_pk g; (T_pk, G_pk) chosen by the same inner cross-validation and criterion as D2, T_pk in {50, 100, 150, 200, 300} ms, G_pk in {2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12} g (reported only where G_pk is below the range).
 For each: detection rate of reference Severe or Critical tests (1 minus under-triage), over-triage, flag time, time saved (full-pulse decision time minus flag time, flagged true positives only), and the share of events that never clip (and therefore get no early flag from D2). Events not flagged early fall back to (i); the under-triage and over-triage reported for D2 are those of the early flag alone.

**Hypotheses for D (primary: +-16 g, 100 Hz, split R, q and T_on chosen as above).**
- **H-D1.** Among reference Severe or Critical tests flagged by the D2 rule, the median time saved against the full-pulse decision is at least 100 ms (lower end of the grouped interval at or above 100 ms; p-value: share of grouped resamples with a median below 100 ms), AND the under-triage rate of the D2 flag is not worse than that of the analogue (ii): the 95% grouped interval of (under-triage of D2 minus under-triage of (ii)) does not lie entirely above zero. Counts against: the time saved is below 100 ms, or that interval lies entirely above zero.
- **H-D2.** The over-triage of the D2 flag (reference Low or Moderate tests flagged) is below 10% on split R. p-value: share of grouped resamples with over-triage at or above 0.10.
- Reported plainly: events that never clip at +-16 g cannot be flagged this way; their share is reported for every range and rate.

## 3. Study B: load-adaptive escalation (SIMULATED)

**Question.** The equal-escalation test (T1) showed that the interval class rule does no better than lowering the table's threshold. This study tests whether *where* the escalations go matters, not how many.

**Simulator.** `sct_sim` with the same payloads (97 B alert, 14 B flash and CANCEL, 26 B ACK), signed ACK, retries, spacing, preemption and duty cap (1% trailing hour, not verified for India), with these additions, written so that the existing configurations give bit-identical results (a regression test against stored `s11` cells is part of the deliverable):
 - the class of an alert is decided at the decision time (arrival + decision delay + compute time) from the pool entry's delta-V and ASI estimates and a threshold scale s, as class(dv / s, ASI / s) with the limits of `ua_core` (s = 1 is the table's default thresholds; s below 1 lowers them);
 - a per-alert flag `extra` = the point class at s = 1 is below Severe while the class at the car's current s is Severe or Critical (an escalation of an event the default table would not escalate);
 - a metric `escalation_used`: the share of true Low or Moderate crash alerts that used the fast path at least once.
 Class pairs come from the NHTSA cross-validated delta-V and ASI estimates at +-16 g (the folds of `ua_core`), with the true class from the reference. Only +-16 g is simulated.

**Starting point.** Every adaptive variant starts at s0 = f, the factor found in T1 for +-16 g (0.6375), which gives the escalation of P3 (0.683 of non-severe events); it can only move s up toward 1 (fewer escalations). The matched fixed baseline of each variant is the same rule with a constant s_b in [s0, 1].

**B1 matched baselines.** For each cell and variant: run the variant, take its mean realised `escalation_used` over the 40 seeds, find s_b by bisection on the pool-level escalation (pool share of true Low or Moderate entries with class at s_b at or above Severe; tolerance 0.005, 40 iterations), run the fixed rule at s_b over the same seeds, and report the match error (realised escalation of baseline minus variant, mean and interval). Also run in every cell: P1 and P2 at default thresholds (s = 1), the fixed lowered-threshold rule at s0 (equal to the P3 escalation) and the severity table with spaced retries (P1b of S13).

**B2 adaptive variants** (parameters fixed here unless listed as tuned; tuned values are chosen on the tuning scenarios only, from the stated grid, by the largest mean critical reach within 10 s over the burst tuning scenarios at the escalation-matched baseline, ties to the simpler setting):
 1. **Controller.** Per car, an exponentially weighted failure rate F of the car's own alert rounds (failure = the listen window ended without an ACK; weight 0.3 per round; F decays toward 0 with a 3,600 s time constant). The scale s moves by a bounded step g (tuned over {0.02, 0.05, 0.10}) up when F exceeds 0.5 and down when F falls below 0.2 (hysteresis: no change in between), within [s0, 1.0], evaluated at each decision.
 2. **Token bucket.** Per car, a bucket of capacity b = 1 token (b in {1, 2} tuned) refilled at r tokens per hour (r in {0.5, 1, 2, 4} tuned). An *extra* escalation consumes a token and is admitted only if a token is available; otherwise the event keeps its default-table class (s = 1). Critical assignments (class 3) never need a token. Classes are assigned at s0.
 3. **Beacon-assisted.** The base broadcasts a 9-byte beacon (1 level byte plus an 8-byte tag) whenever its load level changes and at least every 120 s. Load level = 0 if fewer than 1, 1 if 1 to 4, 2 if 5 to 14, 3 if 15 or more alert or flash messages were received in the last 60 s. A car that has heard a beacon within 300 s uses s = s0 + (1 - s0) x level / 3, otherwise s0. Beacons are transmitted by the base under its own duty window (they count against the base's duty budget and block its receiver while on air); each car hears a beacon with probability 1 minus the downlink loss.
 4. **Severity-scaled backoff.** No change to s (= s0, so the class equals the fixed rule at s0); every transmission of an alert is delayed by a uniform random time from 0 to W_c with W_c = 2.0, 1.0, 0.5, 0.25 s for classes 0 to 3.
 5. **Graceful demotion.** s = s0. An alert with the `extra` flag whose class is Severe (not Critical) is demoted to the normal path (the existing `demoted` mechanism) after k failed rounds, k in {1, 2, 3} tuned. Class 3 is never demoted.

**B3 scenarios** (40 seeds per cell; one factor at a time from the default of 100 cars, SF7, 30-car burst, capture 6 dB, loss 0.1, duty 1%):
 - *Burst cells, unloaded (7):* base, burst of 10, burst of 60, 50 cars, 200 cars, SF9, capture none.
 - *Burst cells, loaded (3):* the base, 200-car and SF9 cells with background crash alerts at 1.0 per car per hour whose pool is resampled so that 80% of the events are true Low or Moderate (heavy non-severe escalation competing with the burst of critical alerts).
 - *Steady cells (6):* base, 50 cars, 200 cars, SF9, capture none, loss 0.3.
 - *Single-car cell (1):* one car, 20 alerts per hour, loss 0.6 both ways, no other traffic.
 - *Mixed cell (1):* steady at 200 cars with the loaded background (no burst).
 Known limitation, written before the runs: in the unloaded burst cells almost no non-severe event is escalated during the burst (the background alert rate is 0.1 per car per hour and each car has at most one alert), and the per-car controller and token bucket have no history to act on, so no gain is expected there; the loaded cells and the mixed cell are where competing escalations exist. The unloaded cells are kept as part of the majority rule below because they are the cells of the earlier sweep.

**B4 metrics.** Primary: critical reach within 10 s (share of true Critical alerts acknowledged within 10 s) in the burst cells, at matched escalation. Secondary: critical reach within 30 s, critical 95th-percentile latency, mis-triage, delivery of true non-severe alerts (they must not be starved: reported as `delivered_normal`), airtime and duty use, runs above the duty cap (must be zero), the extra base airtime of the beacon variant.

**B5 sensitivity.** One-at-a-time sweeps of the controller step and thresholds, bucket rate, demotion k and beacon period (60, 120, 300 s) on the burst cells; the number of cells in which each variant beats its matched baseline (whole interval above zero). EXPLORATORY.

**Hypotheses for B** (difference = variant minus its matched fixed baseline, paired by seed; "gain" in points of critical reach within 10 s).
- **H-B1.** In the burst cells (10), at least one variant has a gain of at least 3 points with the 95% interval above zero in a majority of them (at least 6 of 10). p-value for the Holm family: for the variant with the most such cells, the sixth smallest of its ten one-sided seed-bootstrap p-values (the largest of the six needed), multiplied by 5 (Bonferroni over variants), capped at 1. Counts against: no variant does, or the gain appears only in the cell with the weakest baseline (the cell where the matched baseline has the lowest critical reach).
- **H-B2.** No variant lowers critical reach within 10 s by more than 2 points, and none raises mis-triage by more than 2 points, in the steady cells and the single-car cell, relative to the matched baseline (non-inferiority: the upper end of the one-sided 95% bound of the harm is below 2 points). p-value: the largest of the one-sided non-inferiority p-values over variants, cells and the two metrics (an intersection test; no further correction inside it). Counts against: any variant with a harm above 2 points whose interval excludes 2.
- **H-B3.** Decided only if H-B1 holds. The mean gain over the burst cells of the token bucket is at least 80% of the mean gain of the best variant. No p-value; point rule. If H-B1 does not hold, H-B3 is reported "not evaluable" and counted as not supported.
- The Holm family contains H-B1 and H-B2 only; the 50 variant-by-cell tests are shown in full.

## 4. Study E: authentication-tag length against capacity (ANALYTICAL plus SIMULATED)

**E1 forgery model (ANALYTICAL).** Tag lengths t in {4, 6, 8, 12, 16} bytes, a truncated HMAC-SHA256 with a secret key (the attacker's best attack is guessing; no key recovery or truncation weakness). Probability of one forgery within n independent attempts: 1 - (1 - 2^(-8t))^n. Attempts needed for success probability 1e-6: ln(1 - 1e-6) / ln(1 - 2^(-8t)). Attempt rate: a single transmitter limited by the duty cycle: floor(0.01 x 3,600 s / airtime) alert frames per hour, airtime from the Semtech formula (125 kHz, coding rate 4/5, explicit header, CRC on) for the alert of (89 + t) bytes at SF7, SF8, SF9 (the alert body is 89 bytes). Expected time to one forgery at that rate; the uncapped rate (back-to-back frames) is shown as a sensitivity; the duty limit does not bind an attacker, which is stated. Work factor = expected attempts 2^(8t - 1) (as log2). No claim of cryptographic proof; truncating a tag is a known trade-off and the contribution is the curve for this protocol.

**E2 capacity effect (SIMULATED).** The simulator gets message lengths: alert 89 + t, flash 14 - (8 - t) = 6 + t (flash = 6-byte header plus tag), CANCEL the same as flash, ACK unchanged (EXPLORATORY variant: ACK 18 + t). Policy P2 (SCT) and P1 at the tuned parameters of S11, all seven unloaded burst cells and the three loaded burst cells of Study B, 40 seeds, point classes at s = 1. Metrics: critical reach within 10 s and 30 s, share of the duty budget used, collided share.

**E3.** One table and one figure (tag bytes against log2 forgery work factor and critical reach within 10 s). The smallest tag whose duty-capped forgery work factor at SF9 stays above 2^32 attempts is stated, with the assumptions.

**Hypotheses for E.**
- **H-E1.** Going from 8 to 4 bytes raises critical reach within 10 s in the burst cells (the 7 unloaded cells, mean of paired seed differences, P2) by at least 2 points with a 95% interval above zero. p-value: one-sided seed bootstrap of the mean over cells (seeds resampled inside each cell). Counts against: no gain; this would justify keeping 8 bytes or more.
- **H-E2.** An 8-byte tag keeps the forgery work factor above 2^32 attempts under the duty-capped attacker at SF7 to SF9: that is, attempts for success probability 1e-6 and the expected attempts are both above 2^32. Formula; no p-value. Counts against: either is not.

## 5. Study F: severity-dependent hold (ANALYTICAL plus SIMULATED)

**F1 cost model (ANALYTICAL).** For an alert of class c with hold h (0 to 5 s, grid of 0.5 s): expected cost = p x D_c x h + (1 - p) x C_false x R(h), where p = share of alerts that are real, D_c = C_delay x w_c is the delay cost per second for a real event of class c (w = 0.01, 0.1, 0.3, 1.0 for classes 0 to 3; C_delay is that of Critical), C_false the cost of a false alert reaching the base, and R(h) = 0.1 + 0.9 x clip((4 - h) / 3, 0, 1) the chance a false alert is still sent after hold h (the driver cancels a false alert with probability 0.9, uniformly 1 to 4 s after the decision, the cancel model of `sct_sim`; a cancel inside the hold prevents transmission). p is the precision of the deployed forest at 0.02% prevalence (`s10.rf15.pi0p02pct.a.ppv`, 0.0043) as the default, and the mix of the simulator's false-alarm cell (0.1 real per 0.25 false alerts per car-hour) as a sensitivity. Grid of the ratio r = C_delay / C_false (per second): 1, 10, 100, 1,000, 10,000. Report the optimal hold per class and ratio, and the cost curve. The ratio grid, the class weights and the cancel model are assumptions fixed here.

**F2 simulation check (SIMULATED).** The simulator gets a hold parameter for Critical (`h_c`, default 0.0, so existing runs are unchanged) and the hold of Severe `h_s` (tuned value in S11: 0.0 s). Compared in the steady cell with false alarms at the 5% target (0.25 per car-hour) and the same with the 1% target (0.05 per car-hour), and in the loaded burst cells with false alarms: (a) the current rule (Critical 0 s, Severe 0 s as tuned), (b) a short positive Severe hold of 2.0 s (Critical 0 s) (the SctParams default; 1 s and 3 s are secondary), (c) the model-optimal holds at r = 100 from F1, (d) a flat hold of 2.0 s for all classes. 40 seeds. Metrics: critical reach within 10 s and p95 latency, share of false alarms of assigned class Severe whose alert or flash reached the base, the same for all false alarms, and the expected-cost value of F1 with the simulated shares.

**Hypotheses for F.**
- **H-F1.** For Critical alerts the model-optimal hold is zero for every ratio in the grid (1 to 10,000) under both values of p. Formula; no p-value. Counts against: any ratio with a positive optimal hold. (The structure of the cost model makes the optimum 0 or about 4 s, so this is a statement about where the crossing ratio lies.)
- **H-F2.** For Severe alerts a 2.0 s hold (b) reduces the share of false Severe alerts that reach the base by at least 20% relative to no hold (a), in the 5%-target false-alarm cell, with no significant loss in critical reach within 10 s (the paired 95% interval of the change in critical reach does not lie entirely below zero). p-value: one-sided seed bootstrap of the relative reduction being at most 20%. Counts against: the interval of the loss in reach excludes zero on the negative side, or the reduction is below 20%.

## 6. Study C: combination (only if A or B shows a positive result; EXPLORATORY)
Use the A5 interval width or the A6 flag as the ranking score inside the B admission rule (highest class, then widest interval), reported against the best B variant alone. Not preregistered beyond this paragraph; no hypothesis.

## 7. Order, time-box and cut order
Order: this preregistration, A, D, B, E, F, C, documents. Cut order if time runs short: C, F3, extra E2 cells, A6, B2 variant 3 (beacon), B5, A3 families beyond three, D3 comparator (iii). Never cut: this preregistration, B1, the Holm correction, the claim audit of the new rows. Anything cut is listed as NOT_RUN with the reason.

## 8. What would count against each hypothesis (summary)
H-A1 interval includes zero or the 5-point gain on one split only. H-A2 no narrowing in the lowest tercile or coverage outside 0.88 to 0.92. H-A3 containment below 0.90. H-B1 no variant gains 3 points in 6 of 10 burst cells, or only in the weakest-baseline cell. H-B2 any variant harming by more than 2 points in a steady or single-car cell. H-B3 gain of the token bucket below 80% of the best, or H-B1 not met. H-D1 time saved below 100 ms or under-triage significantly worse than the analogue. H-D2 over-triage at or above 10%. H-E1 no gain of 2 points. H-E2 either work factor at or below 2^32. H-F1 any ratio with a positive optimal Critical hold. H-F2 reduction below 20% or a significant loss in critical reach.

# FILE: PREREGISTRATION_V3.md

# Preregistration v3: algorithm studies X1 to X3 and X5 to X8 (written and committed before any Block 4 or Block 4b result exists)

Date: 2026-11-12. Branch `journal-push` (from `v2-algorithms`). Software only; `data/hardware` is not read. Result keys of these studies start with `x.` (for example `x.x1.*`). No existing key or tagged result is changed.

Anything not written here is EXPLORATORY and is labelled so. Primary metrics, splits, thresholds and decision rules below are not changed after the earliest Block 4 number is produced. A mistake in this text is corrected in a dated addendum at the end, never by editing the sentences above it. Blocks 1 to 3 (T13a, T13c, sensor selection, VZCrash audit) are descriptive or confirmatory; their decision rules were written in `docs/07_log/DECISIONS.md` before each run.

Labels: MEASURED_SOFTWARE (computed from NHTSA crash tests or VZCrash windows), ANALYTICAL (formula or host estimate), SIMULATED, NOT_RUN (with reason). NHTSA tests are mostly high-speed barrier tests and the sensor is a software model; VZCrash events were selected on the device before release (the trigger here is a second stage) and no vehicle identifier is released.

## 0. Common rules

- **Seeds.** 42 for single-seeded work; 0 to 4 for repeated runs (forests, models with randomness); folds of grouped cross-validation use seed 42 plus the repeat index, as in `saturation_splits` (10 repeats of grouped 5-fold on make/model groups).
- **Intervals.** 95% percentile bootstrap, 1,000 resamples. Where the unit is a crash test, whole make/model groups are resampled together (`boot.ci_grouped`). Where the unit is a VZCrash test event, events are resampled; the same drawn events feed every compared method, so differences are paired.
- **p-values.** One-sided bootstrap p-value of each hypothesis' null (share of resamples on the wrong side; floor 0.001 with 1,000 resamples). The null of each hypothesis is given with it.
- **Multiplicity.** One Holm correction over the seven hypotheses H-X1, H-X2, H-X3, H-X5, H-X6, H-X7 and H-X8 together. H-X4 (labels) is a statement about the files and has no p-value. A hypothesis is SUPPORTED only if its stated criterion is met and its Holm-adjusted p-value is below 0.05; MIXED if the criterion is met and the adjusted p-value is not below 0.05; NOT SUPPORTED otherwise. If a hypothesis has two conditions (H-X1), the p-value belongs to the statistical condition and the other condition is decided by its rule.
- **Test set use.** Thresholds, rules and hyper-parameters are chosen on validation (VZCrash) or on training folds (NHTSA). The VZCrash test split is scored once per model and seed.
- **VZCrash sets.** Primary test set: the de-duplicated set of the headline pipeline (24,022 events). Sensitivity: the strict set (22,830 events; also without window-level near-duplicates), as in T5 and V4. Supplied splits only; no re-splitting.
- **Reporting.** Every result is reported with its interval, favourable or not. A negative result is an acceptable outcome of every study.
- **Origins.** All hypotheses are MEASURED_SOFTWARE except the flash estimates (ANALYTICAL, host arithmetic, not an ESP32 compile report).

## X1. Fixed-point forest for the microcontroller (VZCrash)

**Model.** The 10-tree depth-9 random forest chosen on validation (`rfsel`; `s4b.recommended.trees` = 10, `s4b.recommended.depth` = 9), trained per seed 0 to 4 on the training windows of the headline pipeline.

**Quantisation.** Per feature, an affine grid of 2^b levels over the range of that feature in the training windows; each split threshold is rounded to the nearest level; each leaf value (crash probability) is rounded to the nearest of 2^b levels on [0, 1]; the sum over trees stays in floating point; inputs are not quantised (the quantised-threshold comparison is the one the microcontroller would make). b = 16 (primary) and b = 8.

**Flash estimate (ANALYTICAL).** Nodes times bytes per node. Primary layout (the existing export): 1 byte feature index, a threshold field, two 2-byte child indices. Float32 thresholds: 9 bytes (the stored `export.bytes_est` rule). 16-bit thresholds: 7 bytes per node (reduction 22.2%); 8-bit thresholds: 6 bytes (33.3%). Variant (EXPLORATORY, a different change from quantisation): pre-order node layout with an implicit left child, one stored child index: float 7 bytes, 16-bit 5 bytes (28.6%), 8-bit 4 bytes (42.9%). **Stated before the run: under the primary layout the 16-bit version cannot cut the estimate by 30% (it cuts 22.2% by arithmetic).**

**Metrics.** AP of triggered test events; system recall at the validation-F1 threshold; system recall at the 1% and 5% false-alarm targets (thresholds re-chosen on validation with the quantised model's scores); the largest absolute difference between the quantised and the float probability over all real test events (and the number of events whose decision at the validation-F1 threshold changes). Intervals: paired bootstrap over test events (5-seed mean), 1,000 resamples. Sets: de-duplicated (primary), strict (sensitivity).

**H-X1.** The 16-bit version loses no more than 0.005 AP (the upper 95% bound of float AP minus 16-bit AP is at most 0.005) AND cuts the flash estimate by at least 30% (primary layout). Null for the p-value: AP loss at least 0.005 (p = share of resamples with loss below 0.005). **Counts against:** either condition fails. Expected: the flash condition fails by arithmetic; the AP condition is expected to hold.

## X2. Cheap-trigger cascade (VZCrash, lowest priority)

**Relation to N15.** N15 (c) already tried a learned trigger on running features inside the full system (a forest re-trained per candidate, chosen on system recall at a fixed false-alarm rate) and found no gain. X2 differs in what is compared: only the trigger stage, at an EQUAL non-crash trigger rate, against the 2 g and 1.5 g thresholds, with the rule selected by that constraint (not by system recall) and evaluated on held-out vehicle-size categories. It uses a wider family of cheap features. It is closer to N15 (c) than to a new idea, and is reported as a check of that null.

**Rule family.** Per event (the whole 16 s record, clipped to +-16 g per axis), running causal features: p = |a|; j = |a_t - a_(t-1)| x 100 (g/s); e = mean of (|a| - 1)^2 over the last 10 samples. Stage 1 fires at the earliest sample where p >= P OR (j >= J AND e >= Ee), with P in {1.2, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5, 3.0} g, J in {off, 25, 50, 100, 200} g/s and Ee in {0.05, 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0} g^2. Stage 2 is the forest (not re-run: only the trigger-stage pass share is evaluated).

**Selection.** On the validation split: among the rules whose non-crash trigger rate does not exceed that of the 2 g threshold on the same events, the one with the largest crash pass share (ties: fewer terms, then larger P). Grouped evaluation: leave-one-vehicle-size-category-out (Light, Medium, Heavy, Unknown): the rule is selected on the validation events of the other categories and scored on the test events of the held-out category; categories are pooled for the point estimate and resampled as events for the interval.

**Metrics.** Crash pass share, non-crash trigger rate, for the cascade, the 2 g and the 1.5 g threshold, on the de-duplicated test set (strict as sensitivity).

**H-X2.** At a non-crash trigger rate not above that of the 2 g threshold, the cascade raises the crash pass share by at least 2 points over the 2 g threshold on the grouped evaluation (point estimate at least 0.02 and the paired interval above zero). Null: gain at most zero. **Counts against:** the interval includes zero, or the gain is below 2 points, or the non-crash trigger rate of the cascade on the test events exceeds that of the 2 g threshold by more than 1 point.

## X3. Group-aware training against near-duplicates (VZCrash)

**Data.** Near-duplicate groups among TRAINING windows: triggered training events whose 2.8 s trigger-aligned window is identical sample for sample (the V1 audit found that every flagged test window equals its training window with a maximum difference of exactly 0, so exact window equality is the T5 outcome). Groups come from the V1 window cache; no label is used.

**Variants.** Standard (baseline: every window weight 1); down-weight (primary): weight 1 / group size; merge (EXPLORATORY): one window per group. Model: the headline 15-tree forest (`rf15`); thresholds from the validation split as in the headline pipeline (validation is not changed).

**Metrics.** System recall at the 1% false-alarm target (threshold from validation) on the strict test set (primary), also at the 5% target, AP, and the same on the de-duplicated and supplied sets (secondary). 5-seed mean, paired bootstrap over strict test events.

**H-X3.** Down-weighting raises recall at the 1% target on the strict set by at least 1 point over standard training (5-seed mean of the paired difference at least 0.01, interval above zero). Null: gain at most zero. **Counts against:** the interval includes zero or the gain is below 1 point.

## X4 (optional): do the labels support more than crash against non-crash?

Read the dataset card (`data/raw/card/README.md`), the supplied columns and the paper's description; report what direction or severity information exists. No labels are invented. No hypothesis, no p-value.

## X5. Does a clipped sensor still rank severity correctly? (NHTSA, 176 tests)

**Data.** The 176 tests, the sensor model of `severity_study.sensor_signal`, ranges +-16, +-32, +-64 g, output rates 100 and 200 Hz. The estimators that were trained at 100 Hz (fill + ridge; the tail-aware median model) are scored at 100 Hz only; plain, spline, ASI and the direction-adjusted variants at both rates. Fitted estimators use out-of-fold predictions of repeated grouped 5-fold cross-validation on make/model groups (split R, 10 repeats); correlations are computed per repeat and averaged.

**Severity measures.** (1) plain delta-V; (2) spline delta-V; (3) fill + ridge delta-V; (4) tail-aware delta-V (the preregistered median model of Study A, `QR`); (5) ASI of the sensor window; (6) direction-adjusted: the longitudinal component of the plain delta-V for frontal tests and the lateral component for side tests. Reference: the N13 reference delta-V, and the ASI of the unclipped original-rate pulse.

**Primary quantities.** Spearman rank correlation between each measure and the reference delta-V; class agreement (accuracy and linear-weighted kappa) between the class of the estimate and the class of the reference delta-V (the limits of `config.SEV_DV_KMH`: 24, 40, 56 km/h), both with 95% grouped bootstrap intervals (make/model groups).

**H-X5 (primary).** At +-16 g and 100 Hz, the Spearman correlation between the fill + ridge delta-V and the reference delta-V is at least 0.90 and the lower end of its 95% grouped interval is at least 0.85. Null: correlation at most 0.85 (p = share of resamples with correlation at or below 0.85). Class agreement is reported with intervals and is not part of the criterion. **Counts against:** point estimate below 0.90 or lower end below 0.85. The plain estimate's correlation is the baseline and is reported next to it, because the plain estimate is the lower bound and a high correlation of both would mean that the ranking was never the problem.

**Exploratory: injury criteria.** Recorded dummy criteria in the NHTSA metadata (`OCCUPANT` records): for frontal tests the driver-seat dummy (location 01): head injury criterion (`HIC`) and chest 3 ms clip (`CLIP3M`); for side tests the driver-side dummy (location 01): thoracic trauma index (`TTI`) and pelvis acceleration (`PELVG`). A value counts only if it is above 0 and below 9,000 (the file stores missing values as 0 or 9999). The number of tests with each criterion is reported. **The reference's own Spearman correlation with each criterion is reported before any comparison; a criterion whose reference correlation is below 0.2 is dropped from the comparison** (prior work, Wusk and Gabler at the 25th ESV: delta-V, ASI and OLC only weakly related to dummy injury criteria in frontal NCAP tests at one speed; the numbers were given in the task and could not be verified here). For retained criteria: Spearman of each estimate with the criterion and the difference from the reference's correlation with grouped intervals; a loss whose interval reaches below -0.05 counts against "the clipped estimate preserves what the reference says about the dummy". No claim about injury in real crashes is made.

## X6. Lower-bound-anchored estimator (NHTSA, 176 tests)

**Related work to distinguish.** US 7,643,921 (Continental, granted 2010; opened 2026-11-11, local text `data/raw/refs/us7643921.txt`) extrapolates clipped samples with a slope set below 100% of the measured slope; our estimator is a learned correction factor applied to a delta-V estimate and constrained to be at least 1. Jao and Shkel 2022 and Zhang et al. 2026 could not be found by search and are not used.

**Estimator.** dv_anchored = dv_plain x exp(max(0, g(x))): g is a regression of y+ = max(0, log(reference / dv_plain)) on the clipping descriptors (clip_ms, n_runs, clip_frac, slope_in, slope_out, rise_ms, area_unclipped, area_clip), the pulse-family features (dv_mean, dv_median, dv_best, dv_wmean, disagree), dv_plain and pulse_ms (the feature set of Study A; `v2_clip`); the model is gradient boosting with squared-error loss (100 trees, depth 2, learning rate 0.05, subsample 0.8, as in Study A), fitted on the training tests of each fold; the output is clipped at zero from below, so the estimate can never fall below the plain (lower-bound) estimate. A ridge variant of g is EXPLORATORY. Tests with reference below the plain estimate (y < 0; 2 of 176 at +-16 g) enter training with y+ = 0 and are listed.

**Comparators.** fill + ridge (M+), the tail-aware median model (QR), the triangle fit (TRI) and plain (B) on the same splits.

**Splits.** R (primary), G, A, O (`saturation_splits`), +-16 g and 100 Hz primary; +-32 and +-64 g EXPLORATORY.

**H-X6.** At +-16 g on split R, the 90th-percentile absolute delta-V error of the anchored estimator is at least 5 points below that of fill + ridge (point estimate of p90(M+) minus p90(anchored) at least 5, with the 95% grouped interval of the difference excluding zero), the gain also has its whole interval above zero on at least one of the splits G, A, O, and the under-estimation share (estimate below the reference by more than 10%) of the anchored estimator is lower than that of the tail-aware estimator (point estimate of the paired difference below zero; its interval is reported). Null for the p-value: p90 difference at most zero on split R. **Counts against:** the interval includes zero, or the gain appears on one split only, or the under-estimation share is not lower.

## X7. Two-part sensor fusion from real parts (NHTSA, 176 tests; extends Block 2)

**Prior art.** Single-chip dual-range parts exist; a datasheet of one (LSM6DSK320X, low-g to +-16 g, high-g to +-320 g) was opened on 2026-11-12 (`docs/05_data_code/SENSOR_DATASHEETS.md`). The page of the LSM6DSV32X named in the task could not be opened. A fusion method is not the contribution; the quantified requirement table on real pulses is.

**Pairs (fixed before the run).** Low-range part: ADXL345 +-16 g at 3200 Hz, MPU-6050 +-16 g at 1000 Hz, LIS3DH +-16 g at 1250 Hz, KX134-1211 +-16 g at 1600 Hz. High-range part: ADXL375 +-200 g at 3200 Hz, H3LIS331DL +-200 g at 1000 Hz, ADXL372 +-200 g at 3200 Hz, KX134-1211 +-64 g at 1600 Hz. All 16 combinations are evaluated (mounting error and time synchronisation are not modelled: both parts see the same original signal; the high-range signal is resampled onto the low-range grid by linear interpolation). Configurations come from `sel_parts.py` (datasheet values, noise at the datasheet density).

**Fusion.** (i) Substitution with gain match: where the low-range sample is within 2% of its range or inside a clipped run, use the gain-matched high-range sample, elsewhere the low-range sample; the gain is the least-squares ratio of low to high on the unclipped samples of the pulse. (ii) Ridge-calibrated blend: the delta-V of the substituted signal times exp of a ridge prediction from [plain low-range delta-V, high-range delta-V, clipped share of the low-range part] (regularisation by leave-one-make/model-out on the training tests of each fold), out-of-fold on split R.

**Metrics.** Share of tests within +-10% of the reference delta-V (grouped interval), median and 90th-percentile error; the cost of the second part as an extra row (a count of parts and of bus transactions, not a price).

**H-X7.** Some pair, at some configuration in the list, has the lower end of the 95% interval of the share within +-10% at least 0.80 while neither of its two parts alone reaches that lower end at any configuration of the list. Null for the p-value: share within +-10% below 0.80 for that pair (the smallest one-sided bootstrap p over the 16 pairs and two fusion rules, multiplied by 32, capped at 1). **Counts against:** no pair does.

## X8. Sequential decision with error control (VZCrash)

**Rule.** The six length-specific forests of N19 give a crash probability p_k at post-trigger lengths 50, 100, 200, 400, 800 and 1,800 ms. Sequential statistic (a CUSUM-style recursion with lower reflection removed, equal to a SPRT with two boundaries): S_k = S_(k-1) + log(p_k / (1 - p_k)) - log(pi / (1 - pi)), S_0 = 0, with pi the validation crash share among triggered events (probabilities clipped to [0.001, 0.999]). Alarm and stop at the earliest checkpoint with S_k >= h_k; no alarm and stop with S_k <= l_k; at 1,800 ms the threshold t_f decides. The upper boundaries h_k are chosen on validation as the smallest values for which the per-event false-alarm rate at stopping is at most the target (1% or 5% of ALL non-crash events, system level, the trigger-discarded events counting as non-alarms); a common level h with k-dependence h_k = h x w_k (w_k from a fixed grid of three shapes) and l_k = -c in a grid of four values; the choice among grid points is the one with the smallest mean decision time on validation crashes subject to the false-alarm constraint (and recall not below the existing rule's recall on validation).

**Comparators.** The existing early-exit rule (N19, `early.py`) and the full-window forest at 1,800 ms, at the same 1% and 5% targets, seeds 0 to 4, on the de-duplicated and strict sets.

**Metrics.** Median decision time (ms) of triggered crash events, recall at the target, false-alarm rate at the target; paired bootstrap over test events; the ensemble-effect arms of S6 (the same six forests released at 1,800 ms) are repeated for this rule.

**H-X8.** The sequential rule gives an earlier median decision than the existing early-exit rule by at least 100 ms (paired interval excluding zero) at equal or better recall at the same false-alarm target. Null: median difference at most zero. **Counts against:** the interval includes zero, or the recall is lower with its interval excluding zero. The earlier recall gain of N19 was an ensemble effect (S6); the same is checked for this rule and reported.

## Seeds, baselines and what is reported (summary)

| Study | Seeds | Baselines | Primary metric | Origin |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| X1 | 0 to 4 | float forest | AP loss (upper bound) and flash estimate | MEASURED_SOFTWARE; flash ANALYTICAL |
| X2 | none (deterministic) | 2 g and 1.5 g trigger | crash pass share at equal non-crash rate | MEASURED_SOFTWARE |
| X3 | 0 to 4 | standard training | recall at the 1% target, strict set | MEASURED_SOFTWARE |
| X5 | 42 (fold seeds 42 + repeat) | plain estimate | Spearman against the reference delta-V | MEASURED_SOFTWARE |
| X6 | 42 (fold seeds 42 + repeat) | fill + ridge, tail-aware, triangle fit, plain | p90 of the absolute error | MEASURED_SOFTWARE |
| X7 | 42 | each part alone | share within +-10% | MEASURED_SOFTWARE |
| X8 | 0 to 4 | early-exit rule (N19), full window | median decision time at equal recall | MEASURED_SOFTWARE |
