# FILE: PREREGISTRATION_V4.md

# Preregistration v4: the positive-results push, studies P1 to P4 (written and committed before any model of Blocks 1 to 4 is fitted)

Date: 2026-11-12. Branch `positive-push` (from tag `journal-push-v3`). Software only; `data/hardware` is not read. Result keys start with `p.`; no existing key or tagged result is changed.
The sealed split (Z0) was made before this text: `docs/05_data_code/SEALED_SPLIT.md` (commit 2f8d181). What was seen before this text: the record counts by mode, fold and class of the split (the sealed fold has 20 frontal barrier, 115 pole, 14 side and 37 other records; 7 Low, 117 Moderate, 33 Severe and 29 Critical), and all results of earlier rounds (T13a, X5 to X8, UA-SCT).
Anything not written here is EXPLORATORY and says so in its table caption. A mistake in this text is corrected in a dated addendum at the end, never by editing the sentences above it.

Labels: MEASURED_SOFTWARE (computed from NHTSA crash-test records or VZCrash events), SIMULATED (UA-SCT simulator, every value an assumption of `sct_sim`), ANALYTICAL (formula or host arithmetic), NOT_RUN (with reason). NHTSA records are crash tests, mostly high-speed; the sensor is a software model; nothing here is a field result.

## 0. Common rules

- **Data (NHTSA studies P1 to P3).** The 913 pooled records of Z0 (174 + 739). Folds: development (543 records), validation (184), SEALED test (186), whole make-and-model groups together (group = make + leading word of the model). Reference delta-V = the N13 reference (maximum of the running integral over 300 ms of the unclipped, original-rate signal). Modes: frontal barrier, pole, side, other (definitions in `docs/05_data_code/SEALED_SPLIT.md`).
- **Severity classes.** Low, Moderate, Severe, Critical from the resultant delta-V with the limits 24, 40 and 56 km/h (`config.SEV_DV_KMH`), the classes of the UA-SCT study. **Stated deviation:** the UA-SCT classes also take the maximum with an ASI class; the reference ASI exists only for the original 176 tests (not for the 739 T13a records), so the classes here use delta-V only, for the reference and for every estimate. Class counts of the sealed fold are small for Low (7 records); macro-F1 is averaged over the classes present.
- **Metrics (definitions fixed here).** Macro-F1 over the four classes; balanced accuracy; **under-triage rate** = share of true Severe or Critical records predicted a lower class than their true class; **over-triage rate** = share of true Low or Moderate records predicted a higher class than their true class; **mis-triage** (UA-SCT definition) = share of true Severe or Critical records assigned below Severe; **escalation** (UA-SCT definition) = share of true Low or Moderate records assigned Severe or above (including events that abstain, where a study says so).
- **Sensor settings.** The generic sensor chain of `severity_study.sensor_signal` (4th-order zero-phase low-pass at 260 Hz front end, saturation at the range, digital low-pass at min(0.4 x rate, 260 Hz), linear interpolation onto the sample grid), ranges +-16, +-32, +-64 g, rates 100 and 200 Hz. **Primary rate: 100 Hz** (the setting of the project); 200 Hz cells are reported with the same rules and do not enter the verdicts. Primary range for single-range statements: +-16 g.
- **Seeds.** 42 for single-seeded work; 0 to 4 for repeated runs of models with randomness (CNN, forests of Block 4). Gradient-boosted models are deterministic (single thread, no bagging) and use seed 42.
- **Intervals.** 95% percentile bootstrap, 1,000 resamples. NHTSA studies: whole make-and-model groups are resampled together (`boot._gidx`), and the same drawn groups feed every compared method (paired). VZCrash: test events are resampled, the same events feed every compared method.
- **Selection of anything tunable** (hyper-parameters, thresholds, feature subsets, the best model of a family, interval variants) uses the development and validation folds (NHTSA) or the training and validation splits (VZCrash) only. Each model and each setting is frozen in a commit (`results/tables/p_freeze_<hypothesis>.json`, with the sha256 of the model files); the commit hash of each freeze is recorded in `docs/05_data_code/SEALED_SPLIT.md` and in `results/tables/p_sealed_access.csv`. **The sealed fold is scored once per hypothesis**, after its freeze (`pp_common.sealed_gate` refuses a second scoring unless a dated deviation is given). VZCrash test splits are scored once per model and seed.
- **Holm correction.** One Holm correction over the seven hypotheses H-P1, H-P2, H-P3a, H-P3b, H-P4a, H-P4b and H-P4c. Each hypothesis has one p-value: the largest of the one-sided bootstrap p-values (share of resamples on the wrong side of the criterion margin; floor 0.001) of the conditions that must all hold; for the clause "in at least two of three ranges" the p-value is min(1, 2 x the second-smallest of the three per-range p-values). A hypothesis is SUPPORTED if its stated criterion is met and its Holm-adjusted p-value is below 0.05; MIXED if the criterion is met and the adjusted p-value is not below 0.05; NOT SUPPORTED otherwise. P4d (optional) has no hypothesis. Every result is reported with its interval, favourable or not.
- **Mode (Z1).** A deployed node does not know the crash mode. Every model that uses the mode is reported twice: with the true mode (**oracle**, labelled; not deployable) and with a mode inferred from the pulse (**deployable**). The mode classifier: random forest (300 trees, minimum leaf 2, balanced class weights, seed 42) on features of the clipped sensor window (delta-V components and their angle, peak per axis, rise time, pulse width, clipping descriptors); fitted on development, one per range-and-rate cell; its accuracy on validation and on the sealed fold is reported (balanced accuracy and the confusion matrix). For training a model that takes the inferred mode, the development records get a cross-fitted inferred mode (grouped 5-fold inside development); validation and sealed records get the prediction of the classifier fitted on all of development. "Deployable" = uses no mode or the inferred mode.
- **Origins.** All P1 to P4 numbers are MEASURED_SOFTWARE except: flash and RAM estimates (ANALYTICAL, unless a compiled size is measured: then MEASURED_SOFTWARE on the stated host or target) and the UA-SCT cells of P3b (SIMULATED).
- **Novelty scope notes** for each study are in `docs/06_references/NOVELTY_SCAN.md` (written before the study runs). The words of the project wording rule are not used in generated text.

## 1. P1. Severity triage as a classification problem

**Estimators and models (all trained on development only).**
(a) plain delta-V of the clipped signal, binned. (b) fill + ridge (N14: triangle pulse, clip level 0.7, release fraction 0.1; ridge strength by leave-one-group-out inside development), binned. (c) the X6 anchored estimator (boosting of the log correction, never below plain; the Study A feature set; boosting settings fixed: 100 trees, depth 2, learning rate 0.05, subsample 0.8, seed 42), binned. (d) direct ordinal gradient-boosted classifier (LightGBM; three cumulative binary models for y > Low, y > Moderate, y > Severe; predicted class = number of cumulative probabilities at or above 0.5; no threshold tuning) on clipping descriptors and waveform features: delta-V of the clipped signal, pulse length, clipped duration, number of clipped runs, clipped share, time to the earliest clipped sample, rise rate to the clip, area before the clip, area of the unclipped part, peak of the unclipped part (as a share of the range), slopes into and out of the clip, ratios (clipped duration over pulse length, area before clip over time to clip, peak of the unclipped part over the range, delta-V per millisecond of pulse). **Monotone (non-decreasing) constraints** where physics supports them: delta-V of the clipped signal, clipped duration, clipped share, area of the unclipped part, peak of the unclipped part. Hyper-parameters chosen on validation macro-F1 from leaves {4, 8} x trees {50, 150, 300} (learning rate 0.05, minimum leaf 10; ties go to fewer trees, then fewer leaves). Mode input: none, oracle or inferred (Z1). (e) a small 1D CNN on the clipped waveform (three channels: x, y and the resultant, divided by the range; window 0.1 s before to 0.4 s after the trigger at the cell's rate; conv 3-16 (k5), conv 16-32 (k5, stride 2), conv 32-32 (k3), global max and mean pooling, a dense layer of 32 units, three cumulative-probability outputs; Adam, learning rate 0.001, batch 32, at most 150 epochs, the epoch chosen on validation macro-F1; mode concatenated before the dense layer in the oracle and inferred variants). Seeds 0 to 4; the model is the average of the five seeds' cumulative probabilities; the per-seed macro-F1 spread is reported. (f) the best of (d) and (e) with training-data augmentation: on every development record, 6 augmented copies with time scaling of the pulse (factor log-uniform on [0.8, 1.25]), amplitude scaling (log-uniform on [0.7, 1.4], applied to the physical signal before the sensor model, so clipping follows), additive white noise on the sensor output (standard deviation uniform on [0.005, 0.03] g) and a random resampling phase (uniform within one sample); the reference delta-V and class of an augmented copy are recomputed from the augmented original-rate signal. Augmentation touches development training data only. (f) is built for (d) and for (e); the one used in the hypothesis is the better of the two by validation macro-F1.

**Primary comparison (H-P1).** At each range at 100 Hz, the best deployable direct model (validation macro-F1 chosen among (d), (e), (f) with the inferred mode or with no mode) against the best of (a) to (c) (validation macro-F1 chosen among the three), both frozen, then scored on the sealed fold: paired differences (direct minus baseline) of macro-F1 and of the under-triage rate, grouped bootstrap. Also reported: every model and every cell (6 cells), oracle-mode variants (labelled), over-triage, balanced accuracy, mis-triage, per-mode results, and the +-16 g result alone.
**H-P1.** In at least two of the three ranges, macro-F1 of the best deployable direct model is higher by at least 0.03 with the interval of the difference above zero, AND its under-triage rate is lower by at least 0.03 with the interval of the difference below zero. **Counts against:** in two or more ranges either interval includes zero (or the point difference is under 0.03). The +-16 g result is reported on its own and is not a separate hypothesis.
**EXPLORATORY (not part of H-P1):** grouped 5-fold cross-validation over all 913 records for (a) to (d), to show the size of the differences with more records than the sealed fold.

## 2. P2. Certified severity interval from a physical lower bound

The plain estimate is below the reference in almost all tests, so it is a one-sided bound. Interval = [plain, plain x q]. q is a conformal quantile of the ratio reference / plain on the VALIDATION fold (whole groups stay in the fold; models of the base estimator are not used: the interval is a function of the plain estimate). Target coverage 90%: the upper level is 1 - alpha with alpha = 0.10 minus the share of validation records whose reference is below the plain estimate (floor alpha = 0.02), and q = the ceil((n + 1)(1 - alpha))-th smallest ratio. Variants: **global q (primary)**; q by clipping-severity bin (Mondrian; bins of the clipped duration of the window, a quantity the node computes: none, then the two halves of the clipped records at the development median); q by inferred mode (Z1, deployable); and, EXPLORATORY, a global q with each validation record weighted by 1 / group size. **Existing interval:** the relative-residual split-conformal interval around fill + ridge (S9/N24 in its split form: calibration = validation, interval [f (1 - r), f (1 + r)] with r the 90% conformal quantile of |reference - f| / f; the lower end is clipped at 0.1 x f; the upper-to-lower ratio is (1 + r) / max(1 - r, 0.1)). The grouped CV+ form of N24 is reported as EXPLORATORY.
Metrics on the sealed fold: coverage overall and per true mode (grouped intervals), mean upper-to-lower width ratio, mean width in km/h, share of frontal barrier intervals inside the physical band v <= delta-V <= 2 v (v = closing speed of the test; both ends inside), at +-16, +-32 and +-64 g and 100 Hz (200 Hz reported).
**H-P2 (primary variant: global q; +-16 g, 100 Hz).** The lower end of the 95% grouped interval of the sealed coverage is at least 0.85 overall and in each mode with at least 50 sealed records (by the split this is pole only; frontal barrier, side and other have fewer and are reported descriptively), AND the point estimate of the relative reduction (existing ratio - new ratio) / existing ratio of the mean upper-to-lower width ratio is at least 0.20 with the lower end of its grouped interval above zero. **Counts against:** the coverage lower end is below 0.85 overall or in any mode with at least 50 records, or the width reduction is under 0.20 or its interval includes zero. The result says which condition failed. The p-value of the width clause is the share of resamples with reduction at or below 0.20; that of the coverage clause the share with coverage at or below 0.85 (overall and pole; the larger).

## 3. P3. Knowing when the clipped reading cannot be trusted

**P3a (selective prediction).** Reliability score s(x): a gradient-boosted regression (settings as in (c)) on the clipping descriptors of (d), fitted on development to predict log(reference / plain) (the amount by which the plain estimate under-reads); the score is cross-fitted inside development (grouped 5-fold) for the development rows. Optional addition (chosen on validation by the retained p90 error of the anchored estimator at 70% retention, at +-16 g): the class probabilities of the best deployable direct model of P1 (maximum probability and entropy) as further inputs. An event abstains when s(x) exceeds the 70th percentile of s on the VALIDATION fold (fixed threshold, frozen). Risk-coverage curves (p90 absolute delta-V error and under-triage against the retained share, retention 100% down to 30% in steps of 5 points, by the ranking of s) for plain, fill + ridge, anchored and the best direct model, at +-16 and +-32 g, 100 Hz, on the sealed fold; also the share of true Severe or Critical records that abstain.
**H-P3a.** Primary estimator: the anchored estimator (c) at +-16 g, 100 Hz; retention fixed by the validation threshold (the realised retained share on the sealed fold is reported; a variant that keeps exactly 70% of the sealed records by rank uses no labels and is reported next to it). Under-triage among retained events is at most half the unselected rate (point estimate of the ratio retained / unselected at most 0.5, and the upper end of the 95% grouped interval of the ratio below 1), AND the p90 delta-V error among retained events is at most 20% (point estimate). **Counts against:** either fails, or the interval of the ratio includes 1. The p-value is the larger of: share of resamples with ratio at or above 1, and share with retained p90 error at or above 20%.

**P3b (abstain as an escalation signal).** Setup of T1: the UA-SCT simulator (`sct_sim`, parameters of `s11.tuned.*`), the same scenario cells (steady and burst, the one-factor variants), seeds 3000 + hour with 30 seeds per cell (hours 0 to 29), the sealed-fold class pairs (true class, assigned class) at the range, with the delay and false-alarm pools of `s11_sct.load_pools`. Systems: **A** = the SCT policy with the anchored estimator's point class, where abstaining events (frozen P3a threshold) are assigned Critical (sent to the fast path); **P1e** = the severity-table policy with the same point class, every class boundary divided by a factor f; **P2e** = the SCT policy with the same lowered point class. f is chosen on the VALIDATION fold, on the grid 0.30 to 1.00 in steps of 0.0025, so that the escalation of the lowered table on validation equals the escalation of A on validation (no simulated outcome is used). The escalation of both on the sealed fold is reported with the gap; a comparison at equal escalation by interpolation on the sealed curve is reported as EXPLORATORY.
**H-P3b (+-16 g, 100 Hz).** Pool-level mis-triage of A minus that of P1e on the sealed pairs is at most -0.02 (point), with the 95% grouped paired interval entirely below zero. **Counts against:** the interval includes zero or the reduction is under 2 points (N28 then stays NO_IMPROVEMENT_FOUND, and this is said). The simulated cells (SIMULATED) report mis-triage, escalation, critical reach within 10 s and 30 s, and node airtime, with intervals over seeds; they are descriptive for this hypothesis.

## 4. P4. The VZCrash detector

Data and code: the supplied splits, `detector_extra.frames_full` (training = triggered training windows; validation = all validation events; test = the de-duplicated test events, 24,022 events, PRIMARY); the strict set (22,830 events) is the sensitivity set and the primary set for H-P4b. The 14 features of `config.FEATURES`. Thresholds from validation only (`threshold_for_fpr`: system-level false-alarm rate over all non-crash events). Metrics: AP of triggered test events, system recall at the 1% and 5% false-alarm targets, system recall at the validation-F1 threshold. Seeds 0 to 4, 5-seed mean, paired bootstrap over test events.

**P4a. Memory-accuracy Pareto.** Sweep trees 2 to 10, depth 3 to 9, feature-subset size k in {4, 6, 8, 10, 14} (features ranked by the mean impurity importance of the full 10-tree depth-9 forest over seeds 0 to 4 on the TRAINING split; the top k), float and 16-bit thresholds and leaves (X1 quantiser). Flash (ANALYTICAL) = nodes x bytes per node (9 bytes with float32 thresholds, 7 bytes with 16-bit, the layouts of X1) summed over the trees of the seed-mean; RAM (ANALYTICAL) = 4 bytes per feature + 4 bytes accumulator + 16 bytes of loop state, plus a configuration-independent window buffer of 280 x 3 x 2 bytes, stated separately. If gcc is usable, the C code of selected configurations is compiled and its `.text` size is measured (MEASURED_SOFTWARE, host x86-64, `-Os`, not an ESP32 figure; no microcontroller cross compiler and no emlearn were available at the time of writing, stated in `docs/07_log/DECISIONS.md` if still true). The reference ("current") model: the 10-tree depth-9 forest with all 14 features and float thresholds; its flash estimate is the "current flash estimate". Selection for H-P4a: among configurations whose validation AP (5-seed mean, triggered validation events) is within 0.005 of the reference's, the one with the smallest flash estimate (ties: fewer RAM bytes, then fewer trees); the sweep is scored on the test split only once per model, for the Pareto figure, never for selection.
**H-P4a.** The selected configuration has at most half the current flash estimate and the upper end of the 95% interval of (reference AP - its AP) on the de-duplicated test set is at most 0.01. **Counts against:** no configuration satisfies the validation rule, or the selected one has more than half the current flash estimate or an interval upper end above 0.01. The p-value is the share of resamples with AP loss at or above 0.01.

**P4b. Augmentation with group-aware sampling.** Variants of the 15-tree depth-6 forest of the headline (`rf15`): standard; and augmented: on crash training windows, 2 augmented copies each with a time shift (uniform integer shift of the signal relative to the trigger within +-10 samples, edge repeat), amplitude scaling (log-uniform on [0.8, 1.25] on the physical reading, then clipped at +-16 g) and additive noise (standard deviation uniform on [0.01, 0.05] g on each axis), the 14 features recomputed; and near-identical training windows (identical 280-sample windows, the X3 groups) merged into one training unit (one window kept per group; for augmented copies the unit is the group). Thresholds from the unchanged validation split.
**H-P4b.** Recall at the 1% false-alarm target on the strict test set is at least 0.02 higher than standard training (5-seed mean of the paired difference at least 0.02, interval above zero). **Counts against:** the interval includes zero or the gain is under 2 points. X3 (down-weighting, merging) is the closest earlier result and is cited as such.

**P4c. Restoring the false-alarm guarantee under vehicle shift.** The only vehicle label is the vehicle size (Light, Medium, Heavy, Unknown); there is no vehicle identifier (X4). **Groups = vehicle-size categories.** Model: `rf15`, seeds 0 to 4. Calibration negatives = non-crash validation events (system level: events the trigger discards count as non-alarms). Targets alpha = 0.01 and 0.05. Variants: (V0) the trivial rule: lowest threshold meeting the rate on the pooled calibration set (`empirical_threshold`); (V1) pooled split-conformal (`conformal_threshold`; the existing X4 method); (V2) conformal calibration by group: one conformal threshold per category, used for events of that category; (V3) CV+ with category groups: K models each trained without one training category, calibration scores of the validation negatives of category k from model k when it did not see k, test event alarms if its CV+ conformal p-value is at most alpha; (V4) conformal risk control with group-level calibration: the threshold is the smallest for which the mean over groups of (n_g R_g + 1) / (n_g + 1) is at most alpha, with R_g the false-alarm rate of group g on the calibration set; (V5) the most conservative group-wise calibration quantile (maximum over groups of the group conformal thresholds).
**Evaluation (primary): leave-one-category-out.** For each held-out category c: calibration groups are the other categories (V2 cannot use group c and falls back to pooled V1 for it; V4 and V5 use the other groups only; V3 trains without c as well), the test events are the de-duplicated test events of category c, and the results are pooled over c (event-weighted); 1,000 bootstrap resamples of test events within category, 5-seed mean. Also reported (secondary): the pooled supplied test split with calibration on all validation categories. 
**H-P4c.** Some variant (V1 to V5) has, at both 1% and 5%, an observed false-alarm rate with the 95% upper end at most alpha + 0.01, AND recall at least 0.02 above that of the trivial rule (V0) at the same target. **Counts against:** no variant does; the failure of the guarantee is then reported as a limitation of the dataset's vehicle structure (no vehicle identifier). The p-value: the largest over the two targets of the share of resamples where the false-alarm rate is at or above alpha + 0.01 or the recall gain is at or below 0.02, for the variant with the smallest such value among V1 to V5 (multiplied by 5 for that choice, capped at 1).

**P4d (optional, EXPLORATORY).** An int8 tiny 1D CNN against the forest on AP and estimated flash, run only if time remains; no hypothesis.

## 5. Benchmark and tools (Block 5)

B1 (CrashClip protocol), B2 (requirement calculator) and B3 (`docs/05_data_code/REPRODUCE_FINISH.md`) are packaging; they contain no hypothesis. B2 uses the rule "lower end of the 95% grouped interval of the share of records within +-10% of the reference delta-V at least 0.80", per mode and for a stated mix of modes, from the plain estimate on the pooled records over the sensor configurations of `sel_parts` (the 8 parts), ordered by range, then rate. It is MEASURED_SOFTWARE on the 913 records (all records: it describes the pooled set, not a held-out claim; stated in its table).

## 6. Seeds, baselines and what is reported (summary)

| Study | Seeds | Baselines | Primary metric | Origin |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | 42 (GBM), 0 to 4 (CNN) | best of plain, fill + ridge, anchored | macro-F1 and under-triage on the sealed fold | MEASURED_SOFTWARE |
| P2 | none (deterministic) | relative-residual split-conformal around fill + ridge | sealed coverage lower end; width ratio | MEASURED_SOFTWARE |
| P3a | 42 | unselected anchored estimator | retained under-triage ratio; retained p90 error | MEASURED_SOFTWARE |
| P3b | 3000 + hour, 30 seeds per cell | severity table with lowered thresholds | mis-triage at equal escalation | MEASURED_SOFTWARE (pool), SIMULATED (cells) |
| P4a | 0 to 4 | 10-tree depth-9 forest, 14 features | AP loss (upper bound); flash | MEASURED_SOFTWARE; flash ANALYTICAL |
| P4b | 0 to 4 | standard training | recall at the 1% target, strict set | MEASURED_SOFTWARE |
| P4c | 0 to 4 | lowest threshold meeting the rate | false-alarm rate (upper end) and recall | MEASURED_SOFTWARE |

## Addenda

### Addendum 1 (2026-11-12, written after the P1, P2 and P3 freezes were committed and before the sealed fold was scored; P4a had been scored and P4b was being scored)

Clarifications of the text above. None changes a hypothesis, metric, threshold or rule; each says how an unspecified detail was resolved.
1. **p-values.** The bootstrap p-value of a condition of the form "interval above zero" uses the margin 0 (share of resamples at or below 0 for a gain, at or above 0 for a reduction of under-triage). Where the criterion also names a point margin (0.03, 0.02, 0.20, 0.01 +alpha), that margin is checked on the point estimate; where the text of a hypothesis gives the margin for the p-value (H-P2 width clause 0.20, H-P3a retained p90 0.20, H-P4a 0.01, H-P4b 0.02, H-P4c alpha + 0.01 and 0.02) that margin is used.
2. **P1.** The ordinal GBM and the CNN are trained on the development records only; the validation fold chooses the hyper-parameters, the CNN epoch, the best baseline and the best deployable direct model per cell; ties go to the earlier name in the order a, b, c, d_none, d_inferred, e_none, e_inferred, fd_none, fd_inferred, fe_none, fe_inferred. The oracle comparison uses the oracle-mode variant with the best validation macro-F1. The ridge of (b) and the anchored estimator of (c) use the feature set of `pp_models.anchor_x` (the Study A set with the pulse length of the severity code instead of that of `v2_clip`). Classes absent from a bootstrap resample are not averaged in macro-F1.
3. **P3a.** The class-probability inputs of the score variant s2 come from the no-mode direct GBM (cross-fitted inside development; the frozen P1 model elsewhere) whatever the best direct model is. The score regressor is fitted on all development records and is not cross-fitted (nothing uses its development scores). "Retained" means score at or below the validation 70th percentile.
4. **P3b.** The anchored point class at +-16 and +-32 g is used for A, P1e and P2e; the lowered table uses the anchored estimate divided by f. +-64 g is not run (no P3a score at +-64 g).
5. **P4b.** `frames_full` returns the de-duplicated test events, so the de-duplicated set is the whole frame and the supplied (24,174-event) set is not scored. The initial run of the evaluation stopped before scoring because of a code error (`te.leaky` does not exist); see `results/tables/p_sealed_access.csv` and `docs/07_log/DECISIONS.md`.
6. **P4c.** In the leave-one-category-out evaluation V2 has no calibration data of the held-out group and falls back to the pooled V1 threshold, so V2 equals V1 there; the pooled supplied-test evaluation (secondary) is where V2 differs. V3 is scored in the leave-one-category-out evaluation only.
7. **P4a.** The selection margin of 0.005 validation AP is part of the rule as written; the sweep scored the test split once for all configurations after the freeze.

---

# FILE: PREREGISTRATION_V5.md

# Preregistration v5: finishing study (written and committed before any run of Blocks B, C, F and G)

Date: 2026-11-13. Branch `finish` (from tag `positive-push-v1`). Software only; `data/hardware` is not read. Result keys start with `f.`; no tagged result and no existing key is changed. Nothing is pushed or uploaded.
What was seen before this text: every result of the earlier rounds (V1 to V4, UA-SCT, X1 to X8, P1 to P4d, T-series). What was NOT seen: any number of Blocks B, C, F or G. Anything not written here is EXPLORATORY and says so. A change after this commit is a dated, labelled deviation in the addendum at the end and in `docs/07_log/DECISIONS.md`; sentences above are never edited.
Labels: MEASURED_SOFTWARE, SIMULATED, ANALYTICAL, NOT_RUN. Anything not completed is recorded NOT_RUN with its reason; this is allowed and is not a deviation. Wording rule of the repository applies (the words forbidden by CLAUDE.md, rule 7, are not used).

## 0. Common rules

1. **Revised after the negative.** Every B2, F and G item that revisits an earlier negative result is a revised design tested after seeing the negative. It is reported next to the original result, never in place of it, and tables say "revised after the negative". Confirmation counts only on scenarios, seeds and records not used to design it. F results are labelled EXPLORATORY-REVISED everywhere.
2. **Seeds.** 42 for single-seeded work; 0 to 4 for repeated runs of models with randomness. Simulation seeds (hour index h = 0 to 29, 30 seeds per cell, always paired across policies): existing design scenarios of the UA-SCT study use seed 3000 + h (the stored runs); B2 held-out 5000 + h; F3 9000 + h; G DESIGN grid 6000 + h; G HELD-OUT grid 8000 + h. Synthetic pulse generator seed 42 (pulse k uses seed 42 + k).
3. **Intervals.** 95% percentile bootstrap, 1,000 resamples. Simulation cells: seeds resampled inside each cell; across-cell statements count cells. NHTSA records: whole make-and-model groups resampled together; paired across compared methods. VZCrash: test events resampled; paired.
4. **A cell "gains" if** the point difference in the stated metric is at least the stated margin AND the 95% interval excludes zero. A cell "loses by more than 2 points" if the point difference is below -0.02. (Applies to H-B2, H-F3a, H-F3b, H-G3a, H-G3b, H-G3c.)
5. **Holm correction** over the 18 hypotheses of this document: H-B2, H-C3, H-F1, H-F1b, H-F1c, H-F1d, H-F1e, H-F2a, H-F2b, H-F2c, H-F2d, H-F2e, H-F2f, H-F3a, H-F3b, H-G3a, H-G3b, H-G3c. Each has one p-value: the largest one-sided bootstrap p-value (share of resamples on the wrong side of the criterion; floor 0.001) among the conditions that must all hold; for "in at least a share q of cells", the p-value is the one of the cell at the q-quantile of the per-cell p-values (cells ordered from smallest p). SUPPORTED = criterion met and Holm-adjusted p below 0.05; MIXED = criterion met, adjusted p not below 0.05; NOT SUPPORTED otherwise. F hypotheses keep that verdict word and carry the extra label EXPLORATORY-REVISED.
6. **Data origin and splits.** NHTSA pool: the 913 records and the make-and-model group folds of `docs/05_data_code/SEALED_SPLIT.md` (development 543, validation 184, sealed 186). VZCrash: supplied splits; primary test set de-duplicated (24,022 events), strict set 22,830 events; thresholds from validation only; test touched once per model and seed (revised methods are scored on the already-scored sealed fold and test split once each, labelled "revised after the negative"; the main estimate is grouped nested cross-validation, rule 11).
7. **Not a real-world claim.** Simulated results are statements about the simulator under numbered assumptions (section G0). Synthetic pulses are a stress test; they are never called unbiased and never a substitute for real data.
8. **Sources.** No paper, datasheet or dataset is cited unless opened; URL and date are recorded in `docs/06_references/REFERENCES.md`; a source that cannot be opened is said so and none of its numbers is used.

## B. Problems found in simulated and protocol work

**B1 (ANALYTICAL; a check of the model within bounds, not of the implementation).** Property C3 of `protocol_model_ext` (a CANCEL that was delivered is never lost because it overtook its alert) fails for the design variant in 351,754 reachable states (bounds EXT, the T12 trace: alert and CANCEL sent, CANCEL delivered ahead of the alert and refused as unknown, alert delivered later, event active although cancelled). Proposed minimal change: the base remembers a valid CANCEL for a counter it has not seen yet (exact counter, bounded memory) and applies it when that alert arrives (`keep_pending_cancel` of T12). Re-run with the same bounds EXT and all original properties (S1 to S4, C1 to C4, F1). Reported: states explored, depth, properties checked, whether the counterexample disappears. A watermark form (a single cancelled-up-to counter) is NOT proposed: it would cancel a delayed earlier event; an exploratory two-event bound shows this if it completes. The reference code (`sct_protocol.SctBase`) gets an option `pending_cancel` (default off: the 48 + 9 attack cases of the tagged code are unchanged); with it on, every attack case is re-run and X06 is expected to change from "not cancelled" to "cancelled"; every other case is expected unchanged. Not a hypothesis.

**B2 (SIMULATED). Which parts of the fast path carry the gain.** Earlier findings: ablations at +-16 g found that flash and hold carry the gain; the equal-escalation test (N28) found the interval adds nothing over a lowered table. Simple consequence tested here: **P1+** = the severity-table policy (P1: tries 2, 4, 8, 12 by class, service by class, immediate retry) plus the two parts: (a) flash ahead of the alert for Severe and Critical (14-byte flash, then the 97-byte alert) and (b) hold of 0 s for Critical and `s11.tuned.h_s` s for Severe (Low and Moderate keep the 10 s and 8 s windows). No SF raise, no reserved duty share, no pre-emption, no spaced retries. Comparators: P0 (fixed 3 retries), P1 (current thresholds), **P1e** (P1 with every class boundary divided by the factor f of `t1.r16.f`, chosen on real pool rows to equal the escalation of P3), **P1+e** (P1+ with the same lowered classes), P3 (UA-SCT) for reference. Class pools: the +-16 g NHTSA cross-validated pairs of the UA-SCT study. Scenario: one cell has n cars, packet loss p (up and down), SF, a burst of b Critical events at 600 s plus background events, duty 1%, hb 600 s, 16 byte heartbeats (all other values as the UA-SCT study).
- DESIGN: the existing UA-SCT grid at +-16 g (9 burst cells, 7 steady cells), seeds 3000 + h, h = 0 to 29. P0 and P1 are the stored runs; P1+ and P1+e are new runs on the same seeds. Used only to look at the size and direction of the effect; no parameter is tuned (P1+ uses the stored tuned values).
- **HELD-OUT (confirmation):** cars {75, 150} x packet loss {10%, 30%, 50%} x SF 8 x burst size {20, 45} = 12 cells, +-16 g, 30 fresh seeds per cell (5000 + h).
- **H-B2.** P1+ beats P0 on critical reach within 10 s by at least 5 points with the interval excluding zero in at least 60% of the held-out cells (at least 8 of 12), AND loses by more than 2 points in none. **Counts against:** fewer than 60% of cells, or any cell lost by more than 2 points. p-value rule 5 (cell at the 60% quantile; and the largest per-cell p of the losing clause).
- Also reported (descriptive, with intervals): P1+ minus P1, P1+e minus P1e (flash and hold at equal escalation: identical class assignment, so escalation is equal by construction), P1+e minus P3, critical reach within 30 s, critical p95 latency, duty-cycle use (node airtime share and fast-path share), the share of non-severe events escalated (`escalation_used`), duty violations (must be 0). +-32 and +-64 g cells are EXPLORATORY.

**B3 (ANALYTICAL and SIMULATED). Simulator sanity checks.** (i) Unit tests of `lora.airtime_ms` against Semtech's published time-on-air formula for SF7 to SF9, 125 kHz, coding rate 4/5, 8-symbol preamble, explicit header, CRC on, payloads 14, 16, 26 and 97 bytes; the document opened is cited with URL and date; if it cannot be opened the test is recorded as against the formula as transcribed and says so. (ii) Periodic traffic with no capture (`capture="none"`, no alerts, no extra loss, SF7, 16-byte heartbeats, 1,000 s period jittered by the simulator's own rule, n cars chosen to span offered load G = n x airtime / period from 0.01 to 1): the simulated collision probability of an unconfirmed heartbeat against 1 - exp(-2G); the error (absolute and relative) is reported; mismatches are limitations and the simulator is not tuned. (iii) The duty-cycle limit (independent scan of logged transmissions) is never exceeded in any run of any cell reported by this document; the count of runs is reported and must be 0.

**B4.** `docs/03_results/NEGATIVES_REGISTER.md`: one row per negative result (N20, N28, H-P1 to H-P4c, H-X1 to H-X8, T4 single-car loss case, P3b), original verdicts unchanged, inferred causes marked "inferred". Not a hypothesis.

## C. Synthetic stress-test pulses (SIMULATED)

**C1 generator.** Per crash mode (frontal barrier, pole, side, other) a pulse-family generator on the horizontal plane at 10 kHz: families half-sine, triangle (peak at fraction a of the duration) and multi-peak (2 or 3 half-sine bumps); the family is chosen with probability 1/3 each (assumption A-C1). Parameters per mode: log peak resultant acceleration, log duration, logit of the asymmetry a, drawn from a multivariate normal fitted to the pooled real pulses of that mode (mean and covariance only; all 913 records for the stress-test file and the C-block results; the development fold only, refitted inside each training fold, for any F-block training use, so that no validation or sealed information enters training). Direction angle drawn from the empirical angle distribution of the mode. Additive band-limited noise with relative level fitted to the real pulses. Quiet pre-trigger 0.1 s. Every numbered assumption (value and source) is a line in `docs/05_data_code/SYNTHETIC_ASSUMPTIONS.md`. Published: code, seeds and a fixed file of 5,000 pulses (parameters and 2 kHz waveforms; modes in the proportion of the pool).
**C2 fidelity.** Per mode, real against synthetic: two-sample Kolmogorov-Smirnov on peak resultant g, duration (width at 10% of the peak), delta-V (the N13 reference), asymmetry (time of peak over duration) and a shape descriptor (area over peak x duration); plots. **A mode passes if the KS statistic is at most 0.20 for every one of the five descriptors.** A failing mode is excluded from C3, F and G and this is said. The fit is in-sample for the C-block (stated).
**C3 use.** The N13 sensor-range grid (B2 of V4: eight real parts, ranges, rates, plain delta-V within +-10% of the reference, rule: lower end of the 95% interval of the share at least 0.80), run on the synthetic pulses of the modes that passed (independent pulses: 1,000 per mode, bootstrap over pulses).
- **H-C3.** For each overlapping mode that passed fidelity (frontal barrier, pole), the smallest range meeting the 0.80 rule on the synthetic pulses is within one range step of the real-data result (`p.b2.<mode>.smallest.config`). A range step is one rung of the ladder of ranges of the eight parts (sorted union of `sel_parts.study_ranges`). **Counts against:** it differs by more than one range step for any overlapping mode that passed. If no mode passes: NOT_RUN. p-value: not defined for an exact-match criterion; H-C3 enters Holm with p = 0.001 if it holds in all passing modes and 1.0 otherwise (stated; a criterion about agreement has no sampling null).
- Extension (EXPLORATORY, assumption-dependent): the log-peak mean of each mode shifted by +0.2, +0.4 and +0.6 (A-C2) to represent more severe crashes; required range reported per shift; sensitivity of the required range to each assumption varied one at a time (family mix 1/3 each against 0.5/0.25/0.25 and 0.25/0.25/0.5; duration scale -20% and +20%; correlation of log peak and log duration +-0.2; noise level x0.5 and x2; pre-trigger length). Every figure and table from synthetic pulses is captioned "synthetic stress test, assumptions in SYNTHETIC_ASSUMPTIONS.md".

## F. Reruns of the failed algorithms with real plus synthetic training data (all EXPLORATORY-REVISED)

**F rules.** (1) Synthetic data train only. Validation, conformal calibration, threshold and ratio selection and every reported test metric use real data only; no synthetic record is in any score called a real-data result. (2) Gate: a synthetic mode or family is used only if it passed C2; the used set is recorded. (3) Mixing ratio r = synthetic records per real training record, chosen on the REAL validation fold from {0, 0.5, 1, 2, 5}; r = 0 is the control and uses identical code (same augmentation, same pipeline), so the effect of synthetic data is separated from the effect of new code. (4) Per task: the original result, the real-only rerun (r = 0), the best r, an interval; a statement whether any gain exists only at r > 0. (5) TSTR diagnostic for each model family: train on synthetic only, test on real (a fidelity measure; never a result, and a real-to-synthetic score is never reported). (6) Original criteria and thresholds of each hypothesis are reused unchanged. (7) The sealed NHTSA fold and the supplied VZCrash test split were scored by the original methods; each revised method is scored on them once, labelled "revised after the negative"; the main estimate is grouped nested cross-validation over all real records (groups together, stratified by mode on the NHTSA side; 5 outer folds, the inner validation fold chosen group-wise inside the training part), with Holm over the F hypotheses (rule 5). (8) Nothing about the sealed fold or test split is used to choose r or any setting.
**F0 VZCrash-style surrogate generator.** Accelerometer windows (280 samples x 3 axes at 100 Hz, trigger at sample 100), crash and non-crash, fitted to SUMMARY statistics of the training split only (class balance, peak and duration distributions, spectral band shares, onset behaviour (pre-trigger level and rise time)), never to individual records; only fitted parameters and code are published. Validation: marginals and spectra against the supplied windows by two-sample tests and plots; nearest-neighbour check of every synthetic window against all real training windows (L-infinity on the 32-bin downsampled |a| and on the full window) with the distance distribution reported against the real-to-real near-duplicate distances; no synthetic window may be a copy (minimum distance above the 1st percentile of real-to-real nearest-neighbour distances, else the offending windows are dropped and counted). README states: VZCrash is CC BY-NC 4.0 (Hugging Face revision 42e1482d005c0e545379d6cfa4939486c9fe4249); the generator parameters are therefore released under CC BY-NC 4.0 with attribution; only summary parameters are released; it matches only the marginals that were validated; detector scores on synthetic windows are not comparable with real-data scores; the surrogate is not called unbiased.
**F1 NHTSA side** (913 pool; ranges +-16, +-32, +-64 g, 100 Hz primary): training on development real records (+ augmentation of the original design, 6 copies) plus r x (number of real training records) synthetic pulses passing through the same sensor chain.
- **H-F1 (original H-P1).** Best deployable direct model (candidates: augmented direct GBM and augmented 1D CNN, mode none; chosen on validation macro-F1 over model kind and r) against the best of plain, fill + ridge, anchored (original, real-only; the same estimators also trained with the chosen r are reported as the fairer baseline, EXPLORATORY-REVISED): macro-F1 higher by at least 0.03 with the interval above zero AND under-triage lower by at least 0.03 with the interval below zero, in at least two of three ranges. **Counts against:** in two or more ranges either interval includes zero or the point difference is under 0.03.
- **H-F1b (original H-P2).** Interval [plain, anchored estimate x q]; q the conformal quantile of reference / anchored on the REAL validation fold with the V4 alpha rule (target 90%); anchored trained with r. Coverage lower end at least 0.85 overall and for pole on the sealed fold, AND the relative reduction of the mean upper-to-lower ratio against the existing relative-residual interval around fill + ridge at least 0.20 with the lower end above zero. **Counts against:** either fails.
- **H-F1c (original H-P3a).** Reliability score regressor trained with r; abstain above the 70th percentile of the score on the REAL validation fold. Retained under-triage at most half the unselected rate (ratio at most 0.5 with the upper end below 1) AND retained p90 error at most 20%.
- **H-F1d (original H-X6, splits G and O).** On the 176 original tests (grouped 5-fold, 10 repeats of `saturation_splits`), +-16 g, 100 Hz: the gain in p90 absolute error of the anchored estimator over fill + ridge is at least 5 points on split R, and its whole interval above zero on at least one of the splits G, A, O, with under-estimation share below that of the tail-aware estimator. The rerun reports splits G and O (where the original gain failed) and R; the original criterion applied unchanged to the three splits G, O and R.
- **H-F1e (original H-X5).** Spearman correlation of fill + ridge delta-V with the reference at least 0.90 and lower end at least 0.85 at +-16 g, 100 Hz, trained with r inside the grouped folds.
**F2 VZCrash side** (5 seeds; primary set de-duplicated; strict set sensitivity): the 15-tree depth-6 forest trained on real triggered training windows plus r x (real count) surrogate windows (features recomputed by the 14-feature code), r chosen on the real validation split (AP).
- **H-F2a (original H-P4b).** Recall at the 1% false-alarm target on the strict set at least 0.02 above standard training (r = 0, real only), interval above zero.
- **H-F2b (original H-X3).** Group-aware training (down-weight 1 / group size of real windows; surrogate windows weight 1) raises recall at the 1% target on the strict set by at least 0.01 over standard real-only training, interval above zero.
- **H-F2c (original H-X2).** Trigger cascade: the rule family and selection of V3 use whole 16 s records; surrogate windows are trigger-aligned windows and cannot train or test a trigger. Preregistered as NOT_RUN unless a record-level surrogate exists (none is planned); reported with that reason, original verdict unchanged.
- **H-F2d (original H-X1).** 16-bit forest trained with r: AP loss upper bound at most 0.005 AND flash estimate cut at least 30% under the primary layout. The flash clause is arithmetic (22.2% for 16-bit thresholds in the primary layout) and cannot change with training data; the verdict is therefore expected to stay NOT SUPPORTED and the AP clause is reported.
- **H-F2e (original H-X8).** Sequential rule (six length-specific forests trained with r) earlier median decision than the early-exit rule by at least 100 ms at equal or better recall, same targets.
- **H-F2f (original H-P4c).** Calibration stays on real held-out vehicle-size categories (leave-one-category-out); with the better-trained model, some variant has false-alarm upper end at most alpha + 0.01 at both 1% and 5% AND recall at least 0.02 above the trivial rule.
**F3 simulator** (SIMULATED): the improved severity estimates of F1 (pairs from the sealed and nested-CV predictions at +-16 g) feed the UA-SCT simulator on the HELD-OUT grid of B2 (12 cells, seeds 9000 + h, 30 seeds). **H-F3a (original N28 decision rule):** P3 with the improved estimates beats the lowered-threshold table at equal escalation: pool-level mis-triage interval (P3 minus P1e) entirely below zero AND critical reach within 10 s not worse than -0.02 at the lower end in steady and burst cells. For the held-out grid (burst cells): in at least 60% of cells P3 gains at least 5 points over P1e or P2e on critical reach within 10 s and no cell loses more than 2 points. **H-F3b (original H-P3b):** mis-triage of abstain-routing minus lowered table at most -0.02 with the interval below zero, with improved estimates.
**F4.** Summary table, one row per failed hypothesis: original verdict, real-only rerun, real plus synthetic rerun with best r and interval, TSTR score, verdict, whether the verdict changed. If no verdict changes, this is said plainly.

## G. Protocol layers on assumption-driven simulated data (all SIMULATED)

**G0 numbered assumptions** (values and sources; also in `docs/05_data_code/SYNTHETIC_ASSUMPTIONS.md`). Source "assumed" = no document opened; the simulator is `crashguard.world_sim` (a copy of `sct_sim` with optional world features; with every feature off it reproduces `sct_sim` exactly, tested).
- A-G1 Crash arrivals: Poisson 0.1 per car per hour (stress parameter, as in the UA-SCT study; assumed). Burst: b Critical events within 10 s at 600 s (b in the grids).
- A-G2 Severity mix: (true class, assigned class) pairs of synthetic pulses of the modes that passed C2, in the mode proportion of the pool; assigned class from the plain estimate at +-16 g, 100 Hz (policies P0 to P2), from the upper end plain x q with q the real validation conformal ratio (P3, P4); the F1-improved estimate where F1 completed. Burst events are drawn from true-Critical entries.
- A-G3 Vehicle density: n cars share one gateway cell (grid values).
- A-G4 Geometry and path loss: cars uniform in a disc of radius 3 km; one gateway at the centre (grids) or two gateways at +-1.5 km (G5 only); path loss PL(d) = 25 dB + 10 x 3.0 x log10(d / 1 m) (exponent 3.0 assumed; 25 dB at 1 m assumed); transmit power 14 dBm and 10 dB of vehicle and body loss (assumed); static log-normal shadowing sigma 6 dB per car and gateway (assumed) plus the existing per-transmission fading of 2 dB; reception needs a level above the SF sensitivity (assumed typical values for 125 kHz: SF7 -123, SF8 -126, SF9 -129, SF10 -132 dBm; replaced by a document value if Semtech's datasheet is opened for B3, with its URL and date).
- A-G5 Capture threshold 6 dB at equal SF (as the UA-SCT study; assumed); other SF orthogonal with the loss factor of the UA-SCT study.
- A-G6 Independent extra packet loss p on uplink and downlink (grid values).
- A-G7 SF and coding rate: SF 7 to 10; coding rate 4/5 (G2 varies 4/5 to 4/8); 125 kHz, 8-symbol preamble, explicit header, CRC on.
- A-G8 Duty-cycle limit 1% and 0.1% of any trailing hour for each transmitter including the gateway; **the India limit is UNVERIFIED** (WPC/DoT not checked).
- A-G9 ACK timing: the ACK starts 0.1 s after the end of the uplink (assumed; G5 varies it to 0.5 and 1.0 s, a LoRaWAN-like receive window); the car listens for 0.1 s + ACK airtime + margin 0.1 s.
- A-G10 Channels: 1 (grids); 3 equal channels (G5): each transmission picks one uniformly; collisions only on the same channel; the gateway receives on all channels and is half-duplex on the channel it transmits on (assumed).
- A-G11 Tag length: 8 bytes (grids); 4 and 16 in G2 and G5 change the lengths of alert, flash, CANCEL and ACK by the difference (assumed layout otherwise as `docs/01_protocol/PACKET_FORMAT.md`).
- A-G12 Slotted fast path (G3a): the gateway sends a 9-byte beacon every 30 s; a frame is 30 s; each frame starts with a reserved window of 8 slots of length s = max(0.5, ceil(alert airtime / 0.1) x 0.1 + 0.1) s; Severe and Critical fast-path transmissions (flash, alert, CANCEL) start at the beginning of a uniformly chosen slot of the next reserved window; normal-path and heartbeat transmissions avoid the reserved window; a car is synchronised if it heard a beacon in the last 90 s (else it behaves as in the baseline). Beacon airtime is charged to the gateway's duty and reported. Beacon coordination is assumed.
- A-G13 Listen-before-talk (G3b): before every fast-path transmission the car senses; each ongoing transmission on its channel is detected with probability 0.7 (hidden terminals 0.3; assumed); if busy it waits U(0.05, 0.25) s and senses again, at most 4 times, then transmits. The regulatory status of listen-before-talk at 433 MHz is UNVERIFIED.
- A-G14 V2V relay (G3c): K = 3 other cars each hear an alert transmission with probability 0.5 (assumed); if no ACK reached them within the listen window of the sender, each hearing car repeats the alert once after U(1, 5) s (assumed); the relayed copy is identical (same counter), is charged to the relaying car's duty, is received by the gateway under the relayer's link, and the gateway re-ACKs idempotently; it is a duplicate when the gateway already holds the alert.
- A-G15 Receivers: the gateway decodes per gateway independently; with two gateways an uplink is received if any gateway receives it and the ACK goes through the gateway with the best level (G5).
**DESIGN grid (16 cells, fixed here):** n {75, 150} x p {0.1, 0.3} x SF {7, 9} x duty {1%, 0.1%}; burst b = 20; one gateway; one channel; tag 8 bytes; +-16 g pairs; seeds 6000 + h.
**HELD-OUT grid (16 cells, fixed here, nothing is chosen on it):** n {100, 200} x p {0.2, 0.4} x SF {8, 10} x duty {1%, 0.1%}; burst b = 30; same other settings; seeds 8000 + h.
**G1 policies** on both grids, 30 seeds per cell: P0, P1, P1e, P1+, P2 (SCT), P3 (UA-SCT), P4 (capped; phi = `s11.tuned.phi`), P3b abstain-routing (A of V4: abstaining events sent to the fast path), and the policies fed by F1 estimates (P1+ and P3 with improved classes) where F1 completed. Metrics: mis-triage, critical reach within 10 s and 30 s, critical p95 latency, duty-cycle use, share of non-severe events escalated, duty violations (must be 0). Policy parameters are the stored tuned values; nothing is tuned on the held-out grid.
**G2 layer attribution** (one mechanism at a time against the baseline P1+, then a 2x2 or 2x2x2 factorial for interactions, both grids; intervals from seeds): PHY (SF per class: Critical one SF higher; coding rate 4/5 to 4/8), MAC (retry schedule: immediate vs spaced 5 s; hold on/off; backoff; reserved duty share 0 vs 0.35; capture on/off), network and security (tag length 4, 8, 16; signed ACK on/off meaning ACKs of 4 bytes less and spoofable [airtime effect only]; CANCEL on/off), application (class rule point vs upper end; interval upper end level; abstain routing). Interaction: SF raise x retries, hold x spacing, tag length x SF, each as a full factorial. Reported: which layer carries the gain and which layers do not matter. DESIGN-grid results are not reported as confirmed.
**G3 mechanisms**, each against the best earlier policy on the HELD-OUT grid; the baseline policy is the policy with the highest mean critical reach within 10 s on the DESIGN grid (decided before the held-out run, recorded in the table):
- **H-G3a (slotted fast path against pure ALOHA).** Critical reach within 10 s at least 5 points higher (interval excluding zero) in at least 60% of the held-out cells (at least 10 of 16), AND no cell lost by more than 2 points. **Counts against:** fewer than 60% of cells or any cell lost by more than 2 points. Beacon cost reported as airtime and as share of the gateway's duty (assumed).
- **H-G3b (listen-before-talk against duty-cycle-only access).** Same criterion.
- **H-G3c (V2V relay against no relay).** Critical reach within 30 s (reception at the gateway) at least 5 points higher (interval excluding zero) in at least 60% of the held-out cells with p >= 0.30 (all 8 such cells in the grid), AND none of those cells lost by more than 2 points. Extra airtime and the duplicate-alert rate (extra gateway receptions per critical alert) reported.
**G4 regime map** over (density, loss, SF, duty) from G1 and G3: the policy with the best critical reach within 10 s per cell with margin and interval; ties where the best beats the runner-up by less than its interval. HELD-OUT cells give the map; DESIGN cells are shown, marked. Where P3 wins, if anywhere, is reported.
**G5 sensitivity** (HELD-OUT cells, 30 seeds): one assumption at a time, stated amounts: shadowing sigma 6 -> 3 and 9 dB; path-loss exponent 3.0 -> 2.5 and 3.5; capture threshold 6 -> 3 and 10 dB; ACK delay 0.1 -> 0.5 and 1.0 s; tag length 8 -> 4 and 16; relay delay U(1,5) -> U(0.5,2) and U(3,8) s; hear probability 0.5 -> 0.25 and 0.75; slot count 8 -> 4 and 16; sensing probability 0.7 -> 0.4 and 0.9; channels 1 -> 3; gateways 1 -> 2; severity mix (range +-16 -> +-32 g pairs). Reported: change in the main result (critical reach within 10 s, per policy and mechanism) and whether any conclusion flips; the conclusions that depend on a single assumption are listed.
**G6 regression:** the 48 attack cases (+ 9 designed cases) and the bounded model check of B1 are re-run against every protocol change used in G2 and G3 (tag length 4 and 16; relay (duplicate re-ACK budget); slotted fast path and LBT (timing only: no change to message rules; stated); CANCEL variants: design, pending cancel). New counterexamples or failing cases are reported. A check of the model and the designed cases, not the implementation.
**G7 summary:** one row per finding: layer, claim, evidence label SIMULATED, design-grid result, held-out result, sensitivity verdict, new / confirmation / negative. No DESIGN-grid result is a confirmed finding. Negative layer findings go into `docs/03_results/NEGATIVES_REGISTER.md`. The simulator is not tuned to produce a win; ties and losses are reported.

## Summary table

| Study | Seeds | Baseline | Primary metric | Origin |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B2 | 5000 + h (30) | P0 | critical reach within 10 s | SIMULATED |
| B3 | n/a | Semtech formula; 1 - exp(-2G) | airtime error; collision probability error | ANALYTICAL, SIMULATED |
| C3 | 42 + k | real-data smallest range | required range | SIMULATED |
| F1 | 0 to 4; 42 | original estimators and criteria | per hypothesis | MEASURED_SOFTWARE, EXPLORATORY-REVISED |
| F2 | 0 to 4 | real-only training | recall at 1% target; AP; decision time | MEASURED_SOFTWARE, EXPLORATORY-REVISED |
| F3 | 9000 + h (30) | lowered-threshold table | mis-triage; critical reach within 10 s | SIMULATED |
| G | 6000 + h, 8000 + h (30) | best earlier policy | critical reach within 10 s (30 s for relay) | SIMULATED |

## Addenda

### Addendum 1 (2026-11-13; after B1, B2 and B3 were run; nothing in Blocks C, F or G had been run)

1. **B1.** The watermark variant was not modelled, so the sentence in B1 that a watermark "would cancel a delayed earlier event" is a statement from reasoning (inferred), not a model-check result. The exploratory two-event bound that was run (EXT2: counters up to 4, 2 events, 2 alert sends, 1 flash, 2 attacker actions, 1 lost message) covers the exact-counter pending-cancel change and the design only. The 48 original attack cases use `BaseVerifier` (no CANCEL) and are unaffected by construction; the designed cases of `attacks_extra` use `SctBase`.
2. **B3.** The Semtech document could not be opened; (i) is a test against the formula as transcribed and hand arithmetic (stated in DECISIONS). The sensitivities of A-G4 stay "assumed".
3. **B2.** No deviation. The class pools of P1+e are the lowered pools of `t1` (f of P3's escalation), as written.

### Addendum 2 (2026-11-13; written after Blocks B, C and G were run, and after the sealed F1 run; before the F1 cross-validation runs, the F2 test scoring and the F3 runs were evaluated)

1. **C generator.** The generator design changed while its fidelity was inspected on the same real records (in-sample), before any C3, F or G use: Gaussian copula with empirical marginals instead of a multivariate normal; a plateau-plus-spike blend to reach the real area ratios; calibration of the peak and duration medians only (delta-V not shifted). These are generator design choices, not hypothesis changes. Result: only frontal barrier and other pass; pole and side fail and are excluded from C3, F and G (the preregistered gate). H-C3 is therefore tested on frontal barrier only (the pole mode is NOT tested).
2. **F1.** H-F1d (original H-X6 on splits G and O) and H-F1e (original H-X5) are NOT_RUN: the 176-test grouped-split machinery was not re-implemented with synthetic training inside the folds in the available time. Their original verdicts stand. Candidates for H-F1 are the augmented direct GBM and the augmented 1D CNN, mode none, as written; the anchored estimator and the reliability score use real rows plus synthetic rows without the augmented copies (as the original models did). Only the +-16 g cell enters H-F1b and H-F1c.
3. **F2.** The main estimate on the VZCrash side is the supplied-split test result (de-duplicated primary, strict sensitivity); grouped nested cross-validation is NOT_RUN there (no vehicle identifier). H-F2c is NOT_RUN as preregistered. H-F2e (six length-specific forests of N19) and H-F2f (leave-one-category-out calibration variants V0 to V5) are NOT_RUN in the available time. H-F2d reports the AP clause only; the flash clause is arithmetic.
4. **F0.** The surrogate generator has no numeric pass threshold in the preregistration. It is used as it is, with the marginals that match (KS at most 0.20) and those that do not reported in `f.f2.surrogate.ks.*`; the feature `post_quiet` does not match.
5. **F3.** The pool-level clause uses the out-of-fold pairs of the F1 cross-validation (main estimate); the simulated clause compares P3 with the equal-escalation SCT policy P2e (and P1e), as the original N28 rule did.
6. **G.** The DESIGN grid was used to choose only the baseline policy of G3 (P4). The relay hypothesis uses reception at the gateway within 30 s (`reach30_rx_critical`) in the 8 cells with loss 40%. The slot beacon is charged to the gateway's duty allowance; the share is reported against the allowance of the 5,400 s that are simulated.
7. **Holm.** NOT_RUN hypotheses enter the family with p = 1 (family size 18 unchanged).

### Addendum 3 (2026-11-13; correction, written when the full test suite flagged the count)
The text of B1 says "48 + 9 attack cases"; the designed extra cases of `attacks_extra` number 10 (X01 to X10), not 9. Every result of this round uses the real count (10); the sentence above is not edited. `tests/test_consistency.py` treats the preregistration like `docs/07_log/PROGRESS.md` (a dated record).

# FILE: PRIOR_WORK.md

# Prior-work check (searches run on 2026-11-10)

How this was done: web searches (general web, plus runs restricted to ieeexplore.ieee.org, scholar.google.com, arxiv.org and mdpi.com), and a full read of the VZCrash paper (arXiv 2606.06074, PDF text read on 2026-11-10) and the dataset card. Search snippets were not treated as proof. Where only a snippet was seen, that is marked "snippet only". Google Scholar and IEEE Xplore were reached through the web search tool, not through their own search pages, so the checks are not exhaustive. "Closest prior work" means the closest item we found, not a claim that nothing else exists.

## VZCrash paper and dataset card (read in full)
- Paper: Bianconcini et al., "VZCrash: A Large-Scale IMU Dataset of Ego-Vehicle Crashes", arXiv 2606.06074 (June 2026), https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06074.
- Splits: the paper states the splits are "strictly separated by vehicle". It does not mention duplicate recordings, and the release has no vehicle ID, so this cannot be checked.
- Triggers: recordings "were triggered on-device based on multiple threshold heuristics" (hard braking, hard acceleration, shock events) and then processed in the cloud. Our trigger is therefore a second trigger on events that were already selected once.
- Near-miss: counted as a negative class. No false-positive rate for near-miss events alone is reported. The paper does report false-positive counts on a 735,000-event real-world population of harsh-driving events (Section III-D).
- Microcontroller: not covered. Latency is measured on a server CPU core, and the paper says the models "could be deployed in most modern embedded devices".
- Direction and severity labels are listed as future work.
- Table I of the paper gives results on full 16 s events with server-side models. Our windows are 2.8 s after a trigger, so we do not compare with it.
- Dataset card: https://huggingface.co/datasets/vzc-research-chapter/VZCrash (no mention of duplicates, trigger stage, or microcontrollers).

## Queries and closest prior work, by point

### N1 Real-crash-trained detector on a cheap MCU, with transfer to a scale model
Queries: "random forest crash detection microcontroller ESP32 accelerometer exported C model"; "TinyML vehicle crash detection accelerometer microcontroller real crash data"; "crash detection embedded random forest accelerometer microcontroller deployment real crash dataset" (restricted to IEEE Xplore, Scholar, arXiv, MDPI); "scale-invariant features transfer learning from full-size vehicle crash data to scaled model vehicle accelerometer".
Closest: Kapalamula (2024), "Design and Development of a TinyML-IoT-based Vehicle Crash Detection and Emergency Alert System", a thesis deploying a model on an Arduino Nano 33 BLE with LoRaWAN alerts (it analyses acoustic signals and names no dataset): https://dr.ur.ac.rw/handle/123456789/2756. The emlearn library exports random forests to C: https://github.com/emlearn/emlearn. A random-forest crash model is also published on Hugging Face (snippet only): https://huggingface.co/Kankshi/crash_detect.
Not found: training on VZCrash, or any transfer test from full-size crash data to a scale model.

### N2 Impact side and angle from one IMU
Queries: "impact direction estimation single IMU vehicle collision accelerometer front rear side classification".
Closest: "Segmented and Directional Impact Detection", arXiv 1703.05680, https://arxiv.org/abs/1703.05680 (snippet only); "Low-Power Impact Detection and Localization on Forklifts Using Wireless IMU Sensors", arXiv 2509.04096, https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.04096 (snippet only); patent US 9,162,642, "Method and system for determining a primary direction of force resulting from a vehicle collision", https://patents.google.com/patent/US9162642.

### N3 HMAC, anti-replay counter and signed ACK on the MCU
Queries: "LoRa HMAC anti-replay counter signed acknowledgement accident alert ESP32"; "vehicle accident detection ESP32 LoRa MPU6050 alert acknowledgement" (restricted).
Closest: LoRaWAN itself authenticates frames with a MIC and a frame counter. An ESP-IDF component, embedblocks msg_auth, wraps messages in an HMAC-SHA256 envelope with an epoch and counter (snippet only): https://components.espressif.com/components/embedblocks/msg_auth/versions/0.2.0/readme. Gao, Li and Ma (2019), "A Malicious Behavior Awareness and Defense Countermeasure Based on LoRaWAN Protocol", Sensors, https://doi.org/10.3390/S19235122.
Not found: a signed ACK for a vehicle crash alert with the spoofed-ACK case tested.

### N4 Severity-aware delivery under a duty-cycle budget
Queries: "LoRa duty cycle priority emergency alert retransmission policy severity-aware"; "LoRa duty cycle priority-aware emergency messages retransmission acknowledgement simulation" (restricted).
Closest: policies that keep duty-cycle budget for high-priority events (snippet only): https://link.springer.com/10.1186/s13638-019-1522-1; "Environment-Aware Probabilistic Distributed Scheduling for Emergency LoRa Networks" (snippet only): https://mc.net.ist.osaka-u.ac.jp/en/themes/topics/distributed-scheduling-emergency-lora/; Pop et al. (2017), "Does Bidirectional Traffic Do More Harm Than Good in LoRaWAN Based LPWA Networks?", arXiv 1704.04174, https://arxiv.org/abs/1704.04174 (LoRaWANSim; duty cycle, ACKs and retransmission counts).

### N5 Stage-by-stage latency against HTTP, MQTT and CoAP
Query: "latency breakdown vehicle accident alert system ESP32 LoRa MQTT CoAP HTTP comparison measured".
Closest: Jiménez, "Evaluating the Performance of CoAP, MQTT, and HTTP in Vehicular Scenarios", IETF 103 MAPRG slides, https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/slides-103-maprg-evaluating-the-performance-of-coap-mqtt-and-http-in-vehicular-scenarios-jaime-jimenez/00/; "CoAP and MQTT Measurements over LoRaWAN", https://core.ac.uk/works/9932081 (snippet only). Not found: a stage-by-stage breakdown for a crash alert.

### N6 Open labelled scale-model dataset
Query: "scale model car crash dataset accelerometer open dataset collision RC vehicle". Not found: only full-size data (VZCrash) and simulation datasets (for example CarCrashNet, arXiv 2605.07098, https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07098).

### N7 Value of a cheap vibration-sensor array
Query: "SW-420 vibration sensor array crash impact confirmation accident detection".
Closest: hobby and project-level uses of the SW-420 for accident alerts (https://quartzcomponents.com/blogs/electronics-projects/iot-based-accident-detection-system-using-vibration-sensor-module) and a forum note that it gives a binary output and is poor while a vehicle is vibrating (https://forum.arduino.cc/t/ways-to-detect-shock-such-as-an-accident/584924). Not found: a measured ablation.

### N8 Dataset audit: identical recordings across official splits
Queries: "duplicate samples across train and test splits time series benchmark dataset data leakage audit near-duplicate"; "data leakage duplicate recordings official train test splits benchmark telemetry sensor dataset audit" (restricted).
Closest: leakage and de-duplication audits exist for other datasets: "Data Leakage Detection and De-duplication in Large Scale Geospatial Image Datasets", arXiv 2304.02296, https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.02296; "Data Leakage and Redundancy in the LIT-PCBA Benchmark", arXiv 2507.21404, https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.21404; mlleak, a split checker, https://pypi.org/project/mlleak/. Not found: an audit of VZCrash. The VZCrash paper claims vehicle-level separation and does not mention duplicates.

### N9 Trigger-aware evaluation
Queries: "two-stage crash detection threshold trigger followed by classifier system-level recall false alarms telematics accelerometer".
Closest: Taccari et al. (2018), "Classification of crash and near-crash events from dashcam videos and telematics data", ITSC, evaluates a random forest on events already selected by trigger algorithms (read in full): https://leotac.github.io/pdfs/itsc2018.pdf; patent EP 4063198 A1, "Two stage analysis crash determination" (snippet only): https://data.epo.org/gpi/EP4063198A1; the VZCrash paper's evaluation on a real-world event population (Section III-D).

### N10 Size against accuracy on the MCU
Query: "random forest model size accuracy trade-off microcontroller number of trees depth flash footprint embedded classifier crash".
Closest: Buschjäger and Morik (2021), "Improving the Accuracy-Memory Trade-Off of Random Forests Via Leaf-Refinement", arXiv 2110.10075, https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.10075; emlearn (above).

### N11 Hard-negative false alarms (near-miss alone)
Queries: "crash detection smartphone accelerometer false positive rate hard braking near-miss events evaluation"; "arXiv crash detection IMU dataset near-miss false positives hard negatives evaluation telematics".
Closest: the VZCrash paper (near-miss as negatives, false-positive counts on a real-world population); Hongo et al. (2023), automatic emergency calls from crash detection on phones, false-positive rate 71% in one city (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10363817); "Smartphone-based Hard-braking Event Detection at Scale for Road Safety Services", arXiv 2202.01934, https://arxiv.org/abs/2202.01934 (snippet only).

### N12 Retry and replay conflict
Query: "lost acknowledgement retransmission anti-replay counter rejected duplicate re-acknowledge idempotent LoRaWAN confirmed uplink frame counter".
Closest: this conflict is known in LoRaWAN. A ChirpStack maintainer wrote (4 June 2021) that in LoRaWAN 1.0 "there is no way to distinguish a replay-attack from a re-transmission", so retransmitted confirmed uplinks are not acknowledged, and that LoRaWAN 1.1 adds the uplink channel to the MIC so a retransmission on a different channel can be told apart: https://forum.chirpstack.io/t/no-ack-on-retransmitted-confirmed-uplinks/11358. The Things Stack documents confirmed-uplink behaviour: https://thethingsindustries.com/docs/devices/concepts/confirmed-uplinks-behavior.

### Scale-free features and transfer
Query: "scale-invariant features transfer learning from full-size vehicle crash data to scaled model vehicle accelerometer". Not found: direct prior work. Scale-model crash testing is an old topic (for example SAE 740586, https://saemobilus.sae.org/papers/scale-modeling-vehicle-crashes-techniques-applicability-accuracy-cost-effectiveness-740586) but is about structural tests, not detector transfer.

### N13 Physics-based severity (delta-V, ASI, rollover) from a single low-rate IMU, carried in an authenticated alert (searches run 2026-11-10)
Queries: "smartphone accelerometer crash severity delta-V estimation validation against crash test data"; "low-cost IMU MEMS accelerometer range saturation 16 g underestimates delta-V crash sampling rate 100 Hz"; "telematics device crash severity estimation delta-V from accelerometer ASI acceleration severity index road vehicle crash detection"; "arXiv crash severity delta-V estimation from IMU low sampling rate event data recorder 10 ms resolution error" (restricted to arXiv, IEEE Xplore, Scholar, MDPI).
Closest prior work (all snippet only unless noted): event data recorders already report delta-V and are compared with laboratory accelerometers in crash tests (NHTSA EDR round robin, https://www.nhtsa.gov/sites/nhtsa.gov/files/edr-round-robin-report.pdf; average error in frontal pulses just under 6%); a comparison of delta-V and ASI computed from EDRs (SWOV record, https://swov.nl/en/publicatie/comparison-roadside-crash-injury-metrics-using-event-data-recorders); BASt, "Study on Characteristics of Event Data Recorders Using J-NCAP Data", https://bast.opus.hbz-nrw.de/files/494/Study_on_Characteristics_of_Event_Data_Recorders_Using_J_NCAP_Data.pdf; SAE 960899, "Analysis and Calculation of Delta-V from Crash Test Data", https://saemobilus.sae.org/papers/analysis-calculation-delta-v-crash-test-data-960899; arXiv 2001.03165 (corrections to off-axis EDR delta-V) and arXiv 2510.00077 (resultant delta-V from EDR data with yaw); effects of down-sampling and range limiting on peak acceleration in a sports setting (https://lida.sport-iat.de/ta/Record/4043300); smartphone crash detection that uses delta-V and peak acceleration as severity proxies (Sentiance and Autoliv, https://coverager.com/smartphone-based-car-crash-detection-by-sentiance-and-autoliv/). Delta-V as an injury predictor: Kononen et al. 2011 (https://swov.nl/en/publicatie/identification-and-validation-logistic-regression-model-predicting-serious-injuries).
Not found: a measured study of a 100 Hz, plus-or-minus 16 g IMU against crash-test impact speeds with the sensor range as the variable, or a severity result carried in an authenticated alert. Delta-V, ASI, sampling-rate and range effects are all established, so we treat N13 as extending that work.

# Session of 2026-11-11 (long run): what was added

All dates below are 2026-11-11. "Opened" means the page or PDF text was downloaded and read in this session. "Snippet only" means a search result summary was seen and the item cannot support a claim.

## A. The 24 project papers
Full text search of all 24 PDFs (paper 01 by OCR). Method, term table and a row for every novelty point are in `docs/03_results/CLAIM_EVIDENCE.md`. Result: no hit for HMAC, duty cycle, delta-V or Acceleration Severity Index; no protocol ACK; "replay" appears only for voice spoofing (07) and federated-learning attacks (12). Severity labels in alerts do appear (03 cloud deep learning on camera images, 08 vibration levels, 16 weighted severity score with three escalation levels, 17 CCTV levels). Rollover from integrated angle appears in 01.

## B. Searches run (standard mode web search; IEEE Xplore and Scopus were not reachable)
| Date | Query | Result used |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-11-11 | "LoRa accident alert HMAC authentication anti-replay counter acknowledgment ESP32 vehicle crash" | Opened: Espressif component registry entry for `embedblocks/msg_auth` (HMAC-SHA256, 28-byte envelope with epoch and counter, replay rejection, no ACK or retransmission handling). https://components.espressif.com/components/embedblocks/msg_auth/versions/0.2.0/readme. Other hits (Technion collision warning, NSF PAR items, Amrita "real-time accident alert in hilly region using LoRa") are snippet only |
| 2026-11-11 | "severity-aware priority LoRa alert delivery duty cycle budget retransmission emergency vehicle crash" | Snippet only: priority-aware distributed scheduling for emergency LoRa networks (Osaka University group; page could not be opened: certificate error); US patents on alert severity allocation. Not read, so not used to support or reject N4 |
| 2026-11-11 | "random forest crash detection ESP32 microcontroller accelerometer on-device TinyML ..." | Snippet only: hobby and Edge Impulse projects (AegisLink, helmet system, TinyML driving-status RF). Consistent with the earlier N1 record |
| 2026-11-11 | "crash severity delta-V estimation from low-cost MEMS accelerometer smartphone IMU range sampling rate saturation ASI" | Opened: Tadic and Vukajlovic, "Experimental Verification of Inertial Navigation with MEMS for Forensic Investigation of Vehicle Collision", Radioengineering 25(1), 2016. Full-size crash with consumer MEMS: the paper says the low-G sensors "were mostly saturated"; delta-V over 30 ms and principal direction of force are estimated; high-G sensors (about 100 g, 200 Hz) are used. https://www.radioeng.cz/fulltexts/2016/16_01_0187_0193.pdf |
| 2026-11-11 | "LoRaWAN retransmission replay protection frame counter confirmed uplink lost ACK reset problem" | Opened: ChirpStack forum thread "No ack on retransmitted confirmed uplinks" (June 2021): a maintainer states a replay cannot be told from a retransmission in LoRaWAN 1.0, and that 1.1 adds the uplink channel to the MIC. https://forum.chirpstack.io/t/no-ack-on-retransmitted-confirmed-uplinks/11358. Also seen, not opened: arXiv 2009.09397 "A Model for Reliable Uplink Transmissions in LoRaWAN" |
| 2026-11-11 | "VZCrash dataset vehicle crash detection smartphone IMU Hugging Face benchmark" | Confirms the dataset and paper (arXiv 2606.06074); nothing new |
| 2026-11-11 | "clipped accelerometer signal reconstruction crash pulse saturation delta-V estimation half-sine fit" | Snippet only: US patent 7643921 "Clipped sensor data estimator"; SAE 2025-01-5046 (pulse functions: triangular, half-sine, haversine, polynomial, regressed to EDR delta-V); a restoration algorithm for clipped tibial acceleration (Sensors 2023). To be opened in the N14 phase |
| 2026-11-11 | EN 1317-2 ASI levels, THIV flail space, 13 Hz filter (three queries) | See `docs/05_data_code/SEVERITY.md` section "Verification of constants, 2026-11-11" |

## C. What changed in the novelty reading
- N3: an ESP32 HMAC-counter component exists (msg_auth) and LoRaWAN carries a counter. N3 stays PARTIAL, and the wording must say our contribution is the specific alert and ACK layout, the attack suite and the retry fix.
- N12: the ChirpStack thread (opened) is a public statement of the same retry-versus-replay problem, dated 2021. N12 stays "independent re-discovery".
- N13: Tadic and Vukajlovic (2016, opened) already show delta-V and impact direction from consumer MEMS in a full-size crash and note saturation of low-G sensors. N13 cannot say that low-cost MEMS delta-V is new. What remains is the range and rate sweep on 176 NHTSA tests and its use in a 97-byte authenticated alert.
- N2: the same paper estimates the principal direction of force (PDOF) from MEMS, so N2 is not new as a method. Software evidence for N2 is still not built.
- N4: a priority-aware LoRa scheduling paper exists (snippet only). N4 stays PARTIAL until the paper can be opened.

IEEE Xplore and Scopus were not reachable from this environment. The strings to paste are in `docs/06_references/MANUAL_SEARCH_CHECKLIST.md`.
