# N14 saturation-aware severity estimator: prior-work search (2026-11-11)

Queries (standard web search, 2026-11-11): "clipped accelerometer signal reconstruction crash pulse saturation delta-V estimation half-sine fit"; "crash severity delta-V estimation from low-cost MEMS accelerometer smartphone IMU range sampling rate saturation ASI".

Opened and read:
- **US 7,643,921 B2, "Clipped sensor data estimator"**, Andres and McConnell, Continental Automotive Systems US, filed 2005-08-26, granted 2010-01-05 (the PDF has no text layer; read through OCR). Abstract in the sources checked: "estimating values beyond sensor limits" by determining "an actual slope" from measured data and then "an estimated slope ... as a percentage of the actual slope" for the samples outside the limits until the measured values return inside. It cites a clipping-restoration page from audio processing (Gdansk University of Technology). So estimating the values of a clipped vehicle sensor signal is not new. It extrapolates with a slope; our method fits a pulse model (half-sine, haversine or triangle) to the unsaturated rise and fall and then integrates for delta-V.
- **Tadic and Vukajlovic, Radioengineering 25(1), 2016** (already in the N13 record): low-G MEMS sensors "were mostly saturated" in a real crash and were combined with high-G sensors; saturation is handled by using a wider-range sensor, not by reconstruction.
- **Westrom, Adanty and Shimada, SAE 2025-01-5046**, "Pulse and polynomial functions to predict vehicle acceleration using event data recorder delta-V in frontal and rear-end collisions" (abstract page read): triangular, half-sine and haversine pulse functions and polynomials are regressed to EDR delta-V to obtain acceleration. This is the same family of pulse functions, used to go from delta-V to acceleration, not to repair clipped data. No mention of clipping in the abstract.

Seen as a search snippet only (not opened, not used): "Evaluation of a restoration algorithm applied to clipped tibial acceleration signals" (Sensors 2023): restoration of clipped acceleration in a sports-biomechanics setting.

Closest work: US 7,643,921 (clipped data estimate by slope extrapolation in a vehicle sensor) and the pulse-function regressions of SAE 2025-01-5046. Not found in the sources checked: a pulse-model fill of a saturated crash pulse that is then scored on delta-V against crash-test reference values with a held-out split. The novelty claim, if the held-out result clears its interval, is limited to that evaluation on a 100 Hz, 16 g sensor chain; the idea of estimating clipped data is established. The IEEE Xplore and Scopus searches are in `docs/06_references/MANUAL_SEARCH_CHECKLIST.md` (row N14).

# N15 trigger-aware training: prior-work search (2026-11-11)

Queries (standard web search): "cascade detector joint optimization of threshold trigger and classifier early rejection cost-sensitive crash detection accelerometer two-stage"; "Taccari smartphone crash detection two-stage threshold trigger then classifier recall precision accelerometer machine learning".

Opened and read:
- **Kubin, Bianconcini, Coimbra de Andrade, Simoncini, Taccari and Sambo, "Deep Crash Detection from Vehicular Sensor Data with Multimodal Self-Supervision"** (IEEE Transactions on Intelligent Transportation Systems, 2021; PDF text read). IMU and GPS time series, a neural classifier trained end to end; it says the classical methods "rely on filters and thresholds" tuned for low recall and high precision, and it builds a threshold-based baseline. Average precision 0.9 for crashes. The trigger and the model are not designed together there.
- The VZCrash paper (already in the record): events were pre-selected on the device by threshold heuristics.

Seen only as search results (not opened, not used): cascade-detector literature in computer vision (multiple-instance pruning, cost-sensitive decision policies with early rejection, Neyman-Pearson threshold optimisation of cascades), US patents for crash detection with accelerometers. These show that joint design of the early stage and the classifier is an established idea in cascades; whether it has been applied to IMU crash detection with a fixed false-alarm budget was not found in the sources opened.

Closest work: cascade-detector design in general (snippets), and the two-stage threshold-then-model pipelines of the crash-detection papers above. A claim for N15 would be limited to the VZCrash measurement, and only if the interval clears (see `docs/02_preregistration/ALGORITHMS.md`).

# N16 severity- and budget-aware delivery scheduler: prior-work search (2026-11-11)

Query: "LoRa priority-aware retransmission scheduling duty cycle budget reservation critical messages exponential backoff jitter ACK feedback emergency". Opened: the abstracts of arXiv 2212.09453 (Capuzzo et al., energy-aware packet schedulers for battery-less LoRaWAN nodes: maximises packets under harvested energy; no priorities or retransmissions in the abstract) and arXiv 1908.10195 (Sundaram et al., survey of LoRa networking; the abstract names reliable transmission and resource allocation without details). Seen only as snippets: a patent on LoRaWAN retransmission control (resend on another channel within the duty cycle), priority-aware distributed scheduling for emergency LoRa networks (Osaka University group; page not reachable), LoRaHART real-time scheduling, adaptive retransmission for LoRaWAN. Exponential back-off with jitter and priority classes are standard techniques; the specific combination (a reserved critical share of the duty-cycle budget, severity-and-age ordering, ACK-feedback spacing, optional spreading-factor raise) was not found in the sources opened. N16 stays supporting evidence from a simulation, never a headline.

# X1 (N17) impact direction from one IMU: prior-work search (2026-11-11)

Query: "principal direction of force PDOF estimation from vehicle accelerometer crash direction classification impact side" (standard web search). Opened: the abstract page of Kusano and Gabler, "Automated crash notification: Evaluation of in-vehicle principal direction of force estimations" (Transportation Research Part C, 2013; iaorifors abstract page): in-vehicle (EDR) estimates of the principal direction of force compared with crash-test instrumentation in `54` side-impact tests (within 10 degrees, root-mean-square difference 4.4 degrees) and with investigator estimates in `146` real collisions (in 10% of them the EDR direction would give the wrong damage side). Already opened: Ebert et al. 2016 (phone IMU, RC car, four diagonal classes), Tadic and Vukajlovic 2016 (direction from consumer MEMS in one real crash), Balfaqih et al. 2022 (abstract: crash sides reported). Snippet only, not used: US 9,162,642 (a method for the primary direction of force), US 7,162,345 (accident analysing apparatus).
Closest work: Kusano and Gabler (direction of force from in-vehicle accelerometers, tested on crash-test data) and Tadic and Vukajlovic. Direction of force from vehicle accelerometers is an established topic, so N17 is worded as a validation of a supporting feature on a low-rate, range-limited sensor model, not as a contribution. The checks that are ours: the rule on a 100 Hz, clipped sensor model, with a grouped split.

All searches were standard web searches on 2026-11-11. "Opened" means the page or PDF text was read; "snippet only" means a search summary was seen and the item supports no claim.

**X2 (N18) severity measures against dummy injury criteria.** Query: "correlation delta-V peak acceleration HIC chest acceleration NHTSA crash tests dummy injury criteria vehicle crash pulse severity". Snippet only: NHTSA reports and ESV papers stating that HIC and chest g were poor predictors of field injury and that delta-V alone does not capture pulse shape or restraint. Closest work: the ESV and NHTSA literature on dummy criteria against crash severity (snippets). N18 is worded as a descriptive check on a small sample; no claim.

**X3 (N19) early classification.** Query: "early crash detection decision time accelerometer sequential early exit classifier time to detection vehicle collision". Snippet only: USPTO patents on crash detection in sensors and airbag algorithms (for example US 8,155,841, US 12,479,383), the VZCrash paper (opened earlier; no decision-time analysis). Closest work: airbag-deployment algorithms decide within milliseconds, a different regime (restraint control, calibrated on crash pulses; paper 18 of the project set). Early-exit decision rules are standard in sequential classification; what was added is the measurement on VZCrash with a fixed false-alarm target. No claim beyond the measurement.

**X4 (N20) conformal false-alarm control.** Query: "split conformal prediction false alarm rate guarantee threshold anomaly detection exchangeability distribution shift". Snippet only: documentation of conformal anomaly detection (rank rule k = ceil((n + 1)(1 - alpha)), marginal guarantee under exchangeability; weighted conformal prediction for shift). The method is established; the result here is a limitation study on one dataset.

**X5 (N21) sensor-spoofing robustness.** Query: "acoustic injection attack MEMS accelerometer gyroscope resonant frequency spoofing sensor output". Opened (the opening pages): Tu, Lin, Lee and Hei, USENIX Security 2018 (arXiv 1806.07558). Snippet only: a USPTO patent on protecting motion sensors from acoustic injection, a KAIST NDSS 2023 paper, news items. Cited inside the opened paper, not opened: Son et al. 2015 (drone gyroscope), Trippel et al. 2017 (accelerometer integrity). Closest work: the attacks on the sensor itself; defences at the sensor or in the sampling chain. Not found in the sources seen: an evaluation of a crash detector's decision against a bounded added signal with the defences compared here. See `docs/01_protocol/THREAT_MODEL_SPOOFING.md` for which parts of the threat model are taken from published work.

**X6 (N22) many-car LoRa capacity.** Query: "LoRa pure ALOHA collision probability capture effect number of nodes spreading factor gateway acknowledgement downlink duty cycle limit". Opened earlier: Tomic et al. 2020, Wei et al. 2023, Zhong and Nie 2024. Snippet only: LoRa beyond ALOHA (arXiv 2002.10732), frame success probability with capture (arXiv 1902.02866), experimental throughput models with capture. Closest work: the ALOHA and capture analyses of LoRa networks (established) and the duty-cycle blackout analysis of Tomic et al. The analytical part reproduces standard formulas; the simulation adds retry policies and a duty-limited acknowledging base under bursts. Simulation only.

**X7 (N23) scale-model transfer by physical scaling.** Query: "scale model vehicle crash testing similitude scaling law acceleration time scale model car dynamic similarity". Snippet only: scaled crash testing with similitude (Penn State thesis, 2012; Keske), "Crash Pulse Scaling Applied to Accident Reconstruction" (SAE 2008-01-0183), "Scale Model Tests of Vehicle Motions" (SAE 930660), RC-car scaled experiments. # FILE: PROTOCOL_MODEL.md

# Bounded model check of the alert protocol (written 2026-11-11 before the runs; results are appended by `python -m crashguard.protocol_check`)

**Status.** A bounded exhaustive search of a MODEL of the protocol (send, retry, signed ACK, CANCEL, two-stage flash, counter and replay window, re-ACK, reboots, an attacker). Origin ANALYTICAL (bounded model check). It checks the model within the stated bounds. It is NOT a proof of the firmware or of the reference code (`verifier.py`, `sct_protocol.py`). TLC (TLA+) was NOT run: the tool jar could not be fetched in this environment, so the check is an explicit-state level-by-level search written in Python (`src/crashguard/protocol_model.py`); no result is claimed for TLC.

## 1. Participants and assumptions
Car, base station, attacker. Perfect cryptography: only the car can produce a message whose tag is valid; the attacker can replay any captured message to the base or to the car, and can inject messages with an invalid tag (forged alerts, forged CANCEL, spoofed ACKs). The channel can lose a message, deliver in any order, and (through the attacker) duplicate a message. Timers are non-deterministic: the car may send or retry at any time within its limits, but only after its previous copy of the same message has left the channel (delivered or lost), which is what a timeout means. The shared key is secret (a holder of the key can forge anything; not modelled).

## 2. State
- **Car:** counter (1 to `cmax`), phase (idle, active, done = valid ACK received, cancelled), alert sends so far (at most `max_tries`), flash sends (at most `max_flash`), event serial (a ghost number used only to state the properties), the persisted counter, whether the flash and the alert were acknowledged.
- **Base:** last accepted flash counter, last accepted alert counter, the last `recent` accepted messages with their re-ACK counts (budget `reack_max` per message), the set of cancelled counters, and a persisted copy.
- **Network:** messages in flight to the base and to the car (at most `max_inflight` each), every message ever sent by an honest party (what the attacker can replay).
- **Ghost:** the log of accepted messages with the serial of the car event they came from.

## 3. Actions
Car: start an event (counter + 1), send flash, send alert (retry), receive an ACK (accepted only if the tag is valid and the counter matches; stops retries when it is the ACK of the alert), driver cancel (sends a CANCEL), reboot with or without counter persistence (a persisted counter is written before the initial send). Base: receive a message and apply the rules of `sct_protocol.SctBase` (valid tag; flash and alert counters strictly increasing separately; exact repeats of a remembered message are re-ACKed within the budget; CANCEL only for a known counter), reboot with or without persistence. Channel: lose a message. Attacker: replay, inject an invalid-tag message, spoof an ACK, at most `max_att` actions.

## 4. Properties (written before running)
- **S1** A replayed or forged alert is never accepted as a new alert: every accepted message has a valid tag and comes from a car event that has not been accepted before.
- **S2** A spoofed ACK never makes the car stop retrying: the car reaches the done phase only if the base has accepted an alert of the car's current event.
- **S3** A retransmission of an accepted alert that the base still remembers is acknowledged, not rejected, and the re-ACK budget is never exceeded.
- **S4** Accepted counters (separately for flash and alert) are strictly increasing over the whole run.
- **L1** (liveness, bounded) With a restricted environment (no attacker, no CANCEL, no reboot, at most `max_loss` lost messages) every maximal execution ends with the car holding a valid ACK of its alert.
- **L1b** A legitimate opening message of a new car event is never refused as a replay (checked in the reboot runs).
- **L2** After a base reboot without counter persistence, the properties that fail are stated with a counterexample; the same for a car reboot without counter persistence.
- **C1** CANCEL is accepted only with a valid signature. **C2** No send happens after a valid ACK (a valid ACK stops retries).

## 5. Variants and bounds
Variants: the design (idempotent re-ACK, both sides persist counters); strict anti-replay without the re-ACK (the earlier finding: a lost ACK is never repaired); base reboot without persistence; car reboot without persistence; the fixes (counter persisted before the initial send; base persists on accept). Copies of a message in flight are counted (each copy can be lost or delivered on its own) and a full channel blocks the sender instead of dropping silently. Bound sets, chosen so that each search is complete in minutes in Python (the combined bounds (counters up to 4, 3 retries, 6 attacker actions, reboots and several events together) exceed the 4 million state limit of this implementation, so the bounds are split; ATT has the asked bounds for the attacker, 3 retries and counters up to 4):
- **ATT** (attacker): counters up to 4, one event, 3 alert sends, 1 flash send, 6 attacker actions, 1 lost message, at most 3 messages in flight per direction, no reboots.
- **REBB** (base reboot): counters up to 4, one event, 3 alert sends, 1 flash send, 4 attacker actions, 1 lost message, 3 in flight, one base reboot.
- **REBC** (car reboot): counters up to 4, two events, 3 alert sends, 4 attacker actions, 1 lost message, 3 in flight, one car reboot; flash and CANCEL switched off in this run to keep the search complete. (A first version of this bound set, both reboots together with two events, flash and 3 attacker actions, was truncated at 4 million states and gave no result; it was replaced by REBB and REBC. This is logged in `docs/07_log/DECISIONS.md`.)
- **LIVE** (liveness): counters up to 4, two events, 3 alert sends, 2 flash sends, 2 lost messages, 4 in flight, no attacker, no CANCEL, no reboot.
A property that holds is stated as "holds within these bounds"; it says nothing about larger bounds.

## 6. Cross-check with the simulated attack suite
The abstract base rule (`protocol_model.base_rule`) is run on the sequences of a mapped subset of the attack cases of `docs/01_protocol/ATTACK_CASES.md` and its decisions are compared with the observed outcomes of the reference verifier in `results/tables/attacks.csv`. Disagreements are listed in the results section below, not hidden.

<!-- results:start -->

## 7. Results (generated by `python -m crashguard.protocol_check`; ANALYTICAL, bounded model check, not a proof of the implementation)

| Bounds | Variant | States | Depth | S1 | S2 | S3 | S4 | L1 | C1 | C2 | L1b |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ATT | design | 2,733,655 | 29 | holds | holds | holds | holds | not checked here | holds | holds | holds |
| ATT | strict_anti_replay | 759,600 | 25 | holds | holds | holds | holds | not checked here | holds | holds | holds |
| REBB | design | 1,448,162 | 26 | holds | holds | holds | holds | not checked here | holds | holds | holds |
| REBB | base_no_persistence | 3,799,806 | 28 | **FAILS** (6 steps) | holds | holds | **FAILS** (6 steps) | not checked here | holds | holds | holds |
| REBC | design | 359,071 | 25 | holds | holds | holds | holds | not checked here | holds | holds | **FAILS** (7 steps) |
| REBC | car_no_persistence | 188,541 | 22 | holds | **FAILS** (6 steps) | holds | holds | not checked here | holds | holds | holds |
| LIVE | design | 1,373 | 16 | holds | holds | holds | holds | holds | holds | holds | holds |
| LIVE | strict_anti_replay | 701 | 13 | holds | holds | holds | holds | **FAILS** (11 steps) | holds | holds | holds |

Property names: S1 replay or forgery accepted as new; S2 spoofed ACK stops retries; S3 repeat not acknowledged or budget exceeded; S4 accepted counters not increasing; L1 no ACK in a terminal state; C1 CANCEL accepted without a valid signature; C2 send after a valid ACK. 'holds' = no counterexample within the bounds.

### Counterexamples (shortest traces, plain steps)

**REBB / base_no_persistence / S1_replay_or_forgery_accepted_as_new** (shortest counterexample, 6 steps):
1. car starts event
2. car sends flash
3. base receives ('F', 1, 1, True) -> accept
4. base reboots
5. attacker replays ('F', 1, 1, True) to the base
6. base receives ('F', 1, 1, True) -> accept
**REBB / base_no_persistence / S4_accepted_counters_not_increasing** (shortest counterexample, 6 steps):
1. car starts event
2. car sends flash
3. base receives ('F', 1, 1, True) -> accept
4. base reboots
5. attacker replays ('F', 1, 1, True) to the base
6. base receives ('F', 1, 1, True) -> accept
**REBC / design / L1b_legit_new_event_rejected_as_replay** (shortest counterexample, 7 steps):
1. car starts event
2. car sends alert
3. car reboots
4. car starts event
5. car sends alert
6. base receives ('A', 2, 2, True) -> accept
7. base receives ('A', 1, 1, True) -> reject_counter
**REBC / car_no_persistence / S2_spoofed_ack_stops_retries** (shortest counterexample, 6 steps):
1. car starts event
2. car sends alert
3. car reboots
4. car starts event
5. base receives ('A', 1, 1, True) -> accept
6. car receives ('K', 'A', 1, 1, True)
**LIVE / strict_anti_replay / L1_no_ack_in_terminal_state** (shortest counterexample, 11 steps):
1. car starts event
2. car sends flash
3. car sends alert
4. base receives ('A', 1, 1, True) -> accept
5. car sends alert
6. ACK lost
7. base receives ('A', 1, 1, True) -> reject_counter
8. car sends alert
9. base receives ('A', 1, 1, True) -> reject_counter
10. base receives ('F', 1, 1, True) -> accept
11. car receives ('K', 'F', 1, 1, True)

### Cross-check with the attack suite (13 of 14 mapped cases agree)

| Case id | Case | Model | Reference (observed) | Agree |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 02 | Valid alert | accept | accept | yes |
| 04 | Forged tag | reject_tag | reject_tag | yes |
| 11 | Replay (same packet again): not processed again, same ACK re-sent | reack | reack | yes |
| 13 | Strict verifier (before the fix): ACK lost, car retries the same packet | reject_counter | reject_counter | yes |
| 14 | ACK lost, car retries same packet: re-ACKed | reack | reack | yes |
| 16 | Old counter, different content: rejected | reject_counter | reject_counter | yes |
| 17 | Same counter, different content (event changed): rejected | reack | reject_counter | **NO** |
| 18 | Replay of an alert older than the re-ACK window: rejected | reject_counter | reject_counter | yes |
| 19 | Retry of the same event with a fresh counter | accept | accept | yes |
| 30 | Base reboot, counter persisted: replay not processed again | reack | reack | yes |
| 32 | Base reboot, counter lost: replay | accept | accept | yes |
| 25 | Spoofed ACK (attacker key) | reject | reject | yes |
| 27 | Old ACK replayed for a later alert | reject | reject | yes |
| 28 | Genuine ACK for the waiting alert | accept | accept | yes |

Disagreements: case 17 (Same counter, different content (event changed): rejected): the model decides reack, the reference reject_counter. The model has no event id: it keys the re-ACK memory by counter only, while the reference verifier keys it by (node, event, counter). This is an abstraction gap of the model, not a result about the verifier.
<!-- results:end -->

<!-- ext:start -->

## 8. Extension T12: the flash message and CANCEL after a delayed ACK (ANALYTICAL; generated by `python -m crashguard.protocol_check_ext`)

Bounds EXT: counters up to 4, one event, 3 alert sends, 1 flash send, one CANCEL send, 3 attacker actions, 1 lost message, 3 messages in flight, no reboots; delivery in any order, ACKs may arrive after the CANCEL. This checks the model, not the implementation.

| Variant | States | Depth | S1 | S2 | S3 | S4 | C1 | C2 | C3 | C4 | F1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| design | 351,754 | 23 | holds | holds | holds | holds | holds | holds | **FAILS** (5 steps) | holds | holds |
| keep_pending_cancel | 351,754 | 23 | holds | holds | holds | holds | holds | holds | holds | holds | holds |

Property names: C3 a delivered CANCEL is lost when it arrives before its alert; C4 a late valid ACK restarts sending after CANCEL; F1 an ACK of the flash ends the alert retries. 'holds' = no counterexample within the bounds.

### Counterexamples

**design / C3_cancel_lost_by_reordering** (shortest counterexample, 5 steps):
1. car starts event
2. car sends alert
3. driver cancels, car sends CANCEL
4. base receives ('X', 1, 1, True) -> reject_unknown
5. base receives ('A', 1, 1, True) -> accept

The design refuses a CANCEL for a counter it has not seen; if the CANCEL overtakes its alert (reordering), the alert is accepted afterwards and the event stays active although the driver cancelled (attack-suite case X06 shows the same behaviour in the reference code). The fix (remember such a CANCEL) removes it within the bounds. Not changed in the reference code or the firmware in this round.
<!-- ext:end -->

# FILE: PROTOCOL_SCT.md

# Severity-Classified Transmission (SCT): protocol (generated by `python -m crashguard.sct_protocol`; written before the S11 runs)

**Status.** Built in software only; every transmission result is SIMULATED, every MCU timing is an ASSUMPTION, nothing is measured on radios. The 10% duty cycle is a parameter and is NOT verified for India (WPC/DoT not checked). The severity table and the N16 scheduler remain the baselines; SCT is judged against the severity table and a null result is a valid outcome.

## 1. Two paths
- **Normal path:** Low and Moderate alerts, and heartbeats.
- **Fast path:** Severe and Critical alerts.

## 2. Severity-dependent hold (parameters)
| Class | Hold after the decision before the initial send |
|---|---|
| Critical | 0 s: send at once. If the driver cancels afterwards the car sends an authenticated CANCEL message (section 6) |
| Severe | `h_s` seconds (a short hold, tuned on tuning scenarios, grid 0, 1, 2, 3 s) |
| Moderate, Low | the full cancel window of the baselines (8 s and 10 s; Severe's full window is 6 s and Critical's is 5 s) |

## 3. Fast-path rules
1. **Preemption.** While a fast-path alert is active at a car (ready and not yet acknowledged or given up), that car holds back its normal-path alerts, retries and heartbeats; a normal transmission already on the air is not interrupted.
2. **No back-off before the initial send.** No random delay and no carrier sensing before the initial transmission (the baselines do not add one either).
3. **Two-stage send.** The car sends a 14-byte authenticated **flash** (device id, counter, severity class, event type, 8-byte truncated HMAC tag over all fields), listens for the ACK window, then sends the full 97-byte alert. The flash is repeated in each retry round until a signed ACK for it arrives; the alert until the signed ACK for the alert arrives. The base's idempotent re-ACK rule (one ACK per message per retry interval, every ACK paid from the base's duty-cycle budget) applies to both stages.
4. **Retry spacing until a signed ACK arrives:** 5 s for Critical and 10 s for Severe, with the same maximum numbers of tries as the severity table (12 and 8). Fixed spacing, no exponential growth.
5. **Spreading factor raised one step after the second failed try** (parameter, ablated; at most SF12). ASSUMPTION carried from N16: the base receives every spreading factor and the loss per transmission falls by a factor 0.7 per step.

## 4. Normal-path rules
Standard 97-byte alert, at most 3 tries with exponential back-off (4 s, doubling) and jitter (up to 50%), heartbeats are unconfirmed 16-byte packets that are batched (a heartbeat that could not be sent is merged into the next one, so one packet carries the latest state) and dropped under load (no fast-path pending, or tokens below the reserve). The simulator implements "dropped under load" and counts the share dropped; batching is part of the protocol text and is NOT modelled separately.

## 5. Duty-cycle budget
The duty-cycle limit is never exceeded (a token bucket in seconds of airtime, refilled at the duty rate, capacity 360 s). A reserved share `rho_f` (0.2, 0.35 or 0.5) of the bucket capacity can be spent by the fast path only; the normal path may use at most `1 - rho_f` of it (it may spend only while the tokens left after the send are at least `rho_f` times the capacity).

## 6. Byte layouts (little-endian; version 1; HMAC-SHA256 truncated to 8 bytes, same shared key as alert and ACK)
**Flash and CANCEL (14 bytes)**
| Offset | Size | Field |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 1 | version (high nibble) and message type (low nibble): FLASH = 4, CANCEL = 5 |
| 1 | 2 | short node id: low 16 bits of `node_id` (ASSUMPTION: unique in the fleet) |
| 3 | 2 | counter: low 16 bits of the 32-bit alert counter of the event (the flash and the full alert of one event carry the same 32-bit counter) |
| 5 | 1 | severity class (high nibble, 0 to 3) and event type (low nibble: 0 crash, 1 rollover, 2 other; for CANCEL the reason, 0 = driver cancel) |
| 6 | 8 | tag = HMAC-SHA256(key, bytes 0 to 5), leading 8 bytes. **The tag covers every field of the body** |

**Full alert (97 bytes)** and **ACK (26 bytes):** unchanged (`docs/01_protocol/PACKET_FORMAT.md`). The ACK of a flash is the normal 26-byte ACK with `acked_event_id = 0xFFFFFFFF` and `acked_counter` = the reconstructed 32-bit counter; the node accepts it only for the flash it is waiting for.

**CANCEL.** Sent after the driver cancels an alert that was already transmitted (Critical, or Severe after `h_s`). It names the counter of the cancelled event. Unconfirmed, sent three times 2 s apart. The base marks the event cancelled (remembered, not deleted) only if the counter belongs to an accepted flash or alert; a CANCEL for an unknown counter is rejected; a repeated CANCEL changes nothing.

## 7. Base-station rules (reference code: `src/crashguard/sct_protocol.py`)
- A **full alert without a valid flash is accepted** (the flash is optional; the alert rules are those of `verifier.py`: valid tag and counter above the last accepted alert counter of that node, or an exact repeat of one of the last 16 accepted alerts, which gets the identical ACK again).
- A **flash** is accepted if its tag is valid and its counter, reconstructed from 16 bits within a window of +-32,768 of the last accepted flash counter, is above that counter; an exact repeat of a remembered flash gets the identical ACK again; a stale counter is rejected.
- The two counters are kept separately (last flash, last alert): a flash does not advance the alert counter, so the full alert with the same counter is accepted afterwards.
- Counters are never accepted twice; a forged or tampered flash, CANCEL or alert is rejected on the tag before any state changes.

## 8. Airtime (Semtech SX127x formula, `lora.airtime_ms`: 125 kHz, coding rate 4/5, 8-symbol preamble, explicit header, CRC on, low data-rate optimisation when the symbol is longer than 16 ms)
| Packet | Bytes | SF7 airtime (ms) | SF9 (ms) | SF12 (ms) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| flash / CANCEL | 14 | 46.3 | 164.9 | 1155.1 |
| alert | 97 | 169.2 | 533.5 | 3940.4 |
| ACK | 26 | 61.7 | 205.8 | 1646.6 |
| heartbeat (assumed payload) | 16 | 51.5 | 164.9 | 1318.9 |

A flash takes 27% of the alert's airtime at SF7, 31% at SF9 and 29% at SF12 (alert airtime divided by flash airtime: 3.65, 3.24, 3.41). A round that sends flash then alert costs the sum of both.

## 9. What is NOT claimed
The 14-byte layout, the 16-bit counter window and the short node id are our design proposals; the firmware does not implement them; no MCU timing was measured (any timing in the simulator is an ASSUMPTION). Authentication is the same shared key as before: a holder of the key can forge any message. CANCEL is not acknowledged.

---

# FILE: PROTOCOL_UA_SCT.md

# UA-SCT: uncertainty-aware Severity-Classified Transmission (protocol note, 2026-11-11)

Software design only. Nothing here is implemented in firmware or measured on radios; every timing is an ASSUMPTION; the duty-cycle limit is a parameter and NOT verified for India (WPC/DoT not checked). Byte layouts of the flash, CANCEL, alert and ACK are those of `docs/01_protocol/PROTOCOL_SCT.md` and `docs/01_protocol/PACKET_FORMAT.md`; this note adds how the class is chosen and what the car does.

## 1. Idea
The severity estimate has uncertainty (sensor range, clipping, estimator error). A conformal interval over the ordered severity classes turns that uncertainty into a transmission class: the car reports the UPPER end of the interval, so a Severe or Critical event is less likely to travel through the slow normal path. The price is more escalations of non-severe events to the fast path.

## 2. Class selection (on the car)
1. Point class c_hat = class of the estimated delta-V (fill + ridge when the sensor clips) and ASI, by `severity.classify` (delta-V limits 24, 40, 56 km/h; ASI levels 1.0, 1.4, 1.9).
2. Offline, on a calibration set of tests whose make-and-model groups are disjoint from the tuning and test groups, the conformal quantile q_up[k] of the one-sided score max(0, c_true - c_hat) is computed for every predicted class k (class-conditional; the pooled quantile where a class has fewer than 10 calibration tests) for the target coverage (alpha = 0.10 primary). The four integers q_up[0..3] are stored in the firmware.
3. Transmission class U = min(3, c_hat + q_up[c_hat]). U is the class of the flash and of the alert; the point class and the interval ends (lower, upper) go into the reserved bytes of the alert (offsets 42 to 45: point class, lower class, upper class, coverage code), covered by the tag. No length changes (97 bytes).

## 3. Messages
FLASH, CANCEL (14 bytes), ALERT (97 bytes), ACK (26 bytes), heartbeat (16 bytes, unconfirmed): see `docs/01_protocol/PROTOCOL_SCT.md`. The flash and the alert of one event carry the same 32-bit counter. Authentication is the shared-key HMAC-SHA256 truncated to 8 bytes (a holder of the key can forge anything).

## 4. Car state machine
| State | Event | Action | Next state |
|---|---|---|---|
| IDLE | trigger and detector decision (early exit) | compute c_hat and U | HOLD |
| HOLD | hold time h(U) elapsed (Critical 0 s; Severe h_s; Moderate and Low the full cancel window) | none | SEND_FAST if U is Severe or Critical, else SEND_NORMAL |
| HOLD | driver cancels before the hold ends | nothing was sent | IDLE |
| SEND_FAST | enter | preempt the normal path; send FLASH (if no signed ACK for it yet), listen, send ALERT, listen | WAIT_FAST |
| WAIT_FAST | signed ACK of the alert | stop | DONE |
| WAIT_FAST | no ACK | wait 5 s (Critical) or 10 s (Severe); from the third round raise the spreading factor one step (parameter); at most 12 (Critical) or 8 (Severe) alert tries | SEND_FAST or GAVE_UP |
| WAIT_FAST | driver cancels after a send | send CANCEL three times, 2 s apart, stop retries | CANCELLED |
| SEND_NORMAL | enter | send ALERT; at most 3 tries with exponential back-off (4 s, doubling) and jitter | DONE or GAVE_UP |
| any | duty limit | a transmission that would exceed duty x 3,600 s in the trailing hour waits | same state |
| any | fast-path cap (P4 only) | when the fast-path airtime in the trailing hour would exceed the share phi of the allowance, the alert is demoted to the normal path | SEND_NORMAL |
Replay and ACK rules on the base station are those of `docs/01_protocol/PROTOCOL_MODEL.md`.

## 5. Parameters
| Parameter | Meaning | Value or how set |
|---|---|---|
| alpha | miscoverage of the class interval | 0.10 (primary), 0.05 (secondary) |
| q_up[0..3] | conformal quantiles per predicted class | computed offline from the calibration set (`crashguard.ua_conformal`), four integers in firmware |
| h_s | hold of Severe alerts | chosen on tuning scenarios from 0, 1, 2, 3 s (`results.json`: `s11.tuned.h_s`) |
| rho_f | share of the hourly airtime allowance only the fast path may use | chosen from 0.2, 0.35, 0.5 (`s11.tuned.rho_f`) |
| phi | P4 cap on the fast-path airtime share | smallest of 0.1, 0.25, 0.5 within 2 points of P3 on tuning scenarios (`s11.tuned.phi`) |
| spacing | retry spacing | 5 s Critical, 10 s Severe |
| max tries | alert sends | 12 Critical, 8 Severe, 3 normal path |
| duty | duty-cycle limit | parameter, 1% default, NOT verified for India |
| spreading factor | channel setting | 7 (default), 9; raise by one step after the second failed round (parameter) |

## 6. Policies compared (all in `crashguard.sct_sim`)
P0 fixed 3-retry; P1 severity table (point class); P2 SCT (point class); P3 UA-SCT (class U); P4 UA-SCT with the fast-path cap. The preregistered hypotheses and the analysis are in `docs/02_preregistration/PREREGISTRATION.md`; results are in `report/software_results.md` (round 3) and `results/results.json` (`ua.*`, `s11.*`).

## 7. Known weaknesses (from the runs)
- Taking the upper end sends many more non-severe events to the fast path.
- At the same escalation, the severity table with its thresholds lowered does as well as P3 on mis-triage (T1); the interval family has the larger trade-off area (T2); P3 costs less than P1 only if a missed severe event costs several times a false escalation (T3). The conformal interval therefore adds no measured benefit beyond choosing lower thresholds.
- Faster retries (0.3 s spacing) repair the single-car weakness at high loss but lower critical reach within 30 s when many cars share the channel (T4, T9).
- In the ablations the reserved duty share and the CANCEL message change nothing in critical reach in the cells tested; the flash and the severity hold carry the gain (T9, EXPLORATORY).
- The bounded model shows that a CANCEL that overtakes its alert is lost by the design; remembering a valid CANCEL for a counter not yet seen fixes the model (T12). The firmware does not yet do this.
- The fixed 5 s retry spacing is worse than immediate retries for a single car at high loss (EXPLORATORY result).
- NHTSA tests are mostly high-speed barrier tests and are not representative of all real crashes; the class distribution of real traffic is unknown.
- CANCEL is not acknowledged; the short node id and the 16-bit counter window are proposals.

---

# FILE: docs/05_data_code/REPRODUCE.md

# Reproduce the journal-push tables and figures

Run from the repository root with `$env:PYTHONPATH = "src"` (PowerShell) or `PYTHONPATH=src`, in the project virtual environment (`.venv`). The earlier pipeline (`docs/05_data_code/REPRODUCE.md`: data, features, models, NHTSA severity steps) must have been run beforehand, because these modules read its outputs (`data/processed/`, `results/results.json`). The NHTSA harvest needs network access to the public NHTSA API; downloaded files are cached in `data/raw/nhtsa/` (git-ignored). The VZCrash files must be in `data/raw/data/` (gated dataset; never commit them).

Order: `t13_harvest`, `t13_heldout`, `t13_neardup`, `sel_study`, `sel_tables`, `vz_audit`, `vz_leakage`, `vz_docs`, `x_common`, `x1_fixedpoint`, `x2_cascade`, `x3_groupaware`, `x4_labels`, `x5_rank`, `x6_anchored`, `x7_fusion`, `x8_sequential`, `x_verdicts`, `jp_paper_tables`, `claim_table`, `stats_audit`, `jp_docs`, `check_results`. `scripts/run_all.ps1` and the Makefile `journal` target run them in this order.

| Table or figure | Command | Results keys |
|---|---|---|
| jp_tab_t13_heldout, jp_tab_t13_paired, jp_tab_t13_grid | `python -m crashguard.t13_harvest && python -m crashguard.t13_heldout && python -m crashguard.jp_paper_tables` | `t13.heldout.*` |

| vz_audit_cross_split_groups.csv, docs/04_audits/VZCRASH_AUDIT.md | `python -m crashguard.vz_audit && python -m crashguard.vz_leakage && python -m crashguard.vz_docs` | `vz.v1.*, vz.v4.*` |

## Positive-results push: reproduce (branch `positive-push`)

Run from the repository root with `PYTHONPATH=src` in the project virtual environment (`torch` CPU is needed for the CNNs: `pip install torch --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cpu`). The earlier pipeline and the `journal` steps must have been run (T13a signals in `data/processed/`, VZCrash windows, `results/results.json`). `make positive` runs the whole chain; the sealed fold and the test splits are scored once, so a second run of an evaluation stage needs `PP_DEVIATION="reason"` (logged in `results/tables/p_sealed_access.csv`). To rerun from scratch, delete the `p.` keys and `results/tables/p_sealed_access.csv` and the freeze files.

# FILE: RESULTS_SO_FAR.md

# Hardware results so far (from the progress report, Nov 2026)

## Protocols (ESP32 over Wi-Fi; MQTT via the HiveMQ public broker)
| Metric | HTTP | MQTT | CoAP |
|---|---|---|---|
| Latency | 2–3 ms | 1 ms | 1 ms |
| Throughput | 405–792 bps | 1184–1272 bps | 560–632 bps |
| Packet size | 152–198 B | 148–159 B | 70–79 B |
| Loss | 0% | 0% | 0% |

TCP: connect 40 ms, average latency 2 ms, 78 B, 312 bps, 0% loss.

## LoRa (2× ESP32 + SX1278 Ra-02, 433 MHz)
- Test packets: sequences 7, 8, 9; 26 B; RSSI −27 dBm; SNR 9.5–10.0 dB.
- Telemetry packet: 78 B; RSSI −28 dBm; SNR 10.25 dB; payload `M|NODE01|NODE01-22|30|106343|2.023|-1.350|10.034|-0.0184|0.0048|-0.0027|10.325`.
- Gateway snapshot 1 (23 s): 5 valid, 5 unique, 0 duplicates, 2 invalid, 5 ACKs sent, 0 failed.
- Gateway snapshot 2 (108 s): 21 valid, 21 unique, 0 duplicates, 2 invalid, 21 ACKs sent, 0 failed.

## Known gaps
- No range test; no PDR (the transmitter total is missing); end-to-end ACK delivery is unverified.
- No LoRa latency or throughput measurements.
- Sensor noise and calibration are not measured. The resting |a| of about 10.3 m/s² is about 5% above g.
- GPS fix was not always available.
- Vibration-sensor count: the report says 6 and the firmware plan says 5. **Confirm.**
- No crash has been classified yet.

# FILE: SEALED_SPLIT.md

# Sealed split of the pooled NHTSA records (Z0)

Written before any model of the positive-results push was fitted (see `docs/02_preregistration/PREREGISTRATION_V4.md`). **The SEALED fold is scored once per hypothesis**, after the model and its settings are frozen in a commit; every scoring is logged in `results/tables/p_sealed_access.csv` (hypothesis, time, freeze commit).

## What is pooled

- The 176 rows of the N13/N14 set, minus the two duplicated rows of tests 3668 and 3904 (same channel loaded twice under a wrong vehicle label; the signals were checked to be identical) = 174 records.
- The 739 held-out records of T13a (`t13.heldout.*`; selection rules in `docs/07_log/DECISIONS.md`).
- **913 records in 325 make-and-model groups.** Reference delta-V is the N13 reference; classes use the limits 24, 40 and 56 km/h (Low, Moderate, Severe, Critical).

Modes: frontal barrier = the original frontal tests (barrier at 0 degrees); side = the original left-side tests (impactor at 270 degrees); pole = vehicle into pole at any angle; other = every other configuration of T13a (oblique barrier, vehicle into vehicle, impactor at another angle). The mode is the crash configuration of the test record; a deployed node does not know it (Z1).

Group key = make and the leading word of the model name (upper case), a conservative merge of variants of one vehicle (for example COROLLA and COROLLA HYBRID go to one group). Split: groups are shuffled with seed 42 inside the stratum of their primary mode and cut at 60% and 80% of the stratum's records; all records of a group are in one fold.

## Records by fold and mode

| fold | frontal barrier | pole | side | other | all |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| development | 68 | 311 | 36 | 128 | 543 |
| validation | 28 | 103 | 8 | 45 | 184 |
| SEALED test | 20 | 115 | 14 | 37 | 186 |
| all | 116 | 529 | 58 | 210 | 913 |

## Records by fold and class

| fold | Low | Moderate | Severe | Critical |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| development | 21 | 291 | 149 | 82 |
| validation | 5 | 98 | 47 | 34 |
| SEALED test | 7 | 117 | 33 | 29 |

## Groups by fold

- **development**: 189 groups, 543 records. Digest of the sorted group keys (SHA-256 of the keys joined by newlines): `c3d527f74ad4b59638381a5004cdb79efa2570e38d5e0ba537e96179186bda5e`
- **validation**: 65 groups, 184 records. Digest of the sorted group keys (SHA-256 of the keys joined by newlines): `df9ec2e8edaada36ebd42665ccb2725d005a27fa862c090d1cc05b89428382a0`
- **SEALED test**: 71 groups, 186 records. Digest of the sorted group keys (SHA-256 of the keys joined by newlines): `ab69ec7a1a7d5d4ce618663227b54690c0c2b570101dbac0b236a1b84fc73365`

# Accelerometer data sheets opened for the sensor-selection study

Documents were opened as PDF copies on 2026-11-12 (the ADXL375 copy on 2026-11-11). Local copies are in `data/raw/refs/` and `data/raw/refs/sel/` (git-ignored: redistribution rights were not checked). Values in `src/crashguard/sel_parts.py` and in `results/paper/tab_sel_parts.*` were read from these documents. No price is given: no vendor page was opened.

| Part | Address opened | Date opened | Document | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| MPU-6050 | https://www.haoyuelectronics.com/Attachment/GY-521/mpu6050.pdf | 2026-11-12 | InvenSense MPU-6000/MPU-6050 Product Specification, Rev 3.3, 2012-05-16 (a Rev 3.2 copy was also opened: https://alliance.seas.upenn.edu/~medesign/wiki/uploads/Guides/MPU6050_datasheet.pdf) | Bandwidth in the model is 260 Hz (filter off) at both rates; the part's sample rate is 1 kHz at most |
| ADXL345 | https://people.ece.ubc.ca/edc/7660.jan2018/ADXL345.pdf | 2026-11-12 | Analog Devices ADXL345 Data Sheet, Rev. E (copy hosted by UBC ECE) | Noise density derived from 0.75 LSB rms x 3.9 mg/LSB at bandwidth 50 Hz (x, y axes); the z value is higher and is not used |
| ADXL375 | not recorded in the 2026-11-11 session (local copy data/raw/refs/adxl375.pdf, opened 2026-11-11) | 2026-11-11 | Analog Devices ADXL375 Data Sheet, Rev. B (distributor copy; the extracted text is garbled in the specification table, so only the values listed here were read) | Source URL not recorded in the earlier session: the URL field is therefore NOT verified. Bits and noise were not read |
| H3LIS331DL | https://static.chipdip.ru/lib/447/DOC029447674.pdf | 2026-11-12 | STMicroelectronics H3LIS331DL data sheet DS9012, Rev 4, October 2022 (distributor copy) | Noise value used as printed; no other source was opened to check it |
| LIS3DH | https://cdn-shop.adafruit.com/datasheets/LIS3DH.pdf | 2026-11-12 | STMicroelectronics LIS3DH data sheet, Doc ID 17530 Rev 1, May 2010 (copy hosted by Adafruit; the earliest revision, a later one may differ) | 1.25 kHz is the normal-mode rate and 5 kHz the low-power rate; both are used as bandwidth ODR/2 |
| KX134-1211 | https://fscdn.rohm.com/kionix/en/datasheet/kx134-1211-e.pdf | 2026-11-12 | Kionix KX134-1211 Specifications, Rev. 4.0, 24-Nov-2021 | ODR/2 (the wider of the two bandwidth settings) is used in the model |
| ADXL372 | https://datasheet.chipsmall.com/ADXL372BCCZ-RL/497167.pdf | 2026-11-12 | Analog Devices ADXL372 Data Sheet, Rev. 0, 2017 (distributor copy) | Bandwidth ODR/2 used in the model (the widest setting) |
| LSM6DSK320X (high-g channel) | https://components101.com/sites/default/files/2026-10/DS_lsm6dsk320x-compressed.pdf | 2026-11-12 | STMicroelectronics LSM6DSK320X data sheet DS15060, Rev 1, July 2026 (distributor copy). The part named in the task, LSM6DSV32X, could not be opened (its ST datasheet URL timed out), so it is NOT in the table | Dual-channel part: the low-g channel (+-2 to +-16 g, 0.061 to 0.488 mg/LSB) is used in the pair study. Bandwidth is an assumption |

**Part named in the task but not opened:** LSM6DSV32X (ST). The ST data sheet address timed out. The data sheet that was opened is for the LSM6DSK320X, which has a low-g channel (to +-16 g) and a high-g channel (to +-320 g); it is NOT claimed to be the same part.

**Caveats stated in every table row:** C1: the output data rate is the datasheet rate, not a measured I2C or SPI read rate on an ESP32 (NOT_RUN, hardware); C2: NHTSA tests are mostly high-speed barrier crashes; C3: the sensor is a software model (bandwidth, range, rate, resolution); C4: noise is white at the datasheet density where one was read, none otherwise.

# FILE: SEVERITY.md

# Physics-based severity (src/crashguard/severity.py)

Written 2026-11-10. The code computes crash severity from one triggered IMU window. **The classes indicate crash severity (how hard the vehicle's velocity changed), not diagnosed injury.**

Source status: **READ** = the passage was read in a document fetched for this work. **SNIPPET** = seen only in a web-search result snippet, not read in the full text. **OURS** = our design value, not from a standard. **UNVERIFIED** = could not be confirmed from a primary or standard text; treat as an assumption.

## Input
Window of about 2.8 s at 100 Hz (1.0 s before the trigger, 1.8 s after), accelerometer in g (x forward, y left, z up), gyro in deg/s. The accelerometer is clipped to the sensor range before use. Output keys carry their units in the name.

## Steps, formulas and sources
| Step | Formula or rule | Source and status |
|---|---|---|
| Gravity / orientation removal | `a_lin[i] = a[i] - mean(a[0 : trigger])` per axis | OURS (the usual pre-crash zeroing of EDR and IMU data). Leaks gravity if the vehicle rotates during the crash |
| Pulse detection | Starts at the trigger sample. Ends at the first sample from which horizontal `h = sqrt(ax^2 + ay^2)` stays below `release_g` (0.5 g) for `release_ms` (50 ms), or after `max_pulse_ms` (250 ms), or at the window end. Only the pulse is integrated | Release 0.5 g and 50 ms: OURS. 250 ms cap: NHTSA EDR work says 250 ms records 95% of crash pulses (SNIPPET, [NHTSA, Recording Automotive Crash Event Data](https://www.nhtsa.gov/DOT/NHTSA/NRD/Articles/EDR/PDF/Research/Recording_Automotive_Crash_Event_Data.pdf)) |
| Delta-V | `dv = integral of a_lin dt` over the pulse, trapezoidal: `dt * (sum(a) - (a_first + a_last) / 2)`. Longitudinal (x), lateral (y), resultant `sqrt(dvx^2 + dvy^2)`; m/s and km/h (x 3.6) | Definition of delta-V as the integral of acceleration; event data recorders must report longitudinal delta-V to +-10% (SNIPPET, [49 CFR 563.7](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/49/563.7)). Trapezoidal rule: textbook |
| ASI | `ASI(t) = sqrt((ax/12 g)^2 + (ay/9 g)^2 + (az/10 g)^2)`, `ASI = max_t`, with 50 ms moving averages of the accelerations | 12 g, 9 g, 10 g and 50 ms: READ (Nasution et al. 2009 as quoted in [Baltic J. Road and Bridge Eng. 6(1), 2011](https://bjrbe-journals.rtu.lv/bjrbe/article/download/bjrbe.2011.01/139/352)). The EN 1317-2:1998 standard itself was not available. Moving average here is causal (last 50 ms), whole window: OURS |
| ASI pre-filter | 4-pole phase-less Butterworth, 13 Hz (implemented as a 2nd-order filter run forward and backward, zero initial state, no padding) | **UNVERIFIED**: SNIPPET only ([MATEC Web Conf. 338, 01025](https://www.matec-conferences.org/10.1051/matecconf/202133801025), full text not readable here). The forward-backward form is our implementation choice |
| ASI levels | A: ASI <= 1.0 (with THIV <= 33 km/h); B: ASI <= 1.4 (with PHD <= 20 g) | READ ([Shojaati, STRC 2003](https://strc.ch/2003/shojaati.pdf), table 1; the Baltic J. paper also gives 1.0 recommended and 1.4 acceptable) |
| ASI level C | ASI <= 1.9 | READ in a secondary source (2026-11-11): Nouzovsky et al., Acta Polytechnica CTU Proceedings 39 (2022), table 1 ('C: 1.4 < ASI <= 1.9') and the text 'must fulfil the ASI value lower than 1.9'. The EN 1317-2 standard itself was not opened |
| THIV | Head is a free particle; relative velocity `v_r = -integral a dt`, relative position `s = integral v_r dt` from the trigger; the head hits when `|s_x| >= 0.6 m` or `|s_y| >= 0.3 m`; `THIV = |v_r|` then, in km/h | Concept READ (Baltic J. 2011: head moves freely until it strikes the vehicle interior; THIV <= 33 km/h for level A, Shojaati 2003). **UNVERIFIED and in doubt**: the 0.6 m and 0.3 m flail-space distances. The Baltic J. paper (read 2026-11-11) gives a longitudinal head distance of 800 mm, a transversal distance of 300 mm and 600 mm from the centre of gravity to the head, so 0.6 m longitudinal is not confirmed and the standard's yaw-rotation term (NOT implemented). If the head never hits within the window, THIV is 0 and `thiv_hit` is false |
| Related occupant criteria | Occupant impact velocity <= 9 m/s preferred, <= 12 m/s maximum; ridedown acceleration <= 15 g preferred, <= 20 g maximum | READ ([FHWA crash test protocol reports, NCHRP 350 criteria](https://www.fhwa.dot.gov/guardrailsafety/et31crashtestprotocols.pdf)); shown for context, not used |
| Peak and mean pulse acceleration, duration, specific energy | Peak = max `h` over the pulse (g). Duration = pulse length (ms). Mean = resultant delta-V / duration (g). Specific energy = `0.5 * dv_res^2` (J/kg) | OURS (plain definitions; specific energy is kinetic energy per unit mass) |
| Rollover | Roll (about x) and pitch (about y) = integral of `(gyro - mean(gyro before trigger))` from the trigger; rollover if the largest absolute angle reaches 90 degrees | 90 degrees about the longitudinal or lateral axis is the NASS-CDS rollover definition (SNIPPET, [NAP report 10308](https://www.nationalacademies.org/read/10308/chapter/4) and NHTSA ESV paper). The MPU range clips fast rotation (2000 deg/s), so the angle is an underestimate |

## Severity classes (rules)
Class = the larger of the delta-V class and the ASI class. A rollover is always Critical. All limits are in `src/crashguard/config.py`.

| Class | Resultant delta-V | ASI (EN 1317-2 level) | Other |
|---|---|---|---|
| Low | below 24 km/h | <= 1.0 (A) | |
| Moderate | 24 to below 40 km/h | <= 1.4 (B) | |
| Severe | 40 to below 56 km/h | <= 1.9 (C) | |
| Critical | 56 km/h or more | above 1.9 | rollover, always |

Where the delta-V limits come from (all SNIPPET; the class names and the exact cut points are OURS and **UNVERIFIED** as a published scale):
- 24 km/h (15 mph): lowest severity band in NASS-CDS reporting (below 24 km/h, 24 to 48, above 48) and the delta-V an advanced automatic collision notification system uses as a condition ([SAE 2022-01-0835 abstract](https://saemobilus.sae.org/articles/injury-delta-v-front-near-side-far-side-rear-impacts-analysis-1994-2015-nass-cds-2022-01-1008), [FISITA 2016 paper](https://go.fisita.com/store/papers/FISITA2016/F2016-APSH-001)).
- 40 km/h: in NASS data for belted occupants in frontal crashes, about 69% of MAIS 3+ injuries happen at delta-V of 40 km/h or less, and in head-on crashes a delta-V of 40 km/h gave about a 50% risk of moderate to fatal injury for occupants aged 65 and over ([NHTSA, older occupant injury in low and moderate severity crashes](https://www.nhtsa.gov/document/older-occupant-injury-low-moderate-severity-crashes); Gabler and co-workers as summarised in the search result).
- 56 km/h (35 mph): the New Car Assessment Program full-frontal rigid-barrier test speed, so crashes at or above it are at or beyond the benchmark severity ([NHTSA test reports, e.g. v14316](https://nrd-static.nhtsa.dot.gov/reports/vehdb/v10000/v14300/v14316R001.pdf)).
- Delta-V as a predictor of serious injury: Kononen, Flannagan and Wang 2011, Accident Analysis and Prevention 43(1):112-122 ([SWOV record](https://swov.nl/en/publicatie/identification-and-validation-logistic-regression-model-predicting-serious-injuries)). Injury risk also depends on crash direction, belt use, age and vehicle type, which a single IMU cannot see, so the classes are not injury predictions.

The mapping of ASI levels A, B and C onto Low, Moderate and Severe is OURS: EN 1317-2 levels were written for vehicle-barrier tests, not for classifying road crashes.

## Known limits
- A dashcam is not at the vehicle's centre of gravity, so ASI and THIV are not the EN 1317 values. They are indicators computed with the same formulas.
- At 100 Hz, delta-V is biased low for short pulses, and clipping at +-16 g removes area from strong pulses; the size of both biases is measured in the report (synthetic pulses and NHTSA crash tests).
- VZCrash has no severity label, so no accuracy claim is made on it.
- The pulse starts at the trigger sample, so the part of the pulse before the trigger is missed (a small, systematic underestimate).

## Not verified
1. (Settled 2026-11-11 in a secondary source: ASI level C limit 1.9, see below.)
2. The 13 Hz four-pole phase-less Butterworth pre-filter.
3. THIV flail-space distances (0.6 m and 0.3 m in the code; a read source says 0.8 m and 0.3 m), and the yaw term. THIV is not used in the severity class, only reported.
4. The class names and cut points as a published severity scale.
5. The 0.5 g and 50 ms release rule and the causal moving average (OURS).
6. Several supporting numbers are from search snippets only (marked SNIPPET).

Validation: synthetic pulses (SIMULATED), NHTSA crash tests (MEASURED_SOFTWARE) and the VZCrash distributions are in `report/software_results.md`, with keys `severity_synth.*`, `nhtsa.*` and `severity_vzcrash.*` in `results/results.json`.

## Verification of constants, 2026-11-11
Attempt: open primary or standard texts. The EN 1317-1/2 standards are paywalled and were not opened. What was opened (downloaded and text-searched):
- **ASI level C <= 1.9: now READ in a secondary source.** Nouzovsky, Kohout, Vrtal and Novacek, "Dynamic tests of the culvert face within crashtests", Acta Polytechnica CTU Proceedings 39:41-48 (2022), CC BY 4.0, https://doi.org/10.14311/APP.2022.39.0041, table 1: A: ASI <= 1.0; B: 1.0 < ASI <= 1.4 and THIV <= 33 km/h; C: 1.4 < ASI <= 1.9. It cites EN 1317. Status: READ (secondary). The code comment is updated.
- **13 Hz four-pole phase-less Butterworth pre-filter: still UNVERIFIED.** The statement appears in search-result summaries of Polish and Romanian papers (acta.fih.upt.ro 2016 and a Gdansk University of Technology paper). The ACTA 2016 paper was downloaded (4 pages) and has no such text; the Gdansk paper could not be reached. Kept as UNVERIFIED with a `# UNVERIFIED` mark in `config.py`.
- **THIV flail-space distances 0.6 m and 0.3 m: not confirmed, and one source differs.** The Baltic J. Road and Bridge Eng. paper (downloaded and read) states, for THIV and PHD: longitudinal distance from head to vehicle 800 mm, transversal 300 mm, and 600 mm from the vehicle centre of gravity to the head. Our code uses 0.6 m longitudinal. THIV does not enter the severity class (class = delta-V and ASI, plus rollover), so the class is not affected; the reported THIV value is. A sensitivity check with 0.8 m is run in the report. Status: UNVERIFIED, contradicted by one read source.
- **Class names and delta-V cut points (24, 40, 56 km/h): OURS.** No published four-class scale with these names was found. They stay a design choice, documented with their supporting references above, and the threshold sensitivity (plus or minus 20%) is reported in the report.

<!-- phase2:start -->
## Phase 2 updates (generated from `results/results.json`)

- **Release rule.** The pulse ends when horizontal acceleration stays below max(0.5 g, 0.10 x pulse peak) for 50 ms (or after 250 ms). The fraction was chosen on the 118 NHTSA tuning tests (median absolute delta-V error 3.45% against 6.69% for the old rule) and scored once on the 58 held-out tests: 3.76% against 7.53%, paired difference -3.76 points with a 95% interval of -10.25 to -1.36. On VZCrash crash pulses 0.67 still run to the 250 ms cap (descriptive).
- **Rollover.** A drift-corrected flag (gravity direction carried through the crash by the gyro, corrected by the gravity vector at the quiet end of the window) still fires on 0.0177 of VZCrash normal driving (the integrated-angle flag: 0.0203), above the 0.5% limit set in advance, so the flag is reported but kept out of the class rule (`SEV_ROLLOVER_IN_CLASS` is off). VZCrash has no rollover labels, so this is a false-alarm check.
- **Range and rate.** At least +-64 g and 200 Hz give delta-V within 10% of the reference on 80% of 176 NHTSA tests (260 Hz front end); +-16 g at 100 Hz reaches 0.07. Details, bandwidth sweep and named parts are in the report.
- **Constants.** The ASI level C limit 1.9 was read in a secondary source (Acta Polytechnica CTU Proceedings 39, 2022). The 13 Hz filter and the THIV flail distances stay UNVERIFIED; THIV is not used in any claim.
- **N14.** The saturation-aware estimator (pulse family triangle, saturation level 0.7 of the range) is in `severity.h`; see `docs/02_preregistration/ALGORITHMS.md` and the report.
<!-- phase2:end -->

# FILE: STATS_AUDIT.md

# Statistics audit (T14, 2026-11-11)

Generated by `python -m crashguard.stats_audit` from `results/results.json`. For every family of comparisons: the test, the interval, the number of intervals reported and the correction or the flag. Only the three preregistered hypotheses are corrected; every other interval is a 95% interval without multiplicity correction and an exploratory or descriptive result must not be read as confirmed.

## 1. Preregistered hypotheses with multiplicity correction

| Hypothesis | Raw p (grouped bootstrap) | Holm-adjusted p | Survives (adjusted p < 0.05) |
|---|---|---|---|
| H-a | 0.0020 | 0.0040 | yes |
| H-b | 0.0000 | 0.0000 | yes |
| H-c | 0.0180 | 0.0180 | yes |

The p-values are bootstrap p-values (1,000 resamples), so the smallest value is about 0.002; they are a convenience for the correction, not exact tests.

## 2. Families of comparisons

| Id | Comparisons | Test | Interval | Intervals reported | Correction or flag |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Detector headline metrics (system recall, AP, false-alarm rate) | bootstrap of test events (1,000 resamples); mean over 5 seeds for the 5-seed rows | 95% percentile | 26 | descriptive |
| F2 | N8 supplied against de-duplicated and window-level sets | paired bootstrap over the supplied test events (same drawn rows for both sets) | 95% percentile | 80 | descriptive (sensitivity) |
| F3 | N14 clipping methods: paired differences between methods | paired grouped (make and model) bootstrap, 1,000 resamples | 95% percentile | 96 | decision rule fixed in advance for 'M+ against spline and plain' per range and split; no multiplicity correction; fill + ridge was named primary after earlier results were seen |
| F4 | N16 and N22 scheduler against the table (X6 and the burst sweep) | paired bootstrap over scenario seeds | 95% percentile | 6214 | EXPLORATORY: the burst scenario was added after the steady-state null result |
| F5 | N19 arms (F, A, E, E') | paired bootstrap over test events | 95% percentile | 72 | descriptive; the early-exit rule was chosen on validation |
| F6 | N20 conformal false-alarm rate against alpha | bootstrap of the mean over (seed, draw) pairs plus one binomial standard error of the test set | 95% percentile | 120 | decision rule fixed in advance; the 3 variants of T7 are EXPLORATORY |
| F7 | N24 conformal delta-V intervals | grouped bootstrap | 95% percentile | 72 | decision rule fixed in advance (two conditions); the 6 range and level combinations are not corrected |
| F8 | N25 prevalence-adaptive rules against the existing rule | percentile interval over 200 resampled populations | 95% percentile | 1128 | decision rule fixed in advance; 8 rules x 4 prevalences are not corrected |
| F9 | UA-SCT class intervals (coverage, width, mis-triage) | grouped bootstrap (make and model groups, 1,000 resamples) | 95% percentile | 126 | preregistered: H-a, H-b, H-c (corrected below); other rows descriptive |
| F10 | UA-SCT policy differences over the held-out cells | mean over cells of paired seed-mean differences; seeds resampled inside every cell | 95% percentile | 630 | preregistered cells; 6 comparisons x 3 ranges x 2 cell types x 10 metrics reported, not corrected; read the preregistered metrics only |
| F11 | T1 equal-escalation comparison | pool-level grouped bootstrap; cell-level seed bootstrap | 95% percentile | 186 | preregistered in Addendum 1 (decision rule) |
| F12 | T2 Pareto areas | grouped bootstrap of the area | 95% percentile | 27 | preregistered in Addendum 1; matched-escalation rows EXPLORATORY |
| F13 | T3 expected cost and break-even ratio | grouped bootstrap | 95% percentile | 444 | preregistered in Addendum 1; the delivery term is EXPLORATORY |
| F14 | T4 clarifications | grouped bootstrap; analytical expected values | 95% percentile | 123 | descriptive |
| F15 | T5 near-duplicate sensitivity | bootstrap over test events, paired between sets | 95% percentile | 40 | EXPLORATORY (sensitivity analysis) |
| F16 | T6 threshold sensitivity (17 settings) | grouped bootstrap | 95% percentile | 68 | preregistered in Addendum 1; 17 settings reported, H-a and H-c counted per setting, not corrected |
| F17 | T7 conformal variants | grouped bootstrap (severity) and bootstrap over test events (false alarms) | 95% percentile | 56 | EXPLORATORY |
| F18 | T8 simulator sweep | paired bootstrap over seeds per cell; cells counted | 95% percentile | 1152 | preregistered cells; counts of cells reported |
| F19 | T9 ablations | paired bootstrap over seeds per cell | 95% percentile | 1404 | EXPLORATORY |
| F20 | S11 exploratory blocks (ablations, false alarms, single car) | bootstrap over seeds | 95% percentile | 528 | EXPLORATORY |
| F21 | T13a held-out NHTSA modes: N13 grid and N14 methods | grouped (make and model) bootstrap, 1,000 resamples | 95% percentile | 219 | decision rules written before the run (docs/07_log/DECISIONS.md); 25 grid cells and 12 paired comparisons are reported, not corrected |
| F22 | T13c near-duplicate NHTSA pulses | counts; grouped bootstrap for the headline comparison without the flagged tests | 95% percentile | 87 | EXPLORATORY |

Total intervals in these families: 15006. At a 95% level about one in twenty intervals that include a true zero would exclude it by chance; this is why uncorrected families are flagged.

<!-- PP:BEGIN -->
## Positive-results push: statistics audit (generated)

- **Intervals.** 95% percentile bootstrap, 1,000 resamples, seed 42. NHTSA studies resample whole make-and-model groups (group = make + leading word of the model); the same drawn groups feed every compared method (paired). VZCrash studies resample test events (P4c within vehicle-size category). The simulated cells of P3b use 30 seeds per cell with intervals over seeds.
- **Multiplicity.** One Holm correction over seven hypotheses (`p.holm.*`). Intervals of descriptive rows, exploratory rows (P4d, the cross-validation of P1, matched-escalation, interval variants other than the primary) and per-cell simulated rows are NOT corrected.
- **p-values** are one-sided bootstrap shares with a floor of 0.001; for 'at least two of three ranges' the p-value is min(1, 2 x the second-smallest per-range p).
- **Sealed use.** The sealed fold was scored once per hypothesis after a freeze commit, except where `results/tables/p_sealed_access.csv` shows a labelled repeat (P4b, P4c, P3b; reasons in `docs/07_log/DECISIONS.md`).
- **Known limitations of the intervals.** (i) The conformal quantile of P2 is fixed on the validation fold, so the width-reduction interval of H-P2 does not include calibration uncertainty (both intervals have a constant ratio); (ii) the sealed fold has 7 Low, 14 side and 20 frontal barrier records, so per-class and per-mode statistics are wide or descriptive; (iii) records of one make and model are correlated and are resampled together, but different models of one platform may remain correlated; (iv) the selection of the best direct model on validation among 8 candidates is not part of the sealed interval; (v) VZCrash has no vehicle identifier, so 'vehicle groups' are four size categories.

# FILE: SYNTHETIC_ASSUMPTIONS.md

# Synthetic assumptions (blocks C and G)

Every synthetic pulse, surrogate window and simulated scenario in the finishing branch rests on the numbered lines below. Values are the values used. Source "NHTSA pool" = summary statistics of the 913 pooled NHTSA records (`data/processed/pp_pool.joblib`); "assumed" = our choice, no document opened. **All of it is a stress test or a simulation. None of it is an unbiased sample of real crashes or real radio links, and none of it substitutes for real data.** Captions of every figure and table that use it read: "synthetic stress test, assumptions in SYNTHETIC_ASSUMPTIONS.md".

## C. Synthetic crash pulses (`crashguard.fin_synth`, `crashguard.fin_c`)

| No. | Assumption | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| A-C1 | Pulse families and their mix | half-sine, triangle (peak at fraction a of the duration), multi-peak (2 or 3 half-sine bumps, amplitudes U(0.5, 1)); probability 1/3 each | assumed |
| A-C2 | Joint distribution of (log peak resultant, log duration at 10% of the peak, logit asymmetry, log delta-V) per mode | Gaussian copula (correlation of normal scores) with the empirical 101-point quantile marginals of the real pulses of the mode; modes frontal barrier, pole, side, other | NHTSA pool (all 913 records for block C; the training rows of a run for block F) |
| A-C3 | Pulse shape given the drawn values | blend of the family shape with a plateau of 0.105 of the peak plus a narrow spike (half-width parameter 0.03 of the pulse length) at the asymmetry position; the blend weight is chosen so that area / (peak x duration) equals the value implied by the drawn delta-V (km/h = 35.304 x g s), clipped to [0.12, 0.62] | assumed (real pulses have area ratios of 0.25 to 0.33, below the 0.5 to 0.64 of single smooth shapes) |
| A-C4 | Direction | unit vector of the real velocity change drawn from the records of the mode, plus a 5 degree Gaussian jitter | NHTSA pool |
| A-C5 | High-frequency noise | band-limited (1.5 kHz) Gaussian noise inside the pulse, relative level log-normal with the median and spread of the real pulses of the mode (median 0.19 to 0.40 of the peak); 2 mg noise elsewhere | NHTSA pool |
| A-C6 | Sampling and window | 10 kHz, 0.6 s, 0.1 s quiet before the pulse, z axis noise only | assumed |
| A-C7 | Calibration | the log peak and log duration means are shifted (three pilot rounds of 300 pulses, seeds 900000 and up, never used elsewhere) so that the measured geometric medians of the generated pulses equal the real ones; delta-V is not shifted (decided after an initial look at fidelity: shifting it distorted its narrow upper tail) | assumed procedure |
| A-C8 | Descriptors | 300 Hz low-pass of the horizontal resultant after removing the pre-trigger mean, from the trigger sample, 0.5 s; duration = width at 10% of the peak; asymmetry = time of the peak over the duration; shape = area / (peak x duration); delta-V = the N13 reference (300 ms) | repository definition |
| A-C9 | Stress extension (EXPLORATORY) | log-peak shift of +0.2, +0.4, +0.6 (delta-V follows) to represent more severe crashes than the real tests cover | assumed |
| A-C10 | Fidelity rule | two-sample Kolmogorov-Smirnov statistic at most 0.20 for each of five descriptors; in-sample (the generator is fitted to the same records) | preregistered |

Fidelity outcome (read from `results.json`, keys `f.c.fidelity.*`): frontal barrier and other pass; pole fails (shape descriptor), side fails (asymmetry and peak). Only frontal barrier and other are used in C3, F and G. The pole result of the real-data requirement table is therefore not tested against synthetic pulses.
The synthetic file: `results/synthetic/synthetic_pulses_5000_params.csv` (mode, family, seed, descriptors; each pulse is regenerated exactly by `crashguard.fin_synth.make_pulse` with seed 42 + k and the generator parameters `results/synthetic/generator_params_all913.json`) and the 2 kHz waveforms in the release archive.

<!-- G section is appended below by the protocol study -->

## G. Protocol layers study (`crashguard.world_sim`, `crashguard.fin_g`)

All lines are fixed in `docs/02_preregistration/PREREGISTRATION_V5.md` (committed before the runs). "Assumed" = no document opened; the receiver sensitivities are NOT taken from a Semtech document (it could not be opened). The India duty limit and the regulatory status of listen-before-talk at 433 MHz are UNVERIFIED.

| No. | Assumption | Value | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| A-G1 | Crash arrivals and burst | Poisson 0.1 per car per hour; a burst of b Critical events within 10 s at 600 s (b = 20 on the DESIGN grid, 30 on the HELD-OUT grid) | assumed (stress parameter, as in the UA-SCT study) |
| A-G2 | Severity mix | pairs (true class, assigned class) of 1,500 synthetic pulses of the modes that passed C2 (frontal barrier, other) in the mode proportion of the real pool, +-16 g and +-32 g, 100 Hz; true class from the reference delta-V; assigned class from the plain estimate; upper class from plain x q (q = conformal quantile of reference / plain on the real development and validation records, 90% target); abstain = clipped share above the 70th percentile of the real development and validation rows; the mix is mostly Critical (true classes at +-16 g: 45 Low, 301 Moderate, 424 Severe, 730 Critical) | synthetic pulses (C block) and NHTSA pool |
| A-G3 | Vehicle density | n cars share one gateway cell (grid values) | assumed |
| A-G4 | Geometry and path loss | cars uniform in a disc of radius 3 km; one gateway at the centre (two at +-1.5 km in G5); PL(d) = 25 dB + 10 x 3.0 x log10(d / 1 m); 14 dBm transmit power; 10 dB vehicle and body loss; static log-normal shadowing sigma 6 dB per car and gateway; per-transmission fading 2 dB; reception needs a level above the sensitivity of the SF (SF7 -123, SF8 -126, SF9 -129, SF10 -132 dBm) | assumed |
| A-G5 | Capture | 6 dB at equal SF and channel; other SFs orthogonal with the loss factor 0.7 per SF step of the earlier study | assumed (earlier study) |
| A-G6 | Extra loss | independent loss p on uplink and downlink (grid values) | assumed |
| A-G7 | PHY | 125 kHz, coding rate 4/5 (4/8 in G2), 8-symbol preamble, explicit header, CRC on; SF 7 to 10 | repository airtime formula (formula as transcribed; Semtech document not opened) |
| A-G8 | Duty limit | 1% and 0.1% of any trailing hour per transmitter, gateway included | assumed; India limit UNVERIFIED |
| A-G9 | ACK timing | ACK starts 0.1 s after the uplink ends (0.5 s and 1.0 s in G5); the car listens 0.1 s + ACK airtime + 0.1 s margin | assumed |
| A-G10 | Channels | 1 (3 in G5); the gateway is half-duplex on the channel it transmits on | assumed |
| A-G11 | Tag length | 8 bytes (4 and 16 in G2 and G5): the lengths of alert, flash, CANCEL and ACK change by the difference | assumed layout |
| A-G12 | Slotted fast path | 9-byte beacon every 30 s; frame 30 s; reserved window of 8 slots of max(0.5 s, ceil(alert airtime / 0.1 s) x 0.1 s + 0.1 s); fast-path transmissions at the start of a uniformly chosen slot of the next window; normal and heartbeat traffic avoid the window; synchronised if a beacon was heard in the last 90 s; beacon charged to the gateway duty; CANCEL messages are not slotted | assumed (beacon coordination assumed) |
| A-G13 | Listen-before-talk | each ongoing transmission detected with probability 0.7; busy: wait U(0.05, 0.25) s, at most 4 times, then transmit | assumed; 433 MHz regulatory status UNVERIFIED |
| A-G14 | V2V relay | 3 other cars each hear an alert with probability 0.5; if the sender has no ACK after its listen window, each hearing car repeats the alert once after U(1, 5) s; charged to the relayer's duty | assumed |
| A-G15 | Receivers | per-gateway reception; with two gateways the ACK goes through the best gateway | assumed |

Simplifications stated: collisions are evaluated at the best gateway of the transmitting car; the gateway's receive chain is half-duplex only on the channel of its ACK; a relay is triggered by the sender's missing ACK (neighbours are assumed to share that view). Grids: DESIGN n {75, 150} x loss {10%, 30%} x SF {7, 9} x duty {1%, 0.1%}, burst 20, seeds 6000 + h; HELD-OUT n {100, 200} x loss {20%, 40%} x SF {8, 10} x duty {1%, 0.1%}, burst 30, seeds 8000 + h; 30 seeds per cell.

# FILE: THREAT_MODEL_SPOOFING.md

# Threat model for X5 (N21): bounded signal added to the accelerometer reading

Written 2026-11-11, before the X5 run. This is a software study on stored dataset windows. **No attack code that acts on a physical sensor is written or used** (no signal generator, no resonance search, no hardware interface, no instructions for building one). The perturbation exists only as an array added to a stored window in memory.

## What the attacker can do
The attacker adds a signal d(t) to the accelerometer reading of the node, on one, two or all three axes, from the start to the end of the 2.8 s window.
- **Amplitude budget epsilon** in {0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2} g: the largest absolute value of d on any axis at any sample.
- **Bandwidth limit:** d is a sinusoid or a narrow band of at most three sinusoids, with frequencies between 2 and 45 Hz (the node samples at 100 Hz, so nothing above 50 Hz exists in the data), arbitrary phases, and an arbitrary direction vector per sinusoid. A broadband signal is not considered (a plain random-noise attack is included only as the baseline "random" search).
- **Knowledge:** (1) black box: the attacker can query the deployed forest's decision on a modified window (up to 500 queries per window) but does not know its structure; (2) surrogate: the attacker trains a logistic regression or a small neural network on public data (the training windows) and transfers the result to the forest.
- **Timing:** the attacker is present for the whole window; trigger timing is not attacked separately (a quiet vehicle crosses the 2 g trigger as soon as about 1 g is added along the gravity axis, which is analytical and trivial: the trigger is not a defence).

## Goals
- **(a) False crash:** a non-crash window (one that already passes the trigger) is classified as a crash.
- **(b) Hidden crash:** a real crash window is not alarmed.

## What is taken from published work, and what is ours
Opened (2026-11-11): Tu, Lin, Lee and Hei, "Injected and Delivered: Fabricating Implicit Control over Actuation Systems by Spoofing Inertial Sensors", USENIX Security 2018 (first pages read; arXiv 1806.07558). It states that MEMS gyroscopes are susceptible to resonant acoustic interference, that out-of-band signals injected at resonant frequencies can deliver chosen digital values to the processor (output control, not only biasing), that low-frequency signals in the sensing channel can be shaped by adjusting amplitude and phase, and that 17 of 25 tested devices could be controlled implicitly. It cites Son et al. (2015, drone gyroscope) and Trippel et al. (2017, accelerometer integrity under acoustic attacks); those two were seen only as citations inside this paper and as search snippets, not opened.
- **Taken from published work:** the premise that an attacker can make a MEMS accelerometer or gyroscope report attacker-chosen low-frequency content (output control), and that amplitude and phase are the controllable parameters.
- **Ours:** the budget grid, the restriction to one to three sinusoids between 2 and 45 Hz, the target (a crash detector on a vehicle node), the surrogate and black-box search procedures, the defences and their evaluation on the VZCrash windows. Whether a real speaker could deliver such a signal at such amplitudes to a car-mounted sensor is NOT studied and is not claimed.

## Defences evaluated (parameters chosen on validation windows only)
D1 median filter (3 and 5 samples); D2 plausibility check on delta-V against peak acceleration and on cross-axis share, limits from the training crashes; D3 adversarial training with the same budgets; D4 ensemble with a minimum physics-based delta-V. See `docs/02_preregistration/EXPERIMENTS_EXTRA.md`.
